Saṅgītisuttaṃ

Dīgha Nikāya 10
← Back to Dīgha Nikāya
296
296. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā mallesu cārikaṃ caramāno mahatā bhikkhusaṅghena saddhiṃ pañcamattehi bhikkhusatehi yena pāvā nāma mallānaṃ nagaraṃ tadavasari. Tatra sudaṃ bhagavā pāvāyaṃ viharati cundassa kammāraputtassa ambavane. Ubbhatakanavasandhāgāraṃ
Thus have I heard:

On one occasion, the Blessed One was wandering among the Mallas, together with a large Saṅgha of monks, approximately five hundred monks, and he arrived at Pāvā, a city of the Mallas.

There, it is said, the Blessed One stayed near Pāvā in the mango grove of Cunda, the smith's son.

Ubbhatakanavasandhāgāraṃ
296.Evaṃme sutanti saṅgītisuttaṃ. Tatrāyamapubbapadavaṇṇanā – cārikaṃ caramānoti nibaddhacārikaṃ caramāno. Tadā kira satthā dasasahassacakkavāḷe ñāṇajālaṃ pattharitvā lokaṃ volokayamāno pāvānagaravāsino mallarājāno disvā ime rājāno mayhaṃ sabbaññutaññāṇajālassa anto paññāyanti, kiṃ nu khoti āvajjanto ‘‘rājāno ekaṃ sandhāgāraṃ kāresuṃ, mayi gate maṅgalaṃ bhaṇāpessanti, ahaṃ tesaṃ maṅgalaṃ vatvā uyyojetvā ‘bhikkhusaṅghassa dhammakathaṃ kathehī’ti sāriputtaṃ vakkhāmi, sāriputto tīhi piṭakehi sammasitvā cuddasapañhādhikena pañhasahassena paṭimaṇḍetvā bhikkhusaṅghassa saṅgītisuttaṃ nāma kathessati, suttantaṃ āvajjetvā pañca bhikkhusatāni saha paṭisambhidāhi arahattaṃ pāpuṇissantī’’ti imamatthaṃ disvā cārikaṃ pakkanto. Tena vuttaṃ – ‘‘mallesu cārikaṃ caramāno’’ti. Ubbhatakanavasandhāgāravaṇṇanā
296. Thus, me suta is the saṅgītisuttaṃ.

Here, the commentary on the apubbapadavaṇṇanā is: cārikaṃ caramāno means constantly wandering on cārikaṃ.

At that time, the teacher, having spread the net of knowledge in the ten thousand world-systems, gazed upon the world. Seeing the Malla kings dwelling in Pāvā city, and reflecting, "These kings appear within the net of my omniscient knowledge, what now?", [he thought,] "The kings have built a sandhāgāraṃ; when I go, they will have auspicious verses recited. I will speak auspicious verses to them and then dismiss them, and I will say to Śāriputra, 'Preach a dhammakathaṃ to the bhikkhusaṅghassa.' Śāriputra, having considered the three piṭakas, having adorned [the sermon] with a thousand questions along with fourteen additional questions, will preach the saṅgītisuttaṃ to the bhikkhusaṅghassa. Having reflected on the suttantaṃ, five hundred bhikkhus together with the paṭisambhidā will attain arahattaṃ," having seen this purpose, he departed on cārikaṃ.

Therefore, it was said: "wandering on cārikaṃ among the Mallas."

Commentary on the ubbhatakanavasandhāgāravaṇṇanā.
297
297. Tena kho pana samayena pāveyyakānaṃ mallānaṃ ubbhatakaṃ nāma navaṃ sandhāgāraṃ santhāgāraṃ (sī. pī.), saṇṭhāgāraṃ (syā. kaṃ.) acirakāritaṃ hoti anajjhāvuṭṭhaṃ anajjhāvutthaṃ (sī. syā. pī. ka.) samaṇena vā brāhmaṇena vā kenaci vā manussabhūtena. Assosuṃ kho pāveyyakā mallā – ‘‘bhagavā kira mallesu cārikaṃ caramāno mahatā bhikkhusaṅghena saddhiṃ pañcamattehi bhikkhusatehi pāvaṃ anuppatto pāvāyaṃ viharati cundassa kammāraputtassa ambavane’’ti. Atha kho pāveyyakā mallā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu. Ekamantaṃ nisinnā kho pāveyyakā mallā bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ – ‘‘idha, bhante, pāveyyakānaṃ mallānaṃ ubbhatakaṃ nāma navaṃ sandhāgāraṃ acirakāritaṃ hoti anajjhāvuṭṭhaṃ samaṇena vā brāhmaṇena vā kenaci vā manussabhūtena. Tañca kho, bhante, bhagavā paṭhamaṃ paribhuñjatu, bhagavatā paṭhamaṃ paribhuttaṃ pacchā pāveyyakā mallā paribhuñjissanti. Tadassa pāveyyakānaṃ mallānaṃ dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāyā’’ti. Adhivāsesi kho bhagavā tuṇhībhāvena.
Now, at that time, the Mallas of Pāvā had just built a new reception hall named Ubbhataka, and it had not yet been dwelt in by any ascetic (śramaṇa) or brāhmaṇa, or anyone at all in human form.

The Pāvā Mallas heard, "The Blessed One (bhagavā), they say, wandering among the Mallas with a large saṅgha of monks—approximately 500 monks in all—has arrived at Pāvā and is staying near Pāvā in Cunda the silversmith’s mango grove."

Then the Pāvā Mallas went to the Blessed One (bhagavā) and, on arrival, having bowed down to him, sat to one side.

As they were sitting there, the Pāvā Mallas said to the Blessed One (bhagavā), "Here, venerable sir (bhante), the Mallas of Pāvā have a new reception hall named Ubbhataka, which has just been built, and it has not yet been dwelt in by any ascetic (śramaṇa) or brāhmaṇa, or anyone at all in human form.

May the Blessed One (bhagavā) be the first to use it, venerable sir (bhante). When the Blessed One (bhagavatā) has used it first, the Pāvā Mallas will use it afterwards.

That will be for their long-term welfare and happiness."

The Blessed One (bhagavā) assented by silence.
297.Ubbhatakanti tassa nāmaṃ, uccattā vā evaṃ vuttaṃ. Sandhāgāranti nagaramajjhe sandhāgārasālā. Samaṇena vāti ettha yasmā gharavatthupariggahakāleyeva devatā attano vasanaṭṭhānaṃ gaṇhanti. Tasmā devena vāti avatvā ‘‘samaṇena vā brāhmaṇena vā kenaci vā manussabhūtenā’’ti vuttaṃ. Yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsūti bhagavato āgamanaṃ sutvā ‘‘amhehi gantvāpi na bhagavā ānīto, dūtaṃ pesetvāpi na pakkosāpito, sayameva pana mahābhikkhusaṅghaparivāro amhākaṃ vasanaṭṭhānaṃ sampatto, amhehi ca sandhāgārasālā kāritā, ettha mayaṃ dasabalaṃ ānetvā maṅgalaṃ bhaṇāpessāmā’’ti cintetvā upasaṅkamiṃsu.
297. Ubbhataka means "his name," or it is said thus because of height.

Sandhāgāra means "the sandhāgāra hall in the middle of the city."

"Samaṇena vā" here, because the deities take possession of their dwelling place at the very time of taking possession of the site of a house.

Therefore, instead of saying "by a god (devena)," it is said, "by a śramaṇa, or by a brāhmaṇa, or by anyone in human form."

"Yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsū" means, having heard of the arrival of the Blessed One (bhagavat), "even though we went, the Blessed One was not brought; even though a messenger was sent, he was not summoned; but the great retinue of bhikṣus (mahābhikkhusaṅghaparivāra) itself has arrived at our dwelling place, and we have had a sandhāgāra hall built, here we will bring the one with the ten powers (dasabala) and have auspicious things spoken," thinking thus, they approached.
298
298. Atha kho pāveyyakā mallā bhagavato adhivāsanaṃ viditvā uṭṭhāyāsanā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā yena sandhāgāraṃ tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā sabbasanthariṃ sabbasanthariṃ santhataṃ (ka.) sandhāgāraṃ santharitvā bhagavato āsanāni paññāpetvā udakamaṇikaṃ patiṭṭhapetvā telapadīpaṃ āropetvā yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhaṃsu. Ekamantaṃ ṭhitā kho te pāveyyakā mallā bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ – ‘‘sabbasantharisanthataṃ sabbasanthariṃ santhataṃ (sī. pī. ka.), bhante, sandhāgāraṃ, bhagavato āsanāni paññattāni, udakamaṇiko patiṭṭhāpito, telapadīpo āropito. Yassadāni, bhante, bhagavā kālaṃ maññatī’’ti.
Then, the Mallans of Pāvā, knowing the Blessed One's acceptance, rose from their seats, bowed down to the Blessed One, circumambulated him, and went to the assembly hall (saṃdhāgāra). Having approached it, they covered the assembly hall (saṃdhāgāra) entirely with felt rugs, arranged the seats for the Blessed One, set out a water vessel (udakamaṇika), and lit an oil lamp (telapadīpa). Then they approached the Blessed One; having approached, bowed down to the Blessed One, and stood to one side.

Standing to one side, the Mallans of Pāvā said this to the Blessed One: "The assembly hall (saṃdhāgāra) is entirely covered with felt rugs, venerable sir (bhante). The seats for the Blessed One are arranged, the water vessel (udakamaṇika) is set out, and the oil lamp (telapadīpa) is lit. May the Blessed One do what he thinks it is now time to do."
298.Yena sandhāgāraṃ tenupasaṅkamiṃsūti taṃ divasaṃ kira sandhāgāre cittakammaṃ niṭṭhapetvā aṭṭakā muttamattā honti, buddhā ca nāma araññajjhāsayā araññārāmā, antogāme vaseyyuṃ vā no vā. Tasmā bhagavato manaṃ jānitvāva paṭijaggissāmāti cintetvā te bhagavantaṃ upasaṅkamiṃsu. Idāni pana manaṃ labhitvā paṭijaggitukāmā yena sandhāgāraṃ tenupasaṅkamiṃsu . Sabbasantharinti yathā sabbaṃ santhataṃ hoti, evaṃ. Yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsūti ettha pana te mallarājāno sandhāgāraṃ paṭijaggitvā nagaravīthiyopi sammajjāpetvā dhaje ussāpetvā gehadvāresu puṇṇaghaṭe ca kadaliyo ca ṭhapāpetvā sakalanagaraṃ dīpamālādīhi vippakiṇṇatārakaṃ viya katvā khīrapāyake dārake khīraṃ pāyyetha, dahare kumāre lahuṃ lahuṃ bhojāpetvā sayāpetha, uccāsaddaṃ mā karittha, ajja ekarattiṃ satthā antogāme vasissati, buddhā nāma appasaddakāmā hontīti bheriṃ carāpetvā sayaṃ daṇḍadīpikaṃ ādāya yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsu.
"Where the assembly hall was, there they approached," that day, it is said, having finished the painted work in the assembly hall, the upper stories were exceedingly full. For Buddhas are by nature those who dwell in the wilderness, delighting in the wilderness, and they may or may not dwell within a village. Therefore, thinking, "Having understood the mind of the Blessed One, we will attend upon him," they approached the Blessed One. Now, having obtained the mind, desiring to attend upon [him], they approached where the assembly hall was.

"Sabbasantharinti" means just as everything is spread out, so it is.

"Yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkamiṃsūti" Here, those Malla kings, having prepared the assembly hall, and having caused the city streets to be swept, and having raised flags, and having placed full water pots and banana trees at the house doors, and having made the entire city like a scattering of stars with rows of lamps and so on, the milk-drinking boys were given milk to drink, the young boys were quickly fed and put to sleep, they did not make a loud noise, "Today, for one night, the Teacher will dwell within the village, for Buddhas are those who desire little noise," having caused the drum to be sounded, themselves taking a staff-torch, they approached where the Blessed One was.
299
299. Atha kho bhagavā nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya saddhiṃ bhikkhusaṅghena yena sandhāgāraṃ tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā pāde pakkhāletvā sandhāgāraṃ pavisitvā majjhimaṃ thambhaṃ nissāya puratthābhimukho nisīdi. Bhikkhusaṅghopi kho pāde pakkhāletvā sandhāgāraṃ pavisitvā pacchimaṃ bhittiṃ nissāya puratthābhimukho nisīdi bhagavantaṃyeva purakkhatvā. Pāveyyakāpi kho mallā pāde pakkhāletvā sandhāgāraṃ pavisitvā puratthimaṃ bhittiṃ nissāya pacchimābhimukhā nisīdiṃsu bhagavantaṃyeva purakkhatvā. Atha kho bhagavā pāveyyake malle bahudeva rattiṃ dhammiyā kathāya sandassetvā samādapetvā samuttejetvā sampahaṃsetvā uyyojesi – ‘‘abhikkantā kho, vāseṭṭhā, ratti. Yassadāni tumhe kālaṃ maññathā’’ti. ‘‘Evaṃ, bhante’’ti kho pāveyyakā mallā bhagavato paṭissutvā uṭṭhāyāsanā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā pakkamiṃsu.
Then the Blessed One, having dressed and taking his bowl and outer robe, went together with the Saṅgha of monks to the assembly hall ( sandhāgāraṃ ); having arrived, washed his feet, entered the assembly hall, and sat down facing east, leaning against the central pillar (majjhimaṃ thambhaṃ).

The Saṅgha of monks also washed their feet, entered the assembly hall, and sat down facing east, leaning against the western wall (pacchimaṃ bhittiṃ), with the Blessed One in front of them.

The Mallas of Pāvā also washed their feet, entered the assembly hall, and sat down facing west, leaning against the eastern wall (puratthimaṃ bhittiṃ), with the Blessed One in front of them.

Then the Blessed One, having instructed, encouraged, roused, and delighted the Mallas of Pāvā for much of the night with a Dhamma talk, dismissed them, saying, "The night is far gone, Vāseṭṭhas. Now is the time for you to do as you think fit." (yassadāni tumhe kālaṃ maññatha).

"Even so, venerable sir ( bhante )" the Mallas of Pāvā replied to the Blessed One, and rising from their seats, bowed down to the Blessed One, circumambulated him, and departed.
299.Bhagavantaṃyeva purakkhatvāti bhagavantaṃ purato katvā. Tattha bhagavā bhikkhūnañceva upāsakānañca majjhe nisinno ativiya virocati, samantapāsādiko suvaṇṇavaṇṇo abhirūpo dassanīyo. Purimakāyato suvaṇṇavaṇṇā rasmi uṭṭhahitvā asītihatthaṃ ṭhānaṃ gaṇhāti. Pacchimakāyato. Dakkhiṇahatthato. Vāmahatthato suvaṇṇavaṇṇā rasmi uṭṭhahitvā asītihatthaṃ ṭhānaṃ gaṇhāti. Upari kesantato paṭṭhāya sabbakesāvaṭṭehi moragīvavaṇṇā rasmi uṭṭhahitvā gaganatale asītihatthaṃ ṭhānaṃ gaṇhāti. Heṭṭhā pādatalehi pavāḷavaṇṇā rasmi uṭṭhahitvā ghanapathaviyaṃ asītihatthaṃ ṭhānaṃ gaṇhāti. Evaṃ samantā asīti hatthamattaṃ ṭhānaṃ chabbaṇṇā buddharasmiyo vijjotamānā vipphandamānā vidhāvanti. Sabbe disābhāgā suvaṇṇacampakapupphehi vikiriyamānā viya suvaṇṇaghaṭato nikkhantasuvaṇṇarasadhārāhi siñcamānā viya pasāritasuvaṇṇapaṭaparikkhittā viya verambhavātasamuṭṭhitakiṃsukakaṇikārapupphacuṇṇasamākiṇṇā viya ca vippakāsanti. Bhagavatopi asītianubyañjanabyāmappabhādvattiṃsavaralakkhaṇasamujjalaṃ sarīraṃ samuggatatārakaṃ viya gaganatalaṃ, vikasitamiva padumavanaṃ , sabbapāliphullo viya yojanasatiko pāricchattako paṭipāṭiyā ṭhapitānaṃ dvattiṃsacandānaṃ dvattiṃsasūriyānaṃ dvattiṃsacakkavattirājānaṃ dvattiṃsadevarājānaṃ dvattiṃsamahābrahmānaṃ siriyā siriṃ abhibhavamānaṃ viya virocati. Parivāretvā nisinnā bhikkhūpi sabbeva appicchā santuṭṭhā pavivittā asaṃsaṭṭhā āraddhavīriyā vattāro vacanakkhamā codakā pāpagarahino sīlasampannā samādhisampannā paññāvimutti vimuttiñāṇadassanasampannā. Tehi parivārito bhagavā rattakambalaparikkhitto viya suvaṇṇakkhandho , rattapadumavanasaṇḍamajjhagatā viya suvaṇṇanāvā, pavāḷavedikāparikkhitto viya suvaṇṇapāsādo virocittha. Asītimahātherāpi naṃ meghavaṇṇaṃ paṃsukūlaṃ pārupitvā maṇivammavammitā viya mahānāgā parivārayiṃsu vantarāgā bhinnakilesā vijaṭitajaṭā chinnabandhanā kule vā gaṇe vā alaggā. Iti bhagavā sayaṃ vītarāgo vītarāgehi, vītadoso vītadosehi, vītamoho vītamohehi, nittaṇho nittaṇhehi, nikkileso nikkilesehi, sayaṃ buddho bahussutabuddhehi parivārito pattaparivāritaṃ viya kesaraṃ, kesaraparivāritā viya kaṇṇikā, aṭṭhanāgasahassaparivārito viya chaddanto nāgarājā, navutihaṃsasahassaparivārito viya dhataraṭṭho haṃsarājā, senaṅgaparivārito viya cakkavattirājā, marugaṇaparivārito viya sakko devarājā, brahmagaṇaparivārito viya hārito mahābrahmā, asamena buddhavesena aparimāṇena buddhavilāsena tassaṃ parisati nisinno pāveyyake malle bahudeva rattiṃ dhammiyā kathāya sandassetvā uyyojesi. Ettha ca dhammikathā nāma sandhāgāraanumodanappaṭisaṃyuttā pakiṇṇakakathā veditabbā. Tadā hi bhagavā ākāsagaṅgaṃ otārento viya pathavojaṃ ākaḍḍhanto viya mahājambuṃ matthake gahetvā cālento viya yojaniyamadhugaṇḍaṃ cakkayantena pīḷetvā madhupānaṃ pāyamāno viya ca pāveyyakānaṃ mallānaṃ hitasukhāvahaṃ pakiṇṇakakathaṃ kathesi. dhammikathā
299. bhagavantaṃyeva purakkhatvā means having put the Blessed One in front.

There, the Blessed One, seated in the midst of the monks and lay followers, exceedingly shone forth, being entirely pleasing, of golden color, beautiful, and worthy of being seen.

From the front of his body, a golden-colored ray arose and occupied a space of eighty cubits.

From the back of his body...

From the right hand...

From the left hand, a golden-colored ray arose and occupied a space of eighty cubits.

Starting from the top of his head, with all the whorls of hair, a ray the color of a peacock's neck arose and occupied a space of eighty cubits in the sky.

From beneath the soles of his feet, a coral-colored ray arose and occupied a space of eighty cubits in the solid earth.

Thus, on all sides, a space of eighty cubits was filled by the six-colored Buddha rays, which were shining, pulsating, and darting about.

All directions shone forth as if they were being scattered with golden campaka flowers, as if they were being sprinkled with streams of golden essence flowing from a golden pitcher, as if they were surrounded by a stretched-out golden cloth, and as if they were covered with a powder of kiṃsuka and kaṇikāra flowers stirred up by the verambha wind.

The Blessed One's body, resplendent with the eighty minor marks, a fathom's span of light, and the thirty-two excellent marks, shone forth as if it were the sky filled with rising stars, as if it were a blooming lotus grove, like a pāricchatta tree a hundred yojanas in height covered with all sorts of flowers, and as if it were surpassing the glory of the thirty-two moons, thirty-two suns, thirty-two universal monarchs, thirty-two divine kings, and thirty-two Great Brahmās arranged in order.

The monks who were sitting around him were all of few desires, content, secluded, unassociated, with aroused energy, eloquent, patient of speech, instigators, censurers of evil, accomplished in virtue, accomplished in concentration, accomplished in wisdom-deliverance and vision of the knowledge of deliverance.

The Blessed One, surrounded by them, shone like a golden mountain surrounded by a red blanket, like a golden ship in the middle of a grove of red lotuses, and like a golden palace surrounded by a coral terrace.

The eighty great elders, having put on dust-heap robes the color of clouds and armored with jewels, like great nāgas, surrounded him, free from passion, with broken defilements, with untangled matted hair, with severed bonds, unattached to family or group.

Thus, the Blessed One, himself free from passion, surrounded by those free from passion; free from hatred, surrounded by those free from hatred; free from delusion, surrounded by those free from delusion; free from craving, surrounded by those free from craving; free from defilement, surrounded by those free from defilement; himself awakened, surrounded by the much-learned awakened ones, [shone] like pollen surrounded by petals, like a pericarp surrounded by pollen, like the nāga king Chaddanta surrounded by eight thousand nāgas, like the swan king Dhataraṭṭha surrounded by ninety thousand swans, like a universal monarch surrounded by his army, like Śakra, the king of the gods, surrounded by the host of Maruts, and like Harita, the Great Brahmā, surrounded by the host of Brahmās. Seated in that assembly in an unequaled Buddha's guise, with immeasurable Buddha's splendor, he encouraged the Mallas of Pāveyyaka for much of the night with a dhammī kathā and then dismissed them.

Here, dhammī kathā should be understood as diverse talk connected with approval of the meeting hall.

Then, the Blessed One spoke talk that was diverse and conducive to the benefit and happiness of the Mallas of Pāveyyaka, as if he were letting down the Ganges of the sky, as if he were drawing up the essence of the earth, as if he were taking the great jambu fruit on his head and shaking it, and as if he were squeezing the honey sac of a yojanīya with a wheel press and giving them a drink of honey.

Dhammī kathā.
300
300. Atha kho bhagavā acirapakkantesu pāveyyakesu mallesu tuṇhībhūtaṃ tuṇhībhūtaṃ bhikkhusaṃghaṃ anuviloketvā āyasmantaṃ sāriputtaṃ āmantesi – ‘‘vigatathinamiddho vigatathīnamiddho (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) kho, sāriputta, bhikkhusaṅgho. Paṭibhātu taṃ, sāriputta, bhikkhūnaṃ dhammīkathā. Piṭṭhi me āgilāyati. Tamahaṃ āyamissāmī’’ti āyameyyāmīti (syā. kaṃ.). ‘‘Evaṃ, bhante’’ti kho āyasmā sāriputto bhagavato paccassosi. Atha kho bhagavā catugguṇaṃ saṅghāṭiṃ paññapetvā dakkhiṇena passena sīhaseyyaṃ kappesi pāde pādaṃ accādhāya, sato sampajāno uṭṭhānasaññaṃ manasi karitvā. Bhinnanigaṇṭhavatthu
Then, not long after the Pāvā Mallans had departed, the Blessed One, surveying the silent Saṅgha of monks, addressed the venerable Sāriputta, saying: “Sāriputta, the Saṅgha of monks is free from sloth and torpor (vigatathinamiddho kho, sāriputta, bhikkhusaṅgho). Let a Dhamma talk occur to you, Sāriputta, for the monks. My back aches (piṭṭhi me āgilāyati). I will stretch it (tamahaṃ āyamissāmī’’ti).”

“As you say, venerable sir (evaṃ, bhante’ti), ” the venerable Sāriputta replied to the Blessed One.

Then the Blessed One folded his outer robe in four, and lay down on his right side in the lion's posture (sīhaseyyaṃ kappesi), placing one foot on top of the other, mindful and fully aware, keeping in mind the intention of rising.

The matter of the Dissension among the Nigaṇṭhas.
300.Tuṇhībhūtaṃ tuṇhībhūtanti yaṃ yaṃ disaṃ anuviloketi, tattha tattha tuṇhībhūtameva. Anuviloketvāti maṃsacakkhunā dibbacakkhunāti dvīhi cakkhūhi tato tato viloketvā. Maṃsacakkhunā hi nesaṃ bahiddhā iriyāpathaṃ pariggahesi. Tattha ekabhikkhussāpi neva hatthakukkuccaṃ na pādakukkuccaṃ ahosi, na koci sīsamukkhipi, na kathaṃ kathesi, na niddāyanto nisīdi. Sabbepi tīhi sikkhāhi sikkhitā nivāte padīpasikhā viya niccalā nisīdiṃsu. Iti nesaṃ imaṃ iriyāpathaṃ maṃsacakkhunā pariggahesi. Ālokaṃ pana vaḍḍhayitvā dibbacakkhunā hadayarūpaṃ disvā abbhantaragataṃ sīlaṃ olokesi. So anekasatānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ antokumbhiyaṃ jalamānaṃ padīpaṃ viya arahattupagaṃ sīlaṃ addasa. Āraddhavipassakā hi te bhikkhū. Iti nesaṃ sīlaṃ disvā ‘‘imepi bhikkhū mayhaṃ anucchavikā, ahampi imesaṃ anucchaviko’’ti cakkhutalesu nimittaṃ ṭhapetvā bhikkhusaṅghaṃ oloketvā āyasmantaṃ sāriputtaṃ āmantesi ‘‘piṭṭhi me āgilāyatī’’ti. Kasmā āgilāyati? Bhagavato hi chabbassāni mahāpadhānaṃ padahantassa mahantaṃ kāyadukkhaṃ ahosi. Athassa aparabhāge mahallakakāle piṭṭhivāto uppajji. Saṅghāṭiṃ paññāpetvāti sandhāgārassa kira ekapasse te rājāno kappiyamañcakaṃ paññapesuṃ ‘‘appeva nāma satthā nipajjeyyā’’ti. Satthāpi catūhi iriyāpathehi paribhuttaṃ imesaṃ mahapphalaṃ bhavissatīti tattha saṅghāṭiṃ paññāpetvā nipajji. Saṅghāṭiṃ paññāpetvā Bhinnanigaṇṭhavatthuvaṇṇanā
"Being silent, being silent": Whatever direction he surveys, it is only silent.

"Having surveyed": Having looked around from there in various directions with two eyes, the physical eye and the divine eye.

With the physical eye, he ascertained their outward conduct.

There, not even for one monk was there hesitation regarding the hands or hesitation regarding the feet; no one lifted his head, no one spoke a word, no one sat dozing.

All, trained in the three trainings, sat still like a lamp flame in a windless place.

Thus, with the physical eye, he ascertained this conduct of theirs.

But increasing the light, with the divine eye, having seen the heart-form, he looked at the virtue gone within.

He saw the virtue leading to arahantship, like a lamp burning within the inner vessel of many hundreds of monks.

For those monks were beginning insight.

Thus, having seen their virtue, fixing the sign in the eye sockets, thinking, "These monks are suitable for me, and I am suitable for them," surveying the community of monks, he addressed the venerable Sāriputta, "My back is aching."

Why is it aching? For the Blessed One, while undertaking the great struggle for six years, there was great bodily pain.

Then in the latter part of his life, in old age, back-wind arose for him.

"Having prepared the double robe": It seems those kings prepared a suitable couch on one side of the assembly hall, thinking, "Perhaps the Teacher might lie down."

The Teacher, thinking that it would be of great fruit for them, having used it with the four postures, lay down there, having prepared the double robe (saṃghāṭīṃ prajñāpetvā).

"Having prepared the double robe"
Explanation of the Story of the Split Nigaṇṭha
301
301. Tena kho pana samayena nigaṇṭho nāṭaputto pāvāyaṃ adhunākālaṅkato hoti. Tassa kālaṅkiriyāya bhinnā nigaṇṭhā dvedhikajātā ddheḷhakajātā (syā. kaṃ.) bhaṇḍanajātā kalahajātā vivādāpannā aññamaññaṃ mukhasattīhi vitudantā viharanti vicaranti (syā. kaṃ.) – ‘‘na tvaṃ imaṃ dhammavinayaṃ ājānāsi, ahaṃ imaṃ dhammavinayaṃ ājānāmi, kiṃ tvaṃ imaṃ dhammavinayaṃ ājānissasi! Micchāpaṭipanno tvamasi, ahamasmi sammāpaṭipanno. Sahitaṃ me, asahitaṃ te. Purevacanīyaṃ pacchā avaca, pacchāvacanīyaṃ pure avaca. Adhiciṇṇaṃ te viparāvattaṃ, āropito te vādo, niggahito tvamasi, cara vādappamokkhāya, nibbeṭhehi vā sace pahosī’’ti. Vadhoyeva kho maññe nigaṇṭhesu nāṭaputtiyesu vattati. Yepi yepi te (sī. pī.) nigaṇṭhassa nāṭaputtassa sāvakā gihī odātavasanā , tepi nigaṇṭhesu nāṭaputtiyesu nibbinnarūpā virattarūpā paṭivānarūpā, yathā taṃ durakkhāte dhammavinaye duppavedite aniyyānike anupasamasaṃvattanike asammāsambuddhappavedite bhinnathūpe appaṭisaraṇe.
301. At that time, Nigaṇṭha Nāṭaputta had recently died in Pāvā.

After his death, the Nigaṇṭhas were split into two factions, given to quarreling and disputing, stabbing one another with weapons of the mouth, saying, "You do not understand this Dhamma-vinaya; I understand this Dhamma-vinaya. How could you understand this Dhamma-vinaya? You are practicing wrongly; I am practicing rightly. I am consistent; you are inconsistent. You said last what should have been said first, and you said first what should have been said last. What you had so carefully thought out has been turned inside out. Your doctrine is refuted. You are proved wrong. Go and learn better, or disentangle yourself if you can!"

It seemed as though nothing but slaughter was being committed among the Nigaṇṭha Nāṭaputtas.

And even those lay disciples of Nigaṇṭha Nāṭaputta, dressed in white, were disgusted, dismayed, and disappointed with the Nigaṇṭha Nāṭaputtas, and at how poorly proclaimed and poorly expounded that Dhamma-vinaya was, not leading out, not conducive to calming, expounded by one who was not rightly self-awakened, its foundation now broken, without an arbiter.
301.Tassa kālaṅkiriyāyātiādīsu yaṃ vattabbaṃ, taṃ sabbaṃ heṭṭhā vuttameva.
As for what is to be said regarding "tassa kālaṅkiriyāyā" and the like, all that has already been said below.
302
302. Atha kho āyasmā sāriputto bhikkhū āmantesi – ‘‘nigaṇṭho, āvuso, nāṭaputto pāvāyaṃ adhunākālaṅkato, tassa kālaṅkiriyāya bhinnā nigaṇṭhā dvedhikajātā…pe… bhinnathūpe appaṭisaraṇe’’. ‘‘Evañhetaṃ, āvuso, hoti durakkhāte dhammavinaye duppavedite aniyyānike anupasamasaṃvattanike asammāsambuddhappavedite. Ayaṃ kho panāvuso amhākaṃ asmākaṃ (pī.) bhagavatā bhagavato (ka. sī.) dhammo svākkhāto suppavedito niyyāniko upasamasaṃvattaniko sammāsambuddhappavedito. Tattha sabbeheva saṅgāyitabbaṃ, na vivaditabbaṃ, yathayidaṃ brahmacariyaṃ addhaniyaṃ assa ciraṭṭhitikaṃ, tadassa bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ. ‘‘Katamo cāvuso, amhākaṃ bhagavatā bhagavato (ka. sī.) dhammo svākkhāto suppavedito niyyāniko upasamasaṃvattaniko sammāsambuddhappavedito; yattha sabbeheva saṅgāyitabbaṃ, na vivaditabbaṃ, yathayidaṃ brahmacariyaṃ addhaniyaṃ assa ciraṭṭhitikaṃ, tadassa bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ? Ekakaṃ
“Friends, Nigaṇṭha Nāṭaputta has recently died in Pāvā. On his death, the Nigaṇṭhas have split into two factions… their shrine is broken, left without a refuge.”

“That’s the way it is, friends, with a poorly proclaimed Dhamma-Vinaya, poorly expounded, not leading out, not conducive to calming, expounded by one who is not rightly self-awakened.

“But, friends, our dharma is well-proclaimed by the Blessed One, well-expounded, leading out, conducive to calming, expounded by one who is rightly self-awakened. There it should all be recited together, without dispute, so that this holy life (brahmacariya) will endure & last a long time, and that will be for the benefit & happiness of many, out of sympathy for the world, for the welfare, benefit, & happiness of devas and human beings.

“And which is our dharma, friends, that is well expounded by the Blessed One, leading out, conducive to calming, expounded by one who is rightly self-awakened, that should all be recited together, without dispute, so that this holy life (brahmacariya) will endure & last a long time, and that will be for the benefit & happiness of many, out of sympathy for the world, for the welfare, benefit, & happiness of devas and human beings?
302.Āmantesīti bhaṇḍanādivūpasamakaraṃ svākhyātaṃ dhammaṃ desetukāmo āmantesi. Ekakavaṇṇanā
"āmantesī": He invited, meaning he invited, wishing to teach the well-proclaimed Dharma which pacifies quarrels, etc.

Explanation of the one.
303
303. ‘‘Atthi kho, āvuso, tena bhagavatā jānatā passatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena eko dhammo sammadakkhāto. Tattha sabbeheva saṅgāyitabbaṃ, na vivaditabbaṃ, yathayidaṃ brahmacariyaṃ addhaniyaṃ assa ciraṭṭhitikaṃ , tadassa bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ. Katamo eko dhammo? Sabbe sattā āhāraṭṭhitikā. Sabbe sattā saṅkhāraṭṭhitikā. Ayaṃ kho, āvuso, tena bhagavatā jānatā passatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena eko dhammo sammadakkhāto. Tattha sabbeheva saṅgāyitabbaṃ, na vivaditabbaṃ , yathayidaṃ brahmacariyaṃ addhaniyaṃ assa ciraṭṭhitikaṃ, tadassa bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ. Dukaṃ
303. “There is, friends (āvusho), one dharma that is rightly expounded by the Blessed One—the one who knows (jānatā), the one who sees (passatā), the Worthy One (arahatā), rightly self-awakened (sammāsambuddhena).

There all should recite together (saṅgāyitabbaṃ), without dispute (na vivaditabbaṃ), so that this holy life (brahmacariya) will endure & last a long time (ciraṭṭhitikaṃ), and that will be for the benefit (hitāya) & happiness (sukhāya) of many (bahujanā), out of sympathy (anukampāya) for the world (loka), for the welfare (atthaya), benefit (hitāya), & happiness (sukhāya) of gods (deva) & human beings (manussānaṃ).

What is that one dharma (eko dhammo)? All beings (sabbe sattā) are maintained by nutriment (āhāraṭṭhitikā).
All beings (sabbe sattā) are maintained by fabrication (saṅkhāraṭṭhitikā).

This, friends (āvusho), is one dharma that is rightly expounded by the Blessed One—the one who knows (jānatā), the one who sees (passatā), the Worthy One (arahatā), rightly self-awakened (sammāsambuddhena).
There all should recite together (saṅgāyitabbaṃ), without dispute (na vivaditabbaṃ), so that this holy life (brahmacariya) will endure & last a long time (ciraṭṭhitikaṃ), and that will be for the benefit (hitāya) & happiness (sukhāya) of many (bahujanā), out of sympathy (anukampāya) for the world (loka), for the welfare (atthaya), benefit (hitāya), & happiness (sukhāya) of gods (deva) & human beings (manussānaṃ).

Dukaṃ
303.Tatthāti tasmiṃ dhamme. Saṅgāyitabbanti samaggehi gāyitabbaṃ, ekavacanehi aviruddhavacanehi bhaṇitabbaṃ. Na vivaditabbanti atthe vā byañjane vā vivādo na kātabbo. Eko dhammoti ekakadukatikādivasena bahudhā sāmaggirasaṃ dassetukāmo paṭhamaṃ tāva ‘‘eko dhammo’’ti āha. Sabbe sattāti kāmabhavādīsu saññābhavādīsu ekavokārabhavādīsu ca sabbabhavesu sabbe sattā. Āhāraṭṭhitikāti āhārato ṭhiti etesanti āhāraṭṭhitikā. Iti sabbasattānaṃ ṭhiti hetu āhāro nāma eko dhammo amhākaṃ satthārā yāthāvato ñatvā sammadakkhāto āvusoti dīpeti. Nanu ca evaṃ sante yaṃ vuttaṃ ‘‘asaññasattā devā ahetukā anāhārā aphassakā’’tiādi, (vibha. 1017) taṃ vacanaṃ virujjhatīti, na virujjhati. Tesañhi jhānaṃ āhāro hoti. Evaṃ santepi ‘‘cattārome, bhikkhave, āhārā bhūtānaṃ vā sattānaṃ ṭhitiyā sambhavesīnaṃ vā anuggahāya. Katame cattāro? Kabaḷīkāro āhāro oḷāriko vā sukhumo vā, phasso dutiyo, manosañcetanā tatiyā, viññāṇaṃ catuttha’’nti (saṃ. ni. 2.11) idampi virujjhatīti, idampi na virujjhati. Etasmiñhi sutte nippariyāyena āhāralakkhaṇāva dhammā āhārāti vuttā. Idha pana pariyāyena paccayo āhāroti vutto. Sabbadhammānañhi paccayo laddhuṃ vaṭṭati. So ca yaṃ yaṃ phalaṃ janeti, taṃ taṃ āharati nāma, tasmā āhāroti vuccati. Tenevāha ‘‘avijjampāhaṃ, bhikkhave, sāhāraṃ vadāmi, no anāhāraṃ. Ko ca, bhikkhave, avijjāya āhāro? Pañcanīvaraṇātissa vacanīyaṃ. Pañcanīvaraṇepāhaṃ, bhikkhave, sāhāre vadāmi, no anāhāre. Ko ca, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ nīvaraṇānaṃ āhāro? Ayonisomanasikārotissa vacanīya’’nti (a. ni. 10.61). Ayaṃ idha adhippeto. Etasmiñhi paccayāhāre gahite pariyāyāhāropi nippariyāyāhāropi sabbo gahitova hoti. Tattha asaññabhave paccayāhāro labbhati. Anuppanne hi buddhe titthāyatane pabbajitā vāyokasiṇe parikammaṃ katvā catutthajjhānaṃ nibbattetvā tato vuṭṭhāya dhī cittaṃ, dhibbatetaṃ cittaṃ cittassa nāma abhāvoyeva sādhu, cittañhi nissāyeva vadhabandhādipaccayaṃ dukkhaṃ uppajjati. Citte asati natthetanti khantiṃ ruciṃ uppādetvā aparihīnajjhānā kālaṅkatvā asaññabhave nibbattanti. Yo yassa iriyāpatho manussaloke paṇihito ahosi, so tena iriyāpathena nibbattitvā pañca kappasatāni ṭhito vā nisinno vā nipanno vā hoti. Evarūpānampi sattānaṃ paccayāhāro labbhati. Te hi yaṃ jhānaṃ bhāvetvā nibbattā, tadeva nesaṃ paccayo hoti. Yathā jiyāvegena khittasaro yāva jiyāvego atthi, tāva gacchati, evaṃ yāva jhānapaccayo atthi, tāva tiṭṭhanti. Tasmiṃ niṭṭhite khīṇavego saro viya patanti. Ye pana te nerayikā neva uṭṭhānaphalūpajīvī na puññaphalūpajīvīti vuttā, tesaṃ ko āhāroti ? Tesaṃ kammameva āhāro. Kiṃ pañca āhārā atthīti ce. Pañca, na pañcāti idaṃ na vattabbaṃ. Nanu paccayo āhāroti vuttametaṃ. Tasmā yena kammena te niraye nibbattā, tadeva tesaṃ ṭhitipaccayattā āhāro hoti. Yaṃ sandhāya idaṃ vuttaṃ ‘‘na ca tāva kālaṅkaroti, yāva na taṃ pāpakammaṃ byantī hotī’’ti (ma. ni. 3.250). Kabaḷīkāraṃ āhāraṃ ārabbha cettha vivādo na kātabbo. Mukhe uppanno kheḷopi hi tesaṃ āhārakiccaṃ sādheti. Kheḷopi hi niraye dukkhavedaniyo hutvā paccayo hoti, sagge sukhavedaniyo. Iti kāmabhave nippariyāyena cattāro āhārā. Rūpārūpabhavesu ṭhapetvā asaññaṃ sesānaṃ tayo. Asaññānañceva avasesānañca paccayāhāroti iminā āhārena ‘‘sabbe sattā āhāraṭṭhitikā’’ti etaṃ pañhaṃ kathetvā ‘‘ayaṃ kho āvuso’’ti evaṃ niyyātanampi ‘‘atthi kho āvuso’’ti puna uddharaṇampi akatvā ‘‘sabbe sattā saṅkhāraṭṭhitikā’’ti dutiyapañhaṃ vissajjesi. Kasmā pana na niyyātesi na uddharittha? Tattha tattha niyyātiyamānepi uddhariyamānepi pariyāpuṇituṃ vācetuṃ dukkhaṃ hoti, tasmā dve ekābaddhe katvā vissajjesi. Imasmimpi vissajjane heṭṭhā vuttapaccayova attano phalassa saṅkharaṇato saṅkhāroti vutto. Iti heṭṭhā āhārapaccayo kathito, idha saṅkhārapaccayoti ayamettha heṭṭhimato viseso. ‘‘Heṭṭhā nippariyāyāhāro gahito, idha pariyāyāhāroti evaṃ gahite viseso pākaṭo bhaveyya, no ca gaṇhiṃsū’ti mahāsīvatthero āha. Indriyabaddhassapi hi anindriyabaddhassapi paccayo laddhuṃ vaṭṭati. Vinā paccayena dhammo nāma natthi. Tattha anindriyabaddhassa tiṇarukkhalatādino pathavīraso āporaso ca paccayo hoti. Deve avassante hi tiṇādīni milāyanti, vassante ca pana haritāni honti. Iti tesaṃ pathavīraso āporaso ca paccayo hoti. Indriyabaddhassa avijjā taṇhā kammaṃ āhāroti evamādayo paccayā, iti heṭṭhā paccayoyeva āhāroti kathito, idha saṅkhāroti. Ayamevettha viseso. Ayaṃkho, āvusoti āvuso amhākaṃ satthārā mahābodhimaṇḍe nisīditvā sayaṃ sabbaññutaññāṇena sacchikatvā ayaṃ ekadhammo desito. Tattha ekadhamme tumhehi sabbeheva saṅgāyitabbaṃ na vivaditabbaṃ. Yathayidaṃ brahmacariyanti yathā saṅgāyamānānaṃ tumhākaṃ idaṃ sāsanabrahmacariyaṃ addhaniyaṃ assa. Ekena hi bhikkhunā ‘‘atthi, kho āvuso, eko dhammo sammadakkhāto. Katamo eko dhammo? Sabbe sattā āhāraṭṭhitikā. Sabbe sattā saṅkhāraṭṭhitikā’’ti kathite tassa kathaṃ sutvā añño kathessati. Tassapi aññoti evaṃ paramparakathāniyamena idaṃ brahmacariyaṃ ciraṃ tiṭṭhamānaṃ sadevakassa lokassa atthāya hitāya bhavissatīti ekakavasena dhammasenāpati sāriputtatthero sāmaggirasaṃ dassesīti. Ayaṃ kho, āvuso Yathayidaṃ brahmacariya Ekakavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā. Dukavaṇṇanā
Tatthā: That is, in that Dharma.

Saṅgāyitabba: To be chanted by those in harmony, to be recited with concordant words in unison.

Na vivaditabba: There should be no dispute in meaning or expression.

Eko dhammo: "One Dharma," intending to show the taste of harmony in many ways by means of the one, the dyads, the triads, etc., first he said, "one Dharma."

Sabbe sattā: "All beings," that is, all beings in all existences, in the desire realm and so on, in the realm of perception and so on, in the realm of one-idea and so on.

Āhāraṭṭhitikā: Āhāraṭṭhitikā means the condition of being maintained by nutriment, of those beings.

Thus, the Buddha, our teacher, illuminates that nutriment is one dharma for the sake of the maintenance of all beings, having known it as it is, and having well explained it, O friends.

Now, if it is like that, the statement, "Unconscious beings, gods, are without cause, without nutriment, without contact," and so forth (Vibha. 1017), would be contradicted. It is not contradicted. For jhāna is their nutriment. Even when that exists, [the Buddha] said, "There are these four kinds of nutriment, monks, for the maintenance of beings that have already come to be and for the support of those seeking a new existence. What are the four? Edible food, coarse or subtle, is the first; contact is the second; mental volition is the third; and consciousness is the fourth" (Saṃ. Ni. 2.11). If this, too, is contradictory, this too is not contradictory. In this sūtra, the characteristics of nutriment themselves are called nutriment without qualification. Here, however, a condition is called nutriment in a qualified way. For a condition is suitable to be obtained by all things. And that which generates any fruit, that it brings, therefore it is called nutriment. Therefore, he said, "I say that ignorance, monks, has nutriment, not without nutriment. And what, monks, is the nutriment for ignorance? It should be said: the five hindrances. And I say that the five hindrances, monks, have nutriment, not without nutriment. And what, monks, is the nutriment for the five hindrances? It should be said: improper attention" (A. Ni. 10.61).

This is the meaning intended here.

When a conditional nutriment is taken, then qualified nutriment and unqualified nutriment are all taken.

There, in the unconscious realm, conditional nutriment is obtained.

Indeed, when Buddhas have not arisen, ascetics in other sects, having performed the preliminary act on the air kasina, having generated the fourth jhāna, and having arisen from that, [think]: "Consciousness is bad; this is consciousness; the non-existence of consciousness is good," for suffering arises only dependent on the condition of killing, binding, etc., because of consciousness.

When there is no consciousness, they produce forbearance and inclination, and without decline of jhāna, having made an end of life, they are born in the unconscious realm.

Whatever posture of his, standing, sitting or lying down, was practiced in the human world, in that same posture, having been born there, he remains for five hundred kalpas.

Even to such beings, conditional nutriment is obtained.

For the jhāna which they cultivated and in which they were born, that itself is their condition.
Just as an arrow shot with the force of a bow travels as long as the force of the bow lasts, so too, [the beings] remain as long as the condition of jhāna lasts. When that is finished, they fall like an arrow whose force is spent.

But what is the food of those hell-beings who are said to subsist neither on the fruit of effort nor on the fruit of merit? Their karma itself is their food.

What, are there five kinds of food? It should not be said, "five, not five."

Surely, it has been said that a condition is food.

Therefore, by whatever karma they are reborn in hell, that itself is their food, because it is the condition for their existence.

Referring to this, it was said: "And he does not die as long as that evil karma is not exhausted" (Majjhima Nikāya 3.250).


Here, no dispute should be made regarding the food of morsels.

Even the saliva that arises in the mouth accomplishes their function of food.

Even saliva, having become a condition, is painful to experience in hell, and pleasant to experience in heaven.

Thus, in the realm of desire, there are four kinds of food, without any alternative.

In the realms of form and formlessness, setting aside the non-percipient beings, there are three.

Since paccaya is food, after stating the question, "All beings are sustained by food," with this food as paccaya, without stating "This is the cause, friend," nor again taking up "There is indeed, friend," he dismissed the second question: "All beings are sustained by formations."


Why then did he not state and take up [the questions] distinctly? Because it is difficult to learn and recite each one when stating and taking them up distinctly. Therefore, he dismissed [the two questions] by making them one.

In this dismissal too, the saṅkhāra is stated to be the condition previously stated, since it produces the fruit of itself.

Thus, āhāra-paccaya (food condition) has been spoken of previously; here, it is saṅkhāra-paccaya (formation condition). This is the difference between the former and the latter.

"Previously, food without alternatives was taken; here, food with alternatives." Thus, if it is taken in this way, the distinction would be clear. But the Elder Mahāsīva said that they did not take it [in that way].

Even for one bound by the senses and for one not bound by the senses, it is fitting to obtain a condition.

Without a condition, there is no dharma.

There, for one not bound by the senses, earth-essence and water-essence are the condition for grass, trees, creepers, and so on.

When the sky does not rain, grass and so on wither; and when it rains, they become green.

Thus, for them, earth-essence and water-essence are the condition.

For one bound by the senses, ignorance, craving, and karma are food. Thus, previously, it was said that paccaya itself is food; here, it is saṅkhāra.

This alone is the distinction here.


"This, friend," means, friend, this one dharma was taught by our teacher after sitting at the great Bodhi-tree and realizing it himself through the knowledge of omniscience.

There, in that one dharma, you should all be in harmony and not quarrel.

"As this holy life..." means, as you are in harmony, may this holy life of the teaching be lasting.

Indeed, by one monk, "There is, friend, one dharma rightly expounded..."
Which is the one principle? All beings are maintained by nutriment (āhāra).
When it is said, "All beings are maintained by conditions (saṅkhāra)", how will another speak having heard that?
Even of that other, thus, by the rule of continuous story, this religious life (brahmacariyaṃ), long enduring, will be for the welfare and benefit of the world with its gods (sadevakassa lokassa). Thus, in a singular way, the general of the Dhamma, the venerable Sāriputta, showed the taste of concord.
This Indeed, friend, just as this religious life (brahmacariya) The section based on one item is finished (ekakavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā).
Section based on two items
304
304. ‘‘Atthi kho, āvuso, tena bhagavatā jānatā passatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena dve dhammā sammadakkhātā. Tattha sabbeheva saṅgāyitabbaṃ, na vivaditabbaṃ, yathayidaṃ brahmacariyaṃ addhaniyaṃ assa ciraṭṭhitikaṃ, tadassa bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ. Katame dve dve dhammo (syā. kaṃ.) evamuparipi? ‘‘Nāmañca rūpañca. ‘‘Avijjā ca bhavataṇhā ca. ‘‘Bhavadiṭṭhi ca vibhavadiṭṭhi ca. ‘‘Ahirikañca ahirīkañca (katthaci) anottappañca. ahirīkañca (katthaci) ‘‘Hirī ca ottappañca. ‘‘Dovacassatā ca pāpamittatā ca. ‘‘Sovacassatā ca kalyāṇamittatā ca. ‘‘Āpattikusalatā ca āpattivuṭṭhānakusalatā ca. ‘‘Samāpattikusalatā ca samāpattivuṭṭhānakusalatā ca. ‘‘Dhātukusalatā ca manasikārakusalatā ca. ‘‘Āyatanakusalatā ca paṭiccasamuppādakusalatā ca. ‘‘Ṭhānakusalatā ca aṭṭhānakusalatā ca. ‘‘Ajjavañca lajjavañca. ‘‘Khanti ca soraccañca. ‘‘Sākhalyañca paṭisanthāro ca. ‘‘Avihiṃsā ca soceyyañca. ‘‘Muṭṭhassaccañca asampajaññañca. ‘‘Sati ca sampajaññañca . ‘‘Indriyesu aguttadvāratā ca bhojane amattaññutā ca. ‘‘Indriyesu guttadvāratā ca bhojane mattaññutā ca. ‘‘Paṭisaṅkhānabalañca paṭisandhānabalañca (syā.) bhāvanābalañca. paṭisandhānabalañca (syā.) ‘‘Satibalañca samādhibalañca. ‘‘Samatho ca vipassanā ca. ‘‘Samathanimittañca paggahanimittañca. ‘‘Paggaho ca avikkhepo ca. ‘‘Sīlavipatti ca diṭṭhivipatti ca. ‘‘Sīlasampadā ca diṭṭhisampadā ca. ‘‘Sīlavisuddhi ca diṭṭhivisuddhi ca. ‘‘Diṭṭhivisuddhi kho pana yathā diṭṭhissa ca padhānaṃ. ‘‘Saṃvego ca saṃvejanīyesu ṭhānesu saṃviggassa ca yoniso padhānaṃ. ‘‘Asantuṭṭhitā ca kusalesu dhammesu appaṭivānitā ca padhānasmiṃ. ‘‘Vijjā ca vimutti ca. ‘‘Khayeñāṇaṃ anuppādeñāṇaṃ. ‘‘Ime kho, āvuso, tena bhagavatā jānatā passatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena dve dhammā sammadakkhātā. Tattha sabbeheva saṅgāyitabbaṃ, na vivaditabbaṃ, yathayidaṃ brahmacariyaṃ addhaniyaṃ assa ciraṭṭhitikaṃ, tadassa bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ. Tikaṃ
304. Friends, these are the two dharmas that are rightly expounded by the Blessed One—the one who knows, the one who sees, the Worthy One (arhat), rightly self-awakened (sammāsambuddha). That should all be recited together, without dispute, so that this holy life (brahmacariya) will endure & last a long time, and that will be for the benefit & happiness of many, out of sympathy for the world, for the welfare, benefit, & happiness of devas & human beings.

What are the two?

Name (nāma) and form (rūpa).

Ignorance (avijjā) and craving for becoming (bhavataṇhā).

The view of being (bhavadiṭṭhi) and the view of non-being (vibhavadiṭṭhi).

Shamelessness (ahirikañca) and recklessness (anottappañca).

Moral shame (hirī) and moral dread (ottappañca).

Being difficult to instruct (dovacassatā) & evil friendship (pāpamittatā).

Being easy to instruct (sovacassatā) & admirable friendship (kalyāṇamittatā).

Skill in offenses (āpattikusalatā) & skill in exiting from offenses (āpattivuṭṭhānakusalatā).

Skill in attainments (samāpattikusalatā) & skill in exiting from attainments (samāpattivuṭṭhānakusalatā).

Skill in properties (dhātukusalatā) & skill in attention (manasikārakusalatā).

Skill in sense media (āyatanakusalatā) & skill in dependent co-arising (paṭiccasamuppādakusalatā).

Skill in what is possible (ṭhānakusalatā) & skill in what is impossible (aṭṭhānakusalatā).

Uprightness (ajjavañca) & gentleness (lajjavañca).

Patience (khanti) & gentleness (soraccañca).

Sociability (sākhalyañca) and cordiality (paṭisanthāro ca).

Non-harming (avihiṃsā) and purity (soceyyañca).

Forgetfulness (muṭṭhassaccañca) and lack of clear comprehension (asampajaññañca).

Mindfulness (sati) and clear comprehension (sampajaññañca).

Unguarded sense doors (indriyesu aguttadvāratā) & not knowing moderation in eating (bhojane amattaññutā).

Guarded sense doors (indriyesu guttadvāratā) & knowing moderation in eating (bhojane mattaññutā).

Strength of reflection (paṭisaṅkhānabalañca) & strength of development (bhāvanābalañca).

Strength of mindfulness (satibalañca) & strength of samādhi (samādhibalañca).

Serenity (samatho) and insight (vipassanā ca).

The sign of serenity (samathanimittañca) and the sign of striving (paggahanimittañca).
• "Grasp and non-distraction, and

• "Failure of virtue and failure of view, and

• "Success of virtue and success of view, and

• "Purity of virtue and purity of view, and

• "Purity of view and the exertion of one with such a view.

• "Agitation, and appropriate exertion in places causing agitation for one who is agitated, and

• "Lack of satisfaction and non-opposition in wholesome qualities, and exertion, and

• "Clear knowing & release: (vijjā ca vimutti ca.)

• "Knowledge of cessation & knowledge of non-arising.

• “These, friends, are the two dharmas that are rightly expounded by the Blessed One—the one who knows, the one who sees, the Worthy One, rightly self-awakened.

That should all be recited together, without dispute, so that this holy life will endure & last a long time, and that will be for the benefit & happiness of many, out of sympathy for the world, for the welfare, benefit, & happiness of devas & human beings.

Tikaṃ
304. Iti ekakavasena sāmaggirasaṃ dassetvā idāni dukavasena dassetuṃ puna desanaṃ ārabhi. Tattha nāmarūpaduke nāmanti cattāro arūpino khandhā nibbānañca. Tattha cattāro khandhā nāmanaṭṭhena nāmaṃ. Nāmanaṭṭhenāti nāmakaraṇaṭṭhena. Yathā hi mahājanasammatattā mahāsammatassa ‘‘mahāsammato’’ti nāmaṃ ahosi, yathā mātāpitaro ‘‘ayaṃ tisso nāma hotu, phusso nāma hotū’’ti evaṃ puttassa kittimanāmaṃ karonti, yathā vā ‘‘dhammakathiko vinayadharo’’ti guṇato nāmaṃ āgacchati, na evaṃ vedanādīnaṃ. Vedanādayo hi mahāpathavīādayo viya attano nāmaṃ karontāva uppajjanti. Tesu uppannesu tesaṃ nāmaṃ uppannameva hoti. Na hi vedanaṃ uppannaṃ ‘‘tvaṃ vedanā nāma hohī’’ti, koci bhaṇati, na cassā yena kenaci kāraṇena nāmaggahaṇakiccaṃ atthi, yathā pathaviyā uppannāya ‘‘tvaṃ pathavī nāma hohī’’ti nāmaggahaṇakiccaṃ natthi, cakkavāḷasinerumhi candimasūriyanakkhattesu uppannesu ‘‘tvaṃ cakkavāḷaṃ nāma, tvaṃ nakkhattaṃ nāma hohī’’ti nāmaggahaṇakiccaṃ natthi, nāmaṃ uppannameva hoti, opapātikā paññatti nipatati, evaṃ vedanāya uppannāya ‘‘tvaṃ vedanā nāma hohī’’ti nāmaggahaṇakiccaṃ natthi, tāya uppannāya vedanāti nāmaṃ uppannameva hoti. Saññādīsupi eseva nayo atītepi hi vedanā vedanāyeva. Saññā. Saṅkhārā. Viññāṇaṃ viññāṇameva. Anāgatepi. Paccuppannepi. Nibbānaṃ pana sadāpi nibbānamevāti. Nāmanaṭṭhena nāmaṃ. Namanaṭṭhenāpi cettha cattāro khandhā nāmaṃ. Te hi ārammaṇābhimukhaṃ namanti. Nāmanaṭṭhena sabbampi nāmaṃ. Cattāro hi khandhā ārammaṇe aññamaññaṃ nāmenti , nibbānaṃ ārammaṇādhipatipaccayatāya attani anavajjadhamme nāmeti. Rūpanti cattāro ca mahābhūtā catunnañca mahābhūtānaṃ upādāya rūpaṃ, taṃ sabbampi ruppanaṭṭhena rūpaṃ. Tassa vitthārakathā visuddhimagge vuttanayeneva veditabbā. Rūpa rūpaṃ. Avijjāti dukkhādīsu aññāṇaṃ. Ayampi vitthārato visuddhimagge kathitāyeva. Bhavataṇhāti bhavapatthanā. Yathāha ‘‘tattha katamā bhavataṇhā? Yo bhavesu bhavacchando’’tiādi (dha. sa. 1319). Avijjā Bhavataṇhā Bhavadiṭṭhīti bhavo vuccati sassataṃ, sassatavasena uppajjanakadiṭṭhi. Sā ‘‘tattha katamā bhavadiṭṭhi? ‘Bhavissati attā ca loko cā’ti yā evarūpā diṭṭhi diṭṭhigata’’ntiādinā (dha. sa. 1320) nayena abhidhamme vitthāritā. Vibhavadiṭṭhīti vibhavo vuccati ucchedaṃ, ucchedavasena uppajjanakadiṭṭhi. Sāpi ‘‘tattha katamā vibhavadiṭṭhi? ‘Na bhavissati attā ca loko cā’ti (dha. sa. 285). Yā evarūpā diṭṭhi diṭṭhigata’’ntiādinā (dha. sa. 1321) nayena tattheva vitthāritā. Bhavadiṭṭhī Vibhavadiṭṭhī Ahirikanti ‘‘yaṃ na hirīyati hirīyitabbenā’’ti (dha. sa. 1328) evaṃ vitthāritā nillajjatā. Anottappanti ‘‘yaṃ na ottappati ottappitabbenā’’ti (dha. sa. 1329) evaṃ vitthārito abhāyanakaākāro. Ahirika Anottappa Hirī ca ottappañcāti ‘‘yaṃ hirīyati hirīyitabbena, ottappati ottappitabbenā’’ti (dha. sa. 1330-31) evaṃ vitthāritāni hiriottappāni. Api cettha ajjhattasamuṭṭhānā hirī, bahiddhāsamuṭṭhānaṃ ottappaṃ. Attādhipateyyā hirī, lokādhipateyyaṃ ottappaṃ. Lajjāsabhāvasaṇṭhitā hirī, bhayasabhāvasaṇṭhitaṃ ottappaṃ. Vitthārakathā panettha sabbākārena visuddhimagge vuttā. Hirī ca ottappañcā hirī, ottappaṃ. Dovacassatāti dukkhaṃ vaco etasmiṃ vippaṭikūlagāhimhi vipaccanīkasāte anādare puggaleti dubbaco, tassa kammaṃ dovacassaṃ, tassa bhāvo dovacassatā. Vitthārato panesā ‘‘tattha katamā dovacassatā? Sahadhammike vuccamāne dovacassāya’’nti (dha. sa. 1332) abhidhamme āgatā. Sā atthato saṅkhārakkhandho hoti. ‘‘Catunnañca khandhānaṃ etenākārena pavattānaṃ etaṃ adhivacana’’nti vadanti. Pāpamittatāti pāpā assaddhādayo puggalā etassa mittāti pāpamitto, tassa bhāvo pāpamittatā. Vitthārato panesā – ‘‘tattha katamā pāpamittatā? Ye te puggalā assaddhā dussīlā appassutā maccharino duppaññā. Yā tesaṃ sevanā nisevanā saṃsevanā bhajanā saṃbhajanā bhatti saṃbhatti taṃsampavaṅkatā’’ti (dha. sa. 1333) evaṃ āgatā. Sāpi atthato dovacassatā viya daṭṭhabbā. Dovacassatā dovacassatā. Pāpamittatā Sovacassatā ca kalyāṇamittatā ca vuttappaṭipakkhanayena veditabbā. Ubhopi panetā idha lokiyalokuttaramissakā kathitā. Sovacassatā ca kalyāṇamittatā Āpattikusalatāti ‘‘pañcapi āpattikkhandhā āpattiyo, sattapi āpattikkhandhā āpattiyo. Yā tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ āpattikusalatā paññā pajānanā’’ti (dha. sa. 1336) evaṃ vutto āpattikusalabhāvo. Āpattikusalatā Āpattivuṭṭhānakusalatāti ‘‘yā tāhi āpattīhi vuṭṭhānakusalatā paññā pajānanā’’ti (dha. sa. 1337) evaṃ vuttā saha kammavācāya āpattīhi vuṭṭhānaparicchedajānanā paññā. Āpattivuṭṭhānakusalatā Samāpattikusalatāti ‘‘atthi savitakkasavicārā samāpatti, atthi avitakkavicāramattā samāpatti, atthi avitakkaavicārā samāpatti. Yā tāsaṃ samāpattīnaṃ kusalatā paññā pajānanā’’ti (dha. sa. 1338) evaṃ vuttā saha parikammena appanāparicchedajānanā paññā. Samāpattivuṭṭhānakusalatāti ‘‘yā tāhi samāpattīhi vuṭṭhānakusalatā paññā pajānanā’’ti (dha. sa. 1339) evaṃ vuttā yathāparicchinnasamayavaseneva samāpattito vuṭṭhānasamatthā ‘‘ettakaṃ gate sūriye uṭṭhahissāmī’’ti vuṭṭhānakālaparicchedakā paññā. Samāpattikusalatā Samāpattivuṭṭhānakusalatā Dhātukusalatāti ‘‘aṭṭhārasa dhātuyo cakkhudhātu…pe… manoviññāṇadhātu. Yā tāsaṃ dhātūnaṃ kusalatā paññā pajānanā’’ti (dha. sa. 1340) evaṃ vuttā aṭṭhārasannaṃ dhātūnaṃ sabhāvaparicchedakā savanadhāraṇasammasanapaṭivedhapaññā. Manasikārakusalatāti ‘‘yā tāsaṃ dhātūnaṃ manasikārakusalatā paññā pajānanā’’ti (dha. sa. 1341) evaṃ vuttā tāsaṃyeva dhātūnaṃ sammasanapaṭivedhapaccavekkhaṇapaññā. Dhātukusalatā Manasikārakusalatā Āyatanakusalatāti ‘‘dvādasāyatanāni cakkhāyatanaṃ…pe… dhammāyatanaṃ. Yā tesaṃ āyatanānaṃ āyatanakusalatā paññā pajānanā’’ti (dha. sa. 1342) evaṃ vuttā dvādasannaṃ āyatanānaṃ uggahamanasikārapajānanā paññā. Apica dhātukusalatāpi uggahamanasikārasavanasammasanapaṭivedhapaccavekkhaṇesu vattati manasikārakusalatāpi āyatanakusalatāpi. Ayaṃ panettha viseso, savanauggahapaccavekkhaṇā lokiyā, paṭivedho lokuttaro, sammasanamanasikārā lokiyalokuttaramissakā. Paṭiccasamuppādakusalatāti ‘‘avijjāpaccayā saṅkhārā…pe… samudayo hotīti yā tattha paññā pajānanā’’ti (dha. sa. 1343) evaṃ vuttā dvādasannaṃ paccayākārānaṃ uggahādivasena pavattā paññā. Āyatanakusalatā Paṭiccasamuppādakusalatā Ṭhānakusalatāti ‘‘ye ye dhammā yesaṃ yesaṃ dhammānaṃ hetupaccayā uppādāya taṃ taṃ ṭhānanti yā tattha paññā pajānanā’’ti (dha. sa. 1344) evaṃ vuttā ‘‘cakkhuṃ vatthuṃ katvā rūpaṃ ārammaṇaṃ katvā uppannassa cakkhuviññāṇassa cakkhurūpaṃ (dha. sa. aṭṭha. 1344) ṭhānañceva kāraṇañcā’’ti evaṃ ṭhānaparicchindanasamatthā paññā. Aṭṭhānakusalatāti ‘‘ye ye dhammā yesaṃ yesaṃ dhammānaṃ na hetū na paccayā uppādāya taṃ taṃ aṭṭhānanti yā tattha paññā pajānanā’’ti (dha. sa. 1345) evaṃ vuttā ‘‘cakkhuṃ vatthuṃ katvā rūpaṃ ārammaṇaṃ katvā sotaviññāṇādīni nuppajjanti, tasmā tesaṃ cakkhurūpaṃ na ṭhānaṃ na kāraṇa’’nti evaṃ aṭṭhānaparicchindanasamatthā paññā apica etasmiṃ duke ‘‘kittāvatā pana, bhante, ṭhānāṭhānakusalo bhikkhūti alaṃ vacanāyāti. Idhānanda, bhikkhu aṭṭhānametaṃ anavakāso, yaṃ diṭṭhisampanno puggalo kañci saṅkhāraṃ niccato upagaccheyya, netaṃ ṭhānaṃ vijjatīti pajānāti. Ṭhānañca kho etaṃ vijjati, yaṃ puthujjano kañci saṅkhāraṃ niccato upagaccheyyā’’ti (ma. ni. 3.127) imināpi suttena attho veditabbo. Ṭhānakusalatā Aṭṭhānakusalatā Ajjavanti gomuttavaṅkatā candavaṅkatā naṅgalakoṭivaṅkatāti tayo anajjavā. Ekacco hi bhikkhu paṭhamavaye ekavīsatiyā anesanāsu chasu ca agocaresu carati, majjhimapacchimavayesu lajjī kukkuccako sikkhākāmo hoti, ayaṃ gomuttavaṅkatā nāma. Eko paṭhamavayepi pacchimavayepi catupārisuddhisīlaṃ pūreti, lajjī kukkuccako sikkhākāmo hoti, majjhimavaye purimasadiso, ayaṃ candavaṅkatā nāma. Eko paṭhamavayepi majjhimavayepi catupārisuddhisīlaṃ pūreti, lajjī kukkuccako sikkhākāmo hoti, pacchimavaye purimasadiso ayaṃ naṅgalakoṭivaṅkatā nāma. Eko sabbametaṃ vaṅkataṃ pahāya tīsupi vayesu pesalo lajjī kukkuccako sikkhākāmo hoti. Tassa yo so ujubhāvo, idaṃ ajjavaṃ nāma. Abhidhammepi vuttaṃ – ‘‘tattha katamo ajjavo. Yā ajjavatā ajimhatā avaṅkatā akuṭilatā, ayaṃ vuccati ajjavo’’ti (dha. sa. 1346). Lajjavanti ‘‘tattha katamo lajjavo? Yo hirīyati hirīyitabbena hirīyati pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ samāpattiyā. Ayaṃ vuccati lajjavo’’ti evaṃ vutto lajjībhāvo. Ajjava Lajjava Khantīti ‘‘tattha katamā khanti? Yā khanti khamanatā adhivāsanatā acaṇḍikkaṃ anassuropo attamanatā cittassā’’ti (dha. sa. 1348) evaṃ vuttā adhivāsanakhanti. Soraccanti ‘‘tattha katamaṃ soraccaṃ? Yo kāyiko avītikkamo, vācasiko avītikkamo, kāyikavācasiko avītikkamo. Idaṃ vuccati soraccaṃ. Sabbopi sīlasaṃvaro soracca’’nti (dha. sa. 1349) evaṃ vutto suratabhāvo. Khantī Soracca Sākhalyanti ‘‘tattha katamaṃ sākhalyaṃ? Yā sā vācā aṇḍakā kakkasā parakaṭukā parābhisajjanī kodhasāmantā asamādhisaṃvattanikā, tathārūpiṃ vācaṃ pahāya yā sā vācā neḷā kaṇṇasukhā pemanīyā hadayaṅgamā porī bahujanakantā bahujanamanāpā, tathārūpiṃ vācaṃ bhāsitā hoti. Yā tattha saṇhavācatā sakhilavācatā apharusavācatā. Idaṃ vuccati sākhalya’’nti (dha. sa. 1350) evaṃ vutto sammodakamudukabhāvo. Paṭisanthāroti ayaṃ lokasannivāso āmisena dhammena cāti dvīhi chiddo, tassa taṃ chiddaṃ yathā na paññāyati, evaṃ pīṭhassa viya paccattharaṇena āmisena dhammena ca paṭisantharaṇaṃ. Abhidhammepi vuttaṃ ‘‘tattha katamo paṭisanthāro ? Āmisapaṭisanthāro ca dhammapaṭisanthāro ca. Idhekacco paṭisanthārako hoti āmisapaṭisanthārena vā dhammapaṭisanthārena vā. Ayaṃ vuccati paṭisanthāro’’ti (dha. sa. 1351). Ettha ca āmisena saṅgaho āmisapaṭisanthāro nāma. Taṃ karontena mātāpitūnaṃ bhikkhugatikassa veyyāvaccakarassa rañño corānañca aggaṃ aggahetvāpi dātuṃ vaṭṭati. Āmasitvā dinne hi rājāno ca corā ca anatthampi karonti jīvitakkhayampi pāpenti, anāmasitvā dinne attamanā honti. Coranāgavatthuādīni cettha vatthūni kathetabbāni. Tāni samantapāsādikāya vinayaṭṭhakathāyaṃ (pāci. aṭṭha. 185-7) vitthāritāni. Sakkaccaṃ uddesadānaṃ pāḷivaṇṇanā dhammakathākathananti evaṃ dhammena saṅgaho dhammapaṭisanthāro nāma. Sākhalya Paṭisanthāro Avihiṃsāti karuṇāpi karuṇāpubbabhāgopi. Vuttampi cetaṃ – ‘‘tattha katamā avihiṃsā? Yā sattesu karuṇā karuṇāyanā karuṇāyitattaṃ karuṇācetovimutti, ayaṃ vuccati avihiṃsā’’ti. Soceyyanti mettāya ca mettāpubbabhāgassa ca vasena sucibhāvo. Vuttampi cetaṃ – ‘‘tattha katamaṃ soceyyaṃ? Yā sattesu metti mettāyanā mettāyitattaṃ mettācetovimutti, idaṃ vuccati soceyya’’nti. Avihiṃsā Soceyya Muṭṭhassaccanti sativippavāso, yathāha ‘‘tattha katamaṃ muṭṭhassaccaṃ? Yā asati ananussati appaṭissati assaraṇatā adhāraṇatā pilāpanatā sammussanatā, idaṃ vuccati muṭṭhassaccaṃ’’ (dha. sa. 1356). Asampajaññanti, ‘‘tattha katamaṃ asampajaññaṃ? Yaṃ aññāṇaṃ adassanaṃ avijjālaṅgī moho akusalamūla’’nti evaṃ vuttā avijjāyeva. Sati satiyeva. Sampajaññaṃ ñāṇaṃ. Muṭṭhassacca Asampajañña Sati Sampajaññaṃ Indriyesu aguttadvāratāti ‘‘tattha katamā indriyesu aguttadvāratā? Idhekacco cakkhunā rūpaṃ disvā nimittaggāhī hotī’’tiādinā (dha. sa. 1352) nayena vitthārito indriyasaṃvarabhedo. Bhojane amattaññutāti ‘‘tattha katamā bhojane amattaññutā? Idhekacco appaṭisaṅkhā ayoniso āhāraṃ āhāreti davāya madāya maṇḍanāya vibhūsanāya. Yā tattha asantuṭṭhitā amattaññutā appaṭisaṅkhā bhojane’’ti evaṃ āgato bhojane amattaññubhāvo. Anantaraduko vuttappaṭipakkhanayena veditabbo. Indriyesu aguttadvāratā Bhojane amattaññutā Paṭisaṅkhānabalanti ‘‘tattha katamaṃ paṭisaṅkhānabalaṃ? Yā paññā pajānanā’’ti evaṃ vitthāritaṃ appaṭisaṅkhāya akampanañāṇaṃ. Bhāvanābalanti bhāventassa uppannaṃ balaṃ. Atthato vīriyasambojjhaṅgasīsena satta bojjhaṅgā honti. Vuttampi cetaṃ – ‘‘tattha katamaṃ bhāvanābalaṃ? Yā kusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ āsevanā bhāvanā bahulīkammaṃ, idaṃ vuccati bhāvanābalaṃ. Sattabojjhaṅgā bhāvanābala’’nti. Paṭisaṅkhānabala Bhāvanābala Satibalanti assatiyā akampanavasena satiyeva. Samādhibalanti uddhacce akampanavasena samādhiyeva. Samatho samādhi. Vipassanā paññā. Samathova taṃ ākāraṃ gahetvā puna pavattetabbassa samathassa nimittavasena samathanimittaṃ paggāhanimittepi eseva nayo. Paggāho vīriyaṃ. Avikkhepo ekaggatā. Imehi pana sati ca sampajaññañca paṭisaṅkhānabalañca bhāvanābalañca satibalañca samādhibalañca samatho ca vippassanā ca samathanimittañca paggāhanimittañca paggāho ca avikkhepo cāti chahi dukehi parato sīladiṭṭhisampadādukena ca lokiyalokuttaramissakā dhammā kathitā. Satibala Samādhibala Samatho Vipassanā samathanimittaṃ paggāhanimittepi Paggāho Avikkhepo Sīlavipattīti ‘‘tattha katamā sīlavipatti? Kāyiko vītikkamo…pe… sabbampi dussīlyaṃ sīlavipattī’’ti evaṃ vutto sīlavināsako asaṃvaro. Diṭṭhivipattīti ‘‘tattha katamā diṭṭhivipatti? Natthi dinnaṃ natthi yiṭṭha’’nti evaṃ āgatā sammādiṭṭhivināsikā micchādiṭṭhi. Sīlavipattī Diṭṭhivipattī Sīlasampadāti ‘‘tattha katamā sīlasampadā? Kāyiko avītikkamo’’ti evaṃ pubbe vuttasoraccameva sīlassa sampādanato paripūraṇato ‘‘sīlasampadā’’ti vuttaṃ. Ettha ca ‘‘sabbopi sīlasaṃvaro sīlasampadā’’ti idaṃ mānasikapariyādānatthaṃ vuttaṃ. Diṭṭhisampadāti ‘‘tattha katamā diṭṭhisampadā? Atthi dinnaṃ atthi yiṭṭhaṃ…pe… sacchikatvā pavedentīti yā evarūpā paññā pajānanā’’ti evaṃ āgataṃ diṭṭhipāripūribhūtaṃ ñāṇaṃ. Sīlasampadā Diṭṭhisampadā Sīlavisuddhīti visuddhiṃ pāpetuṃ samatthaṃ sīlaṃ. Abhidhamme panāyaṃ ‘‘tattha katamā sīlavisuddhi? Kāyiko avītikkamo vācasiko avītikkamo kāyikavācasiko avītikkamo, ayaṃ vuccati sīlavisuddhī’’ti evaṃ vibhattā. Diṭṭhivisuddhīti visuddhiṃ pāpetuṃ samatthaṃ dassanaṃ. Abhidhamme panāyaṃ ‘‘tattha katamā diṭṭhivisuddhi? Kammassakatañāṇaṃ saccānulomikañāṇaṃ maggasamaṅgissañāṇaṃ phalasamaṅgissañāṇa’’nti evaṃ vuttā. Ettha ca tividhaṃ duccaritaṃ attanā katampi parena katampi sakaṃ nāma na hoti atthabhañjanato. Sucaritaṃ sakaṃ nāma atthajananatoti evaṃ jānanaṃ kammassakatañāṇaṃ nāma. Tasmiṃ ṭhatvā bahuṃ vaṭṭagāmikammaṃ āyūhitvā sukhato sukheneva arahattaṃ pattā gaṇanapathaṃ vītivattā. Vipassanāñāṇaṃ pana vacīsaccañca anulometi, paramatthasaccañca na vilometīti saccānulomikaṃ ñāṇanti vuttaṃ. Sīlavisuddhī Diṭṭhivisuddhī Vipassanāñāṇaṃ ‘‘Diṭṭhivisuddhi kho pana yathādiṭṭhissa ca padhāna’’nti ettha diṭṭhivisuddhīti ñāṇadassanaṃ kathitaṃ. Yathādiṭṭhissa ca padhānanti taṃsampayuttameva vīriyaṃ. Api ca purimapadena catumaggañāṇaṃ. Pacchimapadena taṃsampayuttaṃ vīriyaṃ. Abhidhamme pana ‘‘diṭṭhivisuddhi kho panāti yā paññā pajānanā amoho dhammavicayo sammādiṭṭhi. Yathādiṭṭhissa ca padhānanti yo cetasiko vīriyārambho sammāvāyāmo’’ti evaṃ ayaṃ duko vibhatto. diṭṭhivisuddhī Yathādiṭṭhissa ca padhāna Abhidhamme ‘‘Saṃvego ca saṃvejanīyesu ṭhānesū’’ti ettha ‘‘saṃvegoti jātibhayaṃ jarābhayaṃ byādhibhayaṃ maraṇabhaya’’nti evaṃ jātiādīni bhayato dassanañāṇaṃ. Saṃvejanīyaṃ ṭhānanti jātijarābyādhimaraṇaṃ. Etāni hi cattāri jāti dukkhā, jarā dukkhā, byādhi dukkho, maraṇaṃ dukkhanti evaṃ saṃveguppattikāraṇattā saṃvejanīyaṃ ṭhānanti vuttāni. Saṃviggassa ca yoniso padhānanti evaṃ saṃvegajātassa upāyapadhānaṃ. ‘‘Idha bhikkhu anuppannānaṃ pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ anuppādāya chandaṃ janetī’’ti evaṃ āgatavīriyassetaṃ adhivacanaṃ. ‘‘saṃvego saṃvejanīyaṃ ṭhāna Saṃviggassa ca yoniso padhāna Asantuṭṭhitā ca kusalesu dhammesūti yā kusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ bhāvanāya asantuṭṭhassa bhiyyokamyatā, tāya hi samaṅgībhūto puggalo sīlaṃ pūretvā jhānaṃ uppādeti. Jhānaṃ labhitvā vipassanaṃ ārabhati. Āraddhavipassako arahattaṃ agahetvā antarā vosānaṃ nāpajjati. Appaṭivānitā ca padhānasminti ‘‘kusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ bhāvanāya sakkaccakiriyatā sātaccakiriyatā aṭṭhitakiriyatā anolīnavuttitā anikkhittachandatā anikkhittadhuratā āsevanā bhāvanā bahulīkamma’’nti evaṃ vuttā rattindivaṃ cha koṭṭhāse katvā jāgariyānuyogavasena āraddhe padhānasmiṃ arahattaṃ apatvā anivattanatā. Asantuṭṭhitā ca kusalesu dhammesū Appaṭivānitā ca padhānasmi Vijjāti tisso vijjā. Vimuttīti dve vimuttiyo, cittassa ca adhimutti, nibbānañca. Ettha ca aṭṭha samāpattiyo nīvaraṇādīhi suṭṭhu muttattā adhimutti nāma. Nibbānaṃ sabbasaṅkhatato muttattā vimuttīti veditabbaṃ. Vijjā Vimuttī Khaye ñāṇanti kilesakkhayakare ariyamagge ñāṇaṃ. Anuppāde ñāṇanti paṭisandhivasena anuppādabhūte taṃtaṃmaggavajjhakilesānaṃ vā anuppādapariyosāne uppanne ariyaphale ñāṇaṃ. Tenevāha ‘‘khaye ñāṇanti maggasamaṅgissa ñāṇaṃ. Anuppāde ñāṇanti phalasamaṅgissa ñāṇa’’nti. Ime kho, āvusotiādi ekake vuttanayeneva yojetabbaṃ. Iti pañcatiṃsāya dukānaṃ vasena thero sāmaggirasaṃ dassesīti. Khaye ñāṇa Anuppāde ñāṇa Ime kho, āvuso Dukavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā. Tikavaṇṇanā
304. Having shown the samaggirasaṃ by way of oneness, now, in order to show [it] by way of duality, [the text] again began a teaching.

There, in the nāmarūpaduke, name means the four immaterial aggregates and nirvāṇa.

There, the four aggregates are name because of the meaning of bending toward.

Nāmanaṭṭhena: because of the meaning of naming.

Just as, because of being agreed upon by many people, the name of Mahāsammata was [given]. Just as mothers and fathers do the made-up-name-making of a son, [saying,] "May this one be named Tissa, may [this one] be named Phussa," so too, when one says, "A dharmakathika, a vinayadhara," a name comes from a quality, but it is not like that for feeling and so on.

Feelings and so on, like the great earth element and so on, arise only doing their own naming.

When they have arisen, their name is just what has arisen.

For, when a feeling has arisen, no one says, "May you, feeling, be named," nor does it have a name-taking function by any cause, just as when the earth element has arisen, there is no name-taking function [saying,] "May you, earth element, be named." When the cakravāḷas, Sineru, the moon, the sun, and the stars have arisen, there is no name-taking function [saying,] "May you be named cakravāḷa, may you be named star." Name is just what has arisen; the aupapātikā designation falls [on it]. Thus, when feeling has arisen, there is no name-taking function [saying,] "May you, feeling, be named." Because of that arisal, [it is] feeling. Name is just what has arisen.

This same naya [applies] also to perception and so on. In the past, too, feeling was just feeling.

Perception.

Volitional processes.

Consciousness was just consciousness.

Also in the future.

Also in the present.

But nirvāṇa is always just nirvāṇa.

Nāmanaṭṭhena nāmaṃ: name through the meaning of bending.

Namanatthenāpi: also here, the four aggregates are name because of the meaning of bending.

For they bend toward the object.

Nāmanaṭṭhena sabbampi nāmaṃ: all name is through the meaning of bending.

For the four aggregates bend each other in the object, nirvāṇa bends in itself [those] undefiled dharmas through the condition of object-domination.

Rūpa means the four great elements and the rūpa that takes up the four great elements; all of that is rūpa by way of the meaning of "ruppana."

The talk of its expansion should be understood in just the way it was said in the Visuddhimagga.

Rūpa rūpa.

Avijjā means non-knowing regarding suffering and so on.

This, too, is just what was spoken in the Visuddhimagga in detail.

Bhavataṇhā means desire for becoming.

As it was said: "Therein, what is desire for becoming? That which is desire for becoming in becomings" and so on (Dha.

Sa.
1319).

Avijjā bhavataṇhā

Bhavadiṭṭhī means becoming is called eternal, [it is] the view that arises by way of the eternal.

That, "Therein, what is the view of becoming? 'Self and world will be,' this view, a view-gone-[wrong]" and so on, was expanded in the Abhidhamma by way of the naya (Dha.

Sa.
1320).

Vibhavadiṭṭhī means non-becoming is called annihilation; [it is] the view that arises by way of annihilation.

That, too, "Therein, what is the view of non-becoming? 'Self and world will not be'" (Dha.
Sa.
285).
That such a view, a fixed view, etc., has been extensively explained there itself in the way of (Dha.
Sa.
1321).

bhava-diṭṭhi vibhava-diṭṭhi

ahirika means, as it is expanded "Not being ashamed of what should be ashamed of" (Dha.
Sa.
1328), thus shamelessness is explained.

anottappa means, as it is expanded "Not being afraid of what should be afraid of" (Dha.
Sa.
1329), thus the state of being fearless is explained.

ahirika

anottappa

hirī ca ottappañca means, as it is expanded "Being ashamed of what should be ashamed of, being afraid of what should be afraid of" (Dha.
Sa.
1330-31), thus shame and fear are explained.
Moreover, hirī arises internally, ottappa arises externally.
Hirī has self as its authority; ottappa has the world as its authority.
Hirī is established in the nature of modesty, ottappa is established in the nature of fear.
The detailed explanation of this is given in all respects in the Path of Purification (Visuddhimagga).

hirī ca ottappañca hirī, ottappaṃ.

dovacassatā means, a person who is difficult to instruct is dubbaco, because he speaks painfully, is averse to what is contrary, is indifferent to what is on the side of opposition; the action of that person is dovacassaṃ, the state of that is dovacassatā.
In detail, this has come in the Abhidhamma as, "What is dovacassatā there? In dovacassa, when one is being told what is in accordance with the Dharma" (Dha.
Sa.
1332).
In meaning, that is the saṅkhārakkhandha.
They say, "This designation applies to the occurrence of the four khandhas in this way."

pāpamittatā means, a wicked person, faithless, etc., is a friend of this person, thus a bad friend (pāpamitto), the state of that is pāpamittatā.
In detail, this has come as – "What is pāpamittatā there? Those people who are faithless, immoral, unlearned, miserly, unwise.
Association with them, constant association, close association, intimacy, close intimacy, devotion, close devotion, inclination towards that." (Dha.
Sa.
1333)
That too should be regarded as being like dovacassatā in meaning.

dovacassatā.

pāpamittatā

sovacassatā ca kalyāṇamittatā ca should be understood by way of the opposite of what has been said.
However, both of these are said here as mixed with the mundane and supramundane.

sovacassatā ca kalyāṇamittatā

(1344) ṭhānañceva kāraṇañcā’ti: Thus, the wisdom that is capable of distinguishing causes is ṭhāna (basis, reason) and kāraṇa (cause).

aṭṭhānakusalatāti: "Those things that are not the cause or condition for the arising of those things are called aṭṭhāna; the wisdom and understanding there," (Dha.
Sa.
1345) thus it is said, "Visual consciousness and so forth do not arise by making the eye the base and form the object, therefore, the eye and form are not the basis or cause for them." Thus, the wisdom that is capable of distinguishing non-causes. Moreover, in this pair, "To what extent, venerable sir, is a monk skilled in causes and non-causes?"

"Here, Ānanda, a monk understands: 'It is impossible, it cannot happen that a person possessing right view could treat any formation as permanent - there is no such possibility.' And he understands: 'It is possible that an ordinary person might treat some formation as permanent'," (Ma.
Ni.
