307. ‘‘Catasso samādhibhāvanā. Atthāvuso, samādhibhāvanā bhāvitā bahulīkatā diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārāya saṃvattati. Atthāvuso, samādhibhāvanā bhāvitā bahulīkatā ñāṇadassanapaṭilābhāya saṃvattati. Atthāvuso samādhibhāvanā bhāvitā bahulīkatā satisampajaññāya saṃvattati. Atthāvuso samādhibhāvanā bhāvitā bahulīkatā āsavānaṃ khayāya saṃvattati. ‘‘Katamā cāvuso, samādhibhāvanā bhāvitā bahulīkatā diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārāya saṃvattati? Idhāvuso, bhikkhu vivicceva kāmehi vivicca akusalehi dhammehi savitakkaṃ…pe… catutthaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ, āvuso , samādhibhāvanā bhāvitā bahulīkatā diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārāya saṃvattati. ‘‘Katamā cāvuso, samādhibhāvanā bhāvitā bahulīkatā ñāṇadassanapaṭilābhāya saṃvattati? Idhāvuso, bhikkhu ālokasaññaṃ manasi karoti, divāsaññaṃ adhiṭṭhāti yathā divā tathā rattiṃ, yathā rattiṃ tathā divā. Iti vivaṭena cetasā apariyonaddhena sappabhāsaṃ cittaṃ bhāveti. Ayaṃ, āvuso samādhibhāvanā bhāvitā bahulīkatā ñāṇadassanapaṭilābhāya saṃvattati. ‘‘Katamā cāvuso, samādhibhāvanā bhāvitā bahulīkatā satisampajaññāya saṃvattati? Idhāvuso, bhikkhuno viditā vedanā uppajjanti, viditā upaṭṭhahanti, viditā abbhatthaṃ gacchanti. Viditā saññā uppajjanti, viditā upaṭṭhahanti, viditā abbhatthaṃ gacchanti. Viditā vitakkā uppajjanti, viditā upaṭṭhahanti, viditā abbhatthaṃ gacchanti. Ayaṃ, āvuso, samādhibhāvanā bhāvitā bahulīkatā satisampajaññāya saṃvattati. ‘‘Katamā cāvuso, samādhibhāvanā bhāvitā bahulīkatā āsavānaṃ khayāya saṃvattati? Idhāvuso, bhikkhu pañcasu upādānakkhandhesu udayabbayānupassī viharati. Iti rūpaṃ, iti rūpassa samudayo, iti rūpassa atthaṅgamo. Iti vedanā…pe… iti saññā… iti saṅkhārā… iti viññāṇaṃ, iti viññāṇassa samudayo, iti viññāṇassa atthaṅgamo. Ayaṃ, āvuso, samādhibhāvanā bhāvitā bahulīkatā āsavānaṃ khayāya saṃvattati.
307. Four developments of concentration ( samādhi-bhāvanā):
There is, friend (āvuso), the development of concentration that, when developed and pursued, leads to a pleasant abiding in the here and now (diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārāya saṃvattati).
There is, friend, the development of concentration that, when developed and pursued, leads to the attainment of knowledge and vision (ñāṇadassanapaṭilābhāya saṃvattati).
There is, friend, the development of concentration that, when developed and pursued, leads to mindfulness and alertness (satisampajaññāya saṃvattati).
There is, friend, the development of concentration that, when developed and pursued, leads to the ending of the effluents (āsavānaṃ khayāya saṃvattati).

And which is the development of concentration that, when developed and pursued, leads to a pleasant abiding in the here and now? Here, friend, a bhikkhu, quite secluded from sensual pleasures, secluded from unwholesome states of mind, enters and abides in the fourth jhāna, which is with applied and sustained thought…
This, friend, is the development of concentration that, when developed and pursued, leads to a pleasant abiding in the here and now.

And which is the development of concentration that, when developed and pursued, leads to the attainment of knowledge and vision? Here, friend, a bhikkhu attends to the perception of light (ālokasaññaṃ manasi karoti), establishes the perception of day (divāsaññaṃ adhiṭṭhāti), as by day, so by night; as by night, so by day.
Thus with an open mind (vivaṭena cetasā), unhampered (apariyonaddhena), he develops a brightened mind (sappabhāsaṃ cittaṃ bhāveti).
This, friend, is the development of concentration that, when developed and pursued, leads to the attainment of knowledge and vision.

And which is the development of concentration that, when developed and pursued, leads to mindfulness and alertness? Here, friend, for a bhikkhu feelings are known as they arise (viditā vedanā uppajjanti), known as they are present (viditā upaṭṭhahanti), known as they disappear (viditā abbhatthaṃ gacchanti).
Perceptions are known as they arise, known as they are present, known as they disappear.
Thoughts are known as they arise, known as they are present, known as they disappear.
This, friend, is the development of concentration that, when developed and pursued, leads to mindfulness and alertness.

And which is the development of concentration that, when developed and pursued, leads to the ending of the effluents? Here, friend, a bhikkhu dwells contemplating arising and vanishing (udayabbayānupassī viharati) in the five aggregates subject to clinging (pañcasu upādānakkhandhesu):
'Such is form (rūpaṃ), such is the arising of form (rūpassa samudayo), such is the passing away of form (rūpassa atthaṅgamo).'
Such is feeling... such is perception... such are fabrications... such is consciousness, such is the arising of consciousness, such is the passing away of consciousness.
This, friend, is the development of concentration that, when developed and pursued, leads to the ending of the effluents.
307.Diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārāyāti imasmiṃyeva attabhāve sukhavihāratthāya. Idha phalasamāpattijhānāni, khīṇāsavassa aparabhāge nibbattitajhānāni ca kathitāni. Ālokasaññaṃ manasikarotīti divā vā rattiṃ vā sūriyacandapajjotamaṇiādīnaṃ ālokaṃ ālokoti manasikaroti. Divāsaññaṃ adhiṭṭhātīti evaṃ manasi katvā divātisaññaṃ ṭhapeti. Yathā divā tathā rattinti yathā divā diṭṭho āloko, tatheva taṃ rattiṃ manasikaroti. Yathā rattiṃ tathā divāti yathā rattiṃ āloko diṭṭho, evameva divā manasikaroti. Iti vivaṭena cetasāti evaṃ apihitena cittena. Apariyonaddhenāti samantato anaddhena. Sappabhāsanti saobhāsaṃ. Ñāṇadassanapaṭilābhāyāti ñāṇadassanapaṭilābhatthāya. Iminā kiṃ kathitaṃ? Middhavinodanaāloko kathito parikammaāloko vā. Iminā kiṃ kathitaṃ hoti? Khīṇāsavassa dibbacakkhuñāṇaṃ. Tasmiṃ vā āgatepi anāgatepi pādakajjhānasamāpattimeva sandhāya ‘‘sappabhāsaṃ cittaṃ bhāvetī’’ti vuttaṃ. Ālokasaññaṃ manasikarotī Divāsaññaṃ adhiṭṭhātī Yathā divā tathā ratti Yathā rattiṃ tathā divā Iti vivaṭena cetasā Apariyonaddhenā Sappabhāsa Ñāṇadassanapaṭilābhāyā Satisampajaññāyāti sattaṭṭhānikassa satisampajaññassa atthāya. Viditā vedanā uppajjantītiādīsu khīṇāsavassa vatthu viditaṃ hoti ārammaṇaṃ viditaṃ vatthārammaṇaṃ viditaṃ. Vatthārammaṇaviditatāya evaṃ vedanā uppajjanti, evaṃ tiṭṭhanti, evaṃ nirujjhanti. Na kevalañca vedanā eva idha vuttā saññādayopi , avuttā cetanādayopi, viditā ca uppajjanti ceva tiṭṭhanti ca nirujjhanti ca. Api ca vedanāya uppādo vidito hoti, upaṭṭhānaṃ viditaṃ hoti. Avijjāsamudayā vedanāsamudayo, taṇhāsamudayā kammasamudayo, phassasamudayā vedanāyasamudayo. Nibbattilakkhaṇaṃ passantopi vedanākkhandhassa samudayaṃ passati. Evaṃ vedanāya uppādo vidito hoti. Kathaṃ vedanāya upaṭṭhānaṃ viditaṃ hoti? Aniccato manasikaroto khayatūpaṭṭhānaṃ viditaṃ hoti. Dukkhato manasikaroto bhayatūpaṭṭhānaṃ viditaṃ hoti. Anattato manasikaroto suññatūpaṭṭhānaṃ viditaṃ hoti. Evaṃ vedanāya upaṭṭhānaṃ viditaṃ hoti, khayato bhayato suññato jānāti. Kathaṃ vedanāya atthaṅgamo vidito hoti? Avijjānirodhā vedanānirodho.…Pe… evaṃ vedanāya atthaṅgamo vidito hoti. Imināpi nayenettha attho veditabbo. Satisampajaññāyā Viditā vedanā uppajjantī Itirūpantiādi vuttanayameva. Ayaṃ āvuso samādhibhāvanāti ayaṃ āsavānaṃ khayañāṇassa pādakajjhānasamādhibhāvanā. Iti rūpa Ayaṃ āvuso samādhibhāvanā
307. " Diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārāyā ti" means for the purpose of dwelling in pleasure in this very existence.

Herein, the fruitional attainments (phalasamāpattijhānāni) and the meditative absorptions (nibbattitajhānāni) in the latter part of the life of one whose defilements are destroyed (khīṇāsavassa) are spoken of.

" Ālokasaññaṃ manasikarotī ti" means he attends to the perception of light, thinking, "Light, light," whether by day or by night, in regard to sunlight, moonlight, the light of a lamp, jewels, and so on.
" Divāsaññaṃ adhiṭṭhātī ti" means having made it in his mind in this way, he establishes the perception of "day."
" Yathā divā tathā ratti nti" means just as light is seen during the day, just so he attends to it at night.
" Yathā rattiṃ tathā divā ti" means just as light is seen at night, exactly so he attends to it during the day.
" Iti vivaṭena cetasā ti" means thus, with an open mind.
" Apariyonaddhenā ti" means without being entirely bound.
" Sappabhāsa nti" means with radiance (saobhāsaṃ).
" Ñāṇadassanapaṭilābhāyā ti" means for the purpose of obtaining the gain of knowledge and vision (ñāṇadassanapaṭilābhatthāya).

What is stated by this? The light that dispels drowsiness, or the light of preliminary work, is stated.
What is stated by this? The divine eye knowledge (dibbacakkhuñāṇaṃ) of one whose defilements are destroyed (khīṇāsavassa).

Having reference to just the attainment of the absorption of the foundational level of absorption, whether that has come or has not yet come, it is said, "He cultivates a mind with radiance (sappabhāsaṃ cittaṃ bhāvetī)."

Ālokasaññaṃ manasikarotī divāsaññaṃ adhiṭṭhātī yathā divā tathā ratti yathā rattiṃ tathā divā iti vivaṭena cetasā apariyonaddhenā sappabhāsa ñāṇadassanapaṭilābhāyā satisampajaññāyāti" means for the purpose of mindfulness and full awareness (sattaṭṭhānikassa satisampajaññassa atthāya).

" Viditā vedanā uppajjantī tiādīsu" means in these and other instances, the object (vatthu) of one whose defilements are destroyed (khīṇāsavassa) is known, the object of attention (ārammaṇaṃ) is known, the object-of-attention-as-object (vatthārammaṇaṃ) is known.
Through knowing the object-of-attention-as-object, thus feelings arise, thus they remain, thus they cease.

It is not only feeling (vedanā) that is spoken of here, but also perception (saññā), and so on, and volition (cetanā), and so on, even though not spoken of, are known, and arise, and remain, and cease.

Moreover, the arising (uppādo) of feeling is known, its appearance (upaṭṭhānaṃ) is known.
From the arising of ignorance (avijjāsamudayā) [comes] the arising of feeling (vedanāsamudayo); from the arising of craving (taṇhāsamudayā) [comes] the arising of action (kammasamudayo); from the arising of contact (phassasamudayā) [comes] the arising of feeling (vedanāyasamudayo).

Even while seeing the characteristic of production (nibbattilakkhaṇaṃ passantopi), he sees the arising (samudayaṃ passati) of the aggregate of feeling (vedanākkhandhassa).
Thus, the arising (uppādo) of feeling is known.

How is the appearance (upaṭṭhānaṃ) of feeling known? When attention is given to it as impermanent (aniccato manasikaroto), the appearance as exhaustion (khayatūpaṭṭhānaṃ) is known.
When attention is given to it as painful (dukkhato manasikaroto), the appearance as terror (bhayatūpaṭṭhānaṃ) is known.
When attention is given to it as not-self (anattato manasikaroto), the appearance as voidness (suññatūpaṭṭhānaṃ) is known.
Thus, the appearance (upaṭṭhānaṃ) of feeling is known; he knows exhaustion, terror, voidness.

How is the subsiding (atthaṅgamo) of feeling known? From the cessation of ignorance (avijjānirodhā) [comes] the cessation of feeling (vedanānirodho).
…pe… Thus, the subsiding (atthaṅgamo) of feeling is known.

In this way also, the meaning here should be understood.

Satisampajaññāyā viditā vedanā uppajjantī iti rūpantiādi" [is] just the manner that has been spoken of.
" Ayaṃ āvuso samādhibhāvanāti" means this, friend, is the cultivation of absorption that is the basis for the knowledge of the destruction of defilements (āsavānaṃ khayañāṇassa pādakajjhānasamādhibhāvanā).

Iti rūpa ayaṃ āvuso samādhibhāvanā
308
308. ‘‘Catasso appamaññā. Idhāvuso, bhikkhu mettāsahagatena cetasā ekaṃ disaṃ pharitvā viharati. Tathā dutiyaṃ. Tathā tatiyaṃ. Tathā catutthaṃ. Iti uddhamadho tiriyaṃ sabbadhi sabbattatāya sabbāvantaṃ lokaṃ mettāsahagatena cetasā vipulena mahaggatena appamāṇena averena abyāpajjena abyāpajjhena (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) pharitvā viharati. Karuṇāsahagatena cetasā…pe… muditāsahagatena cetasā…pe… upekkhāsahagatena cetasā ekaṃ disaṃ pharitvā viharati. Tathā dutiyaṃ. Tathā tatiyaṃ. Tathā catutthaṃ. Iti uddhamadho tiriyaṃ sabbadhi sabbattatāya sabbāvantaṃ lokaṃ upekkhāsahagatena cetasā vipulena mahaggatena appamāṇena averena abyāpajjena pharitvā viharati. ‘‘Cattāro āruppā.arūpā (syā. kaṃ. pī.) Idhāvuso, bhikkhu sabbaso rūpasaññānaṃ samatikkamā paṭighasaññānaṃ atthaṅgamā nānattasaññānaṃ amanasikārā ‘ananto ākāso’ti ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Sabbaso ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘anantaṃ viññāṇa’nti viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Sabbaso viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘natthi kiñcī’ti ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Sabbaso ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. āruppā. arūpā (syā. kaṃ. pī.) ‘‘Cattāri apassenāni. Idhāvuso, bhikkhu saṅkhāyekaṃ paṭisevati, saṅkhāyekaṃ adhivāseti, saṅkhāyekaṃ parivajjeti, saṅkhāyekaṃ vinodeti. apassenāni.
308. "Four immeasurables: Here, friends, a monk abides pervading one direction with a mind imbued with loving-kindness, likewise the second direction, likewise the third direction, likewise the fourth direction; thus above, below, and across, everywhere, in its entirety, he keeps pervading the all-encompassing world with an awareness imbued with goodwill—abundant, enlarged, immeasurable, without hostility, without ill will. And he dwells, letting his mind, filled with com­passion,… with sympathetic joy,… with equanimity, per­vade one quarter, likewise the second direction, likewise the third direction, likewise the fourth direction; Thus above, below, & all around, everywhere, in its entirety, he keeps pervading the all-encompassing cosmos with an awareness imbued with equanimity—abundant, enlarged, immeasurable, without hostility, without ill will.

“Four formlessnesses: There is the case, friends, where a monk—with the complete transcending of perceptions of form ( rūpasaṃjñā ), with the disappearance of perceptions of resistance, and not attending to perceptions of multiplicity, ‘Infinite space’ ( ananto ākāso )—enters & remains in the dimension of the infinitude of space (ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ). With the complete transcending of the dimension of the infinitude of space, ‘Infinite consciousness’ (anantaṃ viññāṇa)—enters & remains in the dimension of the infinitude of consciousness (viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ). With the complete transcending of the dimension of the infinitude of consciousness, ‘There is nothing’ (natthi kiñcī)—he enters & remains in the dimension of nothingness (ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ). By utterly transcending the sphere of nothingness, one attains to and abides in the sphere of neither-perception-nor-non-perception (nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ).

Four supports: There is the case, friends, where a monk, contemplating one thing, associates with it; contemplating one thing, acquiesces to it; contemplating one thing, avoids it; contemplating one thing, subdues it.
308.Appamaññāti pamāṇaṃ agahetvā anavasesapharaṇavasena appamaññāva. Anupadavaṇṇanā pana bhāvanāsamādhividhānañca etāsaṃ visuddhimagge vitthāritameva. Arūpakathāpi visuddhimagge vitthāritāva. Apassenānīti apassayāni. Saṅkhāyāti ñāṇena ñatvā. Paṭisevatīti ñāṇena ñatvā sevitabbayuttakameva sevati. Tassa ca vitthāro ‘‘paṭisaṅkhā yoniso cīvaraṃ paribhuñjatī’’tiādinā nayena veditabbo. Saṅkhāyekaṃ adhivāsetīti ñāṇena ñatvā adhivāsetabbayuttakameva adhivāseti. Vitthāro panettha ‘‘paṭisaṅkhā yoniso khamo hoti sītassā’’tiādinā nayena veditabbo. Parivajjetīti ñāṇena ñatvā parivajjetuṃ yuttameva parivajjeti. Tassa vitthāro ‘‘paṭisaṅkhā yoniso caṇḍaṃ hatthiṃ parivajjetī’’tiādinā nayena veditabbo. Vinodetīti ñāṇena ñatvā vinodetabbameva vinodeti, nudati nīharati anto pavisituṃ na deti. Tassa vitthāro ‘‘uppannaṃ kāmavitakkaṃ nādhivāsetī’’tiādinā nayena veditabbo. Apassenānī Saṅkhāyā Paṭisevatī Saṅkhāyekaṃ adhivāsetī Parivajjetī Vinodetī Ariyavaṃsacatukkavaṇṇanā
308. Appamaññā means appamaññāva by not grasping a measure (pamāṇaṃ), but by way of pervasion without remainder (anavasesapharaṇavasena).

Moreover, the commentary on the subsequent verses, the method of cultivation (bhāvanā), and concentration (samādhi) have already been extensively explained in the Visuddhimagga.

The discussion of the formless realms (arūpakathāpi) has also been extensively explained in the Visuddhimagga.

Apassenānī means supports (apassayāni).

Saṅkhāyā means having known with knowledge (ñāṇena ñatvā).

Paṭisevatī means having known with knowledge (ñāṇena ñatvā), one cultivates precisely what is fit to be cultivated (sevitabbayuttakameva sevati).

And its exposition should be understood by the method beginning with "reflecting wisely, he uses the robe" (paṭisaṅkhā yoniso cīvaraṃ paribhuñjatītyādina nayena veditabbo).

Saṅkhāyekaṃ adhivāsetī means having known with knowledge (ñāṇena ñatvā), one endures precisely what is fit to be endured (adhivāsetabbayuttakameva adhivāseti).

The exposition here should be understood by the method beginning with "reflecting wisely, he is forbearing of cold" (paṭisaṅkhā yoniso khamo hoti sītassā’’tiādinā nayena veditabbo).

Parivajjetī means having known with knowledge (ñāṇena ñatvā), one avoids precisely what is fit to be avoided (parivajjetuṃ yuttameva parivajjeti).

Its exposition should be understood by the method beginning with "reflecting wisely, he avoids a fierce elephant" (paṭisaṅkhā yoniso caṇḍaṃ hatthiṃ parivajjetī’’tiādinā nayena veditabbo).

Vinodetī means having known with knowledge (ñāṇena ñatvā), one dispels precisely what should be dispelled (vinodetabbameva vinodeti), he pushes out, he removes, he does not allow it to enter within (nudati nīharati anto pavisituṃ na deti).

Its exposition should be understood by the method beginning with "he does not tolerate a arisen thought of desire" (uppannaṃ kāmavitakkaṃ nādhivāsetī’’tiādinā nayena veditabbo).

Apassenānī, Saṅkhāyā, Paṭisevatī, Saṅkhāyekaṃ adhivāsetī, Parivajjetī, Vinodetī

Commentary on the fourfold lineage of the Noble Ones (ariyavaṃsacatukkavaṇṇanā).
309
309. ‘‘Cattāro ariyavaṃsā. Idhāvuso, bhikkhu santuṭṭho hoti itarītarena cīvarena, itarītaracīvarasantuṭṭhiyā ca vaṇṇavādī, na ca cīvarahetu anesanaṃ appatirūpaṃ āpajjati; aladdhā ca cīvaraṃ na paritassati, laddhā ca cīvaraṃ agadhito agathito (sī. pī.) amucchito anajjhāpanno ādīnavadassāvī nissaraṇapañño paribhuñjati; tāya ca pana itarītaracīvarasantuṭṭhiyā nevattānukkaṃseti na paraṃ vambheti. Yo hi tattha dakkho analaso sampajāno paṭissato, ayaṃ vuccatāvuso – ‘bhikkhu porāṇe aggaññe ariyavaṃse ṭhito’. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu santuṭṭho hoti itarītarena piṇḍapātena, itarītarapiṇḍapātasantuṭṭhiyā ca vaṇṇavādī, na ca piṇḍapātahetu anesanaṃ appatirūpaṃ āpajjati; aladdhā ca piṇḍapātaṃ na paritassati, laddhā ca piṇḍapātaṃ agadhito amucchito anajjhāpanno ādīnavadassāvī nissaraṇapañño paribhuñjati; tāya ca pana itarītarapiṇḍapātasantuṭṭhiyā nevattānukkaṃseti na paraṃ vambheti. Yo hi tattha dakkho analaso sampajāno paṭissato , ayaṃ vuccatāvuso – ‘bhikkhu porāṇe aggaññe ariyavaṃse ṭhito’. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu santuṭṭho hoti itarītarena senāsanena, itarītarasenāsanasantuṭṭhiyā ca vaṇṇavādī, na ca senāsanahetu anesanaṃ appatirūpaṃ āpajjati; aladdhā ca senāsanaṃ na paritassati, laddhā ca senāsanaṃ agadhito amucchito anajjhāpanno ādīnavadassāvī nissaraṇapañño paribhuñjati; tāya ca pana itarītarasenāsanasantuṭṭhiyā nevattānukkaṃseti na paraṃ vambheti. Yo hi tattha dakkho analaso sampajāno paṭissato, ayaṃ vuccatāvuso – ‘bhikkhu porāṇe aggaññe ariyavaṃse ṭhito’. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu pahānārāmo hoti pahānarato, bhāvanārāmo hoti bhāvanārato; tāya ca pana pahānārāmatāya pahānaratiyā bhāvanārāmatāya bhāvanāratiyā nevattānukkaṃseti na paraṃ vambheti. Yo hi tattha dakkho analaso sampajāno paṭissato ayaṃ vuccatāvuso – ‘bhikkhu porāṇe aggaññe ariyavaṃse ṭhito’.
309. “The four lineages of the Noble Ones (ariyavaṃsā):

Here, friends, a monk is content with any old robe cloth at all. He speaks in praise of being content with any old robe cloth at all. He doesn’t, for the sake of robe cloth, do anything unseemly or inappropriate. Not getting cloth, he isn’t agitated. Getting cloth, he uses it unattached to it, uninfatuated, guiltless, seeing the drawbacks, and discerning the escape from them. He doesn’t, on account of his contentment with any old robe cloth at all, exalt himself or disparage others. Any monk who is diligent, deft, alert, & mindful in this is said to be a monk standing firm in the ancient, original traditions of the noble ones.

“And further, friends, the monk is content with any old almsfood at all. He speaks in praise of being content with any old almsfood at all. He doesn’t, for the sake of almsfood, do anything unseemly or inappropriate. Not getting almsfood, he isn’t agitated. Getting almsfood, he uses it unattached to it, uninfatuated, guiltless, seeing the drawbacks, and discerning the escape from them. He doesn’t, on account of his contentment with any old almsfood at all, exalt himself or disparage others. Any monk who is diligent, deft, alert, & mindful in this is said to be a monk standing firm in the ancient, original traditions of the noble ones.

“And further, friends, the monk is content with any old lodging at all. He speaks in praise of being content with any old lodging at all. He doesn’t, for the sake of lodging, do anything unseemly or inappropriate. Not getting lodging, he isn’t agitated. Getting lodging, he uses it unattached to it, uninfatuated, guiltless, seeing the drawbacks, and discerning the escape from them. He doesn’t, on account of his contentment with any old lodging at all, exalt himself or disparage others. Any monk who is diligent, deft, alert, & mindful in this is said to be a monk standing firm in the ancient, original traditions of the noble ones.

“And further, friends, the monk finds pleasure & delight in abandoning, finds pleasure & delight in developing. He doesn’t, on account of his pleasure & delight in abandoning & developing, exalt himself or disparage others. Any monk who is diligent, deft, alert, & mindful in this is said to be a monk standing firm in the ancient, original traditions of the noble ones.
309.Ariyavaṃsāti ariyānaṃ vaṃsā. Yathā hi khattiyavaṃso, brāhmaṇavaṃso, vessavaṃso, suddavaṃso, samaṇavaṃso, kulavaṃso, rājavaṃso, evaṃ ayampi aṭṭhamo ariyavaṃso ariyatanti ariyapaveṇī nāma hoti. So kho panāyaṃ ariyavaṃso imesaṃ vaṃsānaṃ mūlagandhādīnaṃ kāḷānusāritagandhādayo viya aggamakkhāyati. Ke pana te ariyā yesaṃ ete vaṃsāti? Ariyā vuccanti buddhā ca paccekabuddhā ca tathāgatasāvakā ca, etesaṃ ariyānaṃ vaṃsāti ariyavaṃsā. Ito pubbe hi satasahassakappādhikānaṃ catunnaṃ asaṅkhyeyyānaṃ matthake taṇhaṅkaro medhaṅkaro saraṇaṅkaro dīpaṅkaroti cattāro buddhā uppannā, te ariyā, tesaṃ ariyānaṃ vaṃsāti ariyavaṃsā. Tesaṃ buddhānaṃ parinibbānato aparabhāge asaṅkhyeyyaṃ atikkamitvā koṇḍañño nāma buddho uppanno…pe… imasmiṃ kappe kakusandho, koṇāgamano, kassapo, amhākaṃ bhagavā gotamoti cattāro buddhā uppannā. Tesaṃ ariyānaṃ vaṃsāti ariyavaṃsā. Apica atītānāgatapaccuppannānaṃ sabbabuddhapaccekabuddhabuddhasāvakānaṃ ariyānaṃ vaṃsāti ariyavaṃsā. Te kho panete aggaññā aggāti jānitabbā. Rattaññā dīgharattaṃ pavattāti jānitabbā. Vaṃsaññā vaṃsāti jānitabbā. Porāṇāti na adhunuppattikā. Asaṃkiṇṇā avikiṇṇā anapanītā. Asaṃkiṇṇapubbā atītabuddhehi na saṃkiṇṇapubbā. ‘‘Kiṃ imehī’’ti na apanītapubbā? Na saṅkīyantīti idānipi na apanīyanti. Na saṅkīyissantīti anāgatabuddhehipi na apanīyissanti, ye loke viññū samaṇabrāhmaṇā, tehi appaṭikuṭṭhā, samaṇehi brāhmaṇehi viññūhi aninditā agarahitā. Porāṇā Asaṃkiṇṇā Asaṃkiṇṇapubbā Na saṅkīyantī Na saṅkīyissantī Santuṭṭho hotīti paccayasantosavasena santuṭṭho hoti. Itarītarena cīvarenāti thūlasukhumalūkhapaṇītathirajiṇṇānaṃ yena kenaci. Atha kho yathāladdhādīnaṃ itarītarena yena kenaci santuṭṭho hotīti attho. Cīvarasmiñhi tayo santosā – yathālābhasantoso, yathābalasantoso, yathāsāruppasantosoti. Piṇḍapātādīsupi eseva nayo. Tesaṃ vitthārakathā sāmaññaphale vuttanayeneva veditabbā. Ime tayo santose sandhāya ‘‘santuṭṭho hoti, itarītarena yathāladdhādīsu yena kenaci cīvarena santuṭṭho hotī’’ti vuttaṃ. Santuṭṭho hotī Itarītarena cīvarenā Ettha ca cīvaraṃ jānitabbaṃ, cīvarakkhettaṃ jānitabbaṃ, paṃsukūlaṃ jānitabbaṃ, cīvarasantoso jānitabbo, cīvarapaṭisaṃyuttāni dhutaṅgāni jānitabbāni. Tattha cīvaraṃ jānitabbanti khomādīni cha cīvarāni dukūlādīni cha anulomacīvarāni jānitabbāni. Imāni dvādasa kappiyacīvarāni. Kusacīraṃ vākacīraṃ phalakacīraṃ kesakambalaṃ vāḷakambalaṃ potthako cammaṃ ulūkapakkhaṃ rukkhadussaṃ latādussaṃ erakadussaṃ kadalidussaṃ veḷudussanti evamādīni pana akappiyacīvarāni. Cīvarakkhettanti ‘‘saṅghato vā gaṇato vā ñātito vā mittato vā attano vā dhanena paṃsukūlaṃ vā’’ti evaṃ uppajjanato cha khettāni, aṭṭhannañca mātikānaṃ vasena aṭṭha khettāni jānitabbāni. Paṃsukūlanti sosānikaṃ, pāpaṇikaṃ, rathiyaṃ saṅkārakūṭakaṃ, sotthiyaṃ, sinānaṃ, titthaṃ, gatapaccāgataṃ, aggidaḍḍhaṃ, gokhāyitaṃ upacikakhāyitaṃ, undūrakhāyitaṃ, antacchinnaṃ, dasācchinnaṃ, dhajāhaṭaṃ, thūpaṃ, samaṇacīvaraṃ, sāmuddiyaṃ, ābhisekiyaṃ, panthikaṃ, vātāhaṭaṃ, iddhimayaṃ, devadattiyanti tevīsati paṃsukūlāni veditabbāni. cīvaraṃ jānitabba Cīvarakkhetta aṭṭha khettāni Paṃsukūla Ettha ca sotthiyanti gabbhamalaharaṇaṃ. Gatapaccāgatanti matakasarīraṃ pārupitvā susānaṃ netvā ānītacīvaraṃ. Dhajāhaṭanti dhajaṃ ussāpetvā tato ānītaṃ. Thūpanti vammike pūjitacīvaraṃ . Sāmuddiyanti samuddavīcīhi thalaṃ pāpitaṃ. Panthikanti panthaṃ gacchantehi corabhayena pāsāṇehi koṭṭetvā pārutacīvaraṃ. Iddhimayanti ehibhikkhucīvaraṃ. Sesaṃ pākaṭameva. sotthiya Gatapaccāgata Dhajāhaṭa Thūpa Sāmuddiya Panthika Iddhimaya Cīvarasantosoti vīsati cīvarasantosā, vitakkasantoso, gamanasantoso, pariyesanasantoso, paṭilābhasantoso, mattappaṭiggahaṇasantoso, loluppavivajjanasantoso, yathālābhasantoso, yathābalasantoso, yathāsāruppasantoso, udakasantoso, dhovanasantoso, karaṇasantoso, parimāṇasantoso, suttasantoso, sibbanasantoso, rajanasantoso, kappasantoso, paribhogasantoso, sannidhiparivajjanasantoso, vissajjanasantosoti. Cīvarasantoso Tattha sādakabhikkhunā temāsaṃ nibaddhavāsaṃ vasitvā ekamāsamattaṃ vitakketuṃ vaṭṭati. So hi pavāretvā cīvaramāse cīvaraṃ karoti. Paṃsukūliko aḍḍhamāseneva karoti. Iti māsaḍḍhamāsamattaṃ vitakkanaṃ vitakkasantoso. Vitakkasantosena pana santuṭṭhena bhikkhunā pācīnakkhaṇḍarājivāsikapaṃsukūlikattherasadisena bhavitabbaṃ. Thero kira cetiyapabbatavihāre cetiyaṃ vandissāmīti āgato cetiyaṃ vanditvā cintesi ‘‘mayhaṃ cīvaraṃ jiṇṇaṃ bahūnaṃ vasanaṭṭhāne labhissāmī’’ti. So mahāvihāraṃ gantvā saṅghattheraṃ disvā vasanaṭṭhānaṃ pucchitvā tattha vuttho punadivase cīvaraṃ ādāya āgantvā theraṃ vandi. Thero kiṃ āvusoti āha. Gāmadvāraṃ, bhante, gamissāmīti. Ahampāvuso, gamissāmīti. Sādhu, bhanteti gacchanto mahābodhidvārakoṭṭhake ṭhatvā puññavantānaṃ vasanaṭṭhāne manāpaṃ labhissāmīti cintetvā aparisuddho me vitakkoti tatova paṭinivatti. Punadivase ambaṅgaṇasamīpato, punadivase mahācetiyassa uttaradvārato, tatheva paṭinivattitvā catutthadivase therassa santikaṃ agamāsi. Thero imassa bhikkhuno vitakko na parisuddho bhavissatīti cīvaraṃ gahetvā tena saddhiṃyeva pañhaṃ pucchamāno gāmaṃ pāvisi. Tañca rattiṃ eko manusso uccārapalibuddho sāṭakeyeva vaccaṃ katvā taṃ saṅkāraṭṭhāne chaḍḍesi. Paṃsukūlikatthero taṃ nīlamakkhikāhi samparikiṇṇaṃ disvā añjaliṃ paggahesi. Mahāthero ‘‘kiṃ, āvuso, saṅkāraṭṭhānassa añjaliṃ paggaṇhāsī’’ti? ‘‘Nāhaṃ, bhante, saṅkāraṭṭhānassa añjaliṃ paggaṇhāmi, mayhaṃ pitu dasabalassa paggaṇhāmi, puṇṇadāsiyā sarīraṃ pārupitvā chaḍḍitaṃ paṃsukūlaṃ tumbamatte pāṇake vidhunitvā susānato gaṇhantena dukkaraṃ kataṃ, bhante’’ti. Mahāthero ‘‘parisuddho vitakko paṃsukūlikassā’’ti cintesi. Paṃsukūlikattheropi tasmiṃyeva ṭhāne ṭhito vipassanaṃ vaḍḍhetvā tīṇi phalāni patto taṃ sāṭakaṃ gahetvā cīvaraṃ katvā pārupitvā pācīnakkhaṇḍarājiṃ gantvā aggaphalaṃ arahattaṃ pāpuṇi. Thero Paṃsukūlikatthero Cīvaratthāya gacchantassa pana ‘‘kattha labhissāmī’’ti acintetvā kammaṭṭhānasīseneva gamanaṃ gamanasantoso nāma. gamanasantoso Pariyesantassa pana yena vā tena vā saddhiṃ apariyesitvā lajjiṃ pesalaṃ bhikkhuṃ gahetvā pariyesanaṃ pariyesanasantoso nāma. pariyesanasantoso Evaṃ pariyesantassa āhariyamānaṃ cīvaraṃ dūrato disvā ‘‘etaṃ manāpaṃ bhavissati, etaṃ amanāpa’’nti evaṃ avitakketvā thūlasukhumādīsu yathāladdheneva santussanaṃ paṭilābhasantoso nāma. paṭilābhasantoso Evaṃ laddhaṃ gaṇhantassāpi ‘‘ettakaṃ dupaṭṭassa bhavissati, ettakaṃ ekapaṭṭassā’’ti attano pahonakamatteneva santussanaṃ mattappaṭiggahaṇasantoso nāma. mattappaṭiggahaṇasantoso Cīvaraṃ pariyesantassa pana ‘‘asukassa gharadvāre manāpaṃ labhissāmī’’ti acintetvā dvārapaṭipāṭiyā caraṇaṃ loluppavivajjanasantoso nāma. loluppavivajjanasantoso Lūkhapaṇītesu yena kenaci yāpetuṃ sakkontassa yathāladdheneva yāpanaṃ yathālābhasantoso nāma. yathālābhasantoso Attano thāmaṃ jānitvā yena yāpetuṃ sakkoti, tena yāpanaṃ yathābalasantoso nāma. yathābalasantoso Manāpaṃ aññassa datvā attano yena kenaci yāpanaṃ yathāsāruppasantoso nāma. yathāsāruppasantoso ‘‘Kattha udakaṃ manāpaṃ, kattha amanāpa’’nti avicāretvā yena kenaci dhovanupagena udakena dhovanaṃ udakasantoso nāma. Paṇḍumattikagerukapūtipaṇṇarasakiliṭṭhāni pana udakāni vajjetuṃ vaṭṭati. udakasantoso Dhovantassa pana muggarādīhi apaharitvā hatthehi madditvā dhovanaṃ dhovanasantoso nāma. Tathā asujjhantaṃ paṇṇāni pakkhipitvā tāpitaudakenāpi dhovituṃ vaṭṭati. dhovanasantoso Evaṃ dhovitvā karontassa idaṃ thūlaṃ, idaṃ sukhumanti akopetvā pahonakanīhāreneva karaṇaṃ karaṇasantoso nāma. karaṇasantoso Timaṇḍalappaṭicchādanamattasseva karaṇaṃ parimāṇasantoso nāma. parimāṇasantoso Cīvarakaraṇatthāya pana manāpasuttaṃ pariyesissāmīti avicāretvā rathikādīsu vā devaṭṭhāne vā āharitvā pādamūle vā ṭhapitaṃ yaṃkiñcideva suttaṃ gahetvā karaṇaṃ suttasantoso nāma. suttasantoso Kusibandhanakāle pana aṅgulamatte sattavāre na vijjhitabbaṃ, evaṃ karontassa hi yo bhikkhu sahāyo na hoti, tassa vattabhedopi natthi. Tivaṅgulamatte pana sattavāre vijjhitabbaṃ, evaṃ karontassa maggapaṭipannenāpi sahāyena bhavitabbaṃ. Yo na hoti, tassa vattabhedo. Ayaṃ sibbanasantoso nāma. sibbanasantoso Rajantena pana kāḷakacchakādīni pariyesantena na rajitabbaṃ. Somavakkalādīsu yaṃ labhati, tena rajitabbaṃ. Alabhantena pana manussehi araññe vākaṃ gahetvā chaḍḍitarajanaṃ vā bhikkhūhi pacitvā chaḍḍitakasaṭaṃ vā gahetvā rajitabbaṃ, ayaṃ rajanasantoso nāma. rajanasantoso Nīlakaddamakāḷasāmesu yaṃkiñci gahetvā hatthipiṭṭhe nisinnassa paññāyamānakapakaraṇaṃ kappasantoso nāma. kappasantoso Hirikopīnapaṭicchādanamattavasena paribhuñjanaṃ paribhogasantoso nāma. paribhogasantoso Dussaṃ pana labhitvā suttaṃ vā sūciṃ vā kārakaṃ vā alabhantena ṭhapetuṃ vaṭṭati, labhantena na vaṭṭati. Katampi sace antevāsikādīnaṃ dātukāmo hoti, te ca asannihitā yāva āgamanā ṭhapetuṃ vaṭṭati. Āgatamattesu dātabbaṃ. Dātuṃ asakkontena adhiṭṭhātabbaṃ. Aññasmiṃ cīvare sati paccattharaṇampi adhiṭṭhātuṃ vaṭṭati. Anadhiṭṭhitameva hi sannidhi hoti. Adhiṭṭhitaṃ na hotīti mahāsīvatthero āha. Ayaṃ sannidhiparivajjanasantoso nāma. mahāsīvatthero sannidhiparivajjanasantoso Vissajjantena pana na mukhaṃ oloketvā dātabbaṃ. Sāraṇīyadhamme ṭhatvā vissajjitabbanti ayaṃ vissajjanasantoso nāma. vissajjanasantoso Cīvarapaṭisaṃyuttāni dhutaṅgāni nāma paṃsukūlikaṅgañceva tecīvarikaṅgañca. Tesaṃ vitthārakathā visuddhimaggato veditabbā. Iti cīvarasantosamahāariyavaṃsaṃ pūrayamāno bhikkhu imāni dve dhutaṅgāni gopeti. Imāni gopento cīvarasantosamahāariyavaṃsena santuṭṭho hoti. Cīvarapaṭisaṃyuttāni dhutaṅgāni visuddhimaggato Vaṇṇavādīti eko santuṭṭho hoti, santosassa vaṇṇaṃ na katheti, eko na santuṭṭho hoti, santosassa vaṇṇaṃ katheti, eko neva santuṭṭho hoti, na santosassa vaṇṇaṃ katheti, eko santuṭṭho ceva hoti, santosassa ca vaṇṇaṃ katheti, taṃ dassetuṃ ‘‘itarītaracīvarasantuṭṭhiyā ca vaṇṇavādī’’ti vuttaṃ. Vaṇṇavādī Anesananti dūteyyapahinagamanānuyogappabhedaṃ nānappakāraṃ anesanaṃ. Appatirūpanti ayuttaṃ. Aladdhā cāti alabhitvā. Yathā ekacco ‘‘kathaṃ nu kho cīvaraṃ labhissāmī’’ti. Puññavantehi bhikkhūhi saddhiṃ ekato hutvā kohaññaṃ karonto uttasati paritasati, santuṭṭho bhikkhu evaṃ aladdhā cīvaraṃ na paritasati. Laddhā cāti dhammena samena labhitvā. Agadhitoti vigatalobhagiddho. Amucchitoti adhimattataṇhāya mucchaṃ anāpanno. Anajjhāpannoti taṇhāya anotthato apariyonaddho. Ādīnavadassāvīti anesanāpattiyañca gedhitaparibhoge ca ādīnavaṃ passamāno. Nissaraṇapaññoti ‘‘yāvadeva sītassa paṭighātāyā’’ti vuttaṃ nissaraṇameva pajānanto. Anesana Appatirūpa Aladdhā cā Laddhā cā Agadhito Amucchito Anajjhāpanno Ādīnavadassāvī Nissaraṇapañño Itarītaracīvarasantuṭṭhiyāti yena kenaci cīvarena santuṭṭhiyā. Nevattānukkaṃsetīti ‘‘ahaṃ paṃsukūliko mayā upasampadamāḷeyeva paṃsukūlikaṅgaṃ gahitaṃ, ko mayā sadiso atthī’’ti attukkaṃsanaṃ na karoti. Na paraṃ vambhetīti ‘‘ime panaññe bhikkhū na paṃsukūlikā’’ti vā ‘‘paṃsukūlikaṅgamattampi etesaṃ natthī’’ti vā evaṃ paraṃ na vambheti. Yo hi tattha dakkhoti yo tasmiṃ cīvarasantose, vaṇṇavādādīsu vā dakkho cheko byatto. Analasoti sātaccakiriyāya ālasiyavirahito. Sampajāno paṭissatoti sampajānapaññāya ceva satiyā ca yutto. Ariyavaṃse ṭhitoti ariyavaṃse patiṭṭhito. Itarītaracīvarasantuṭṭhiyā Nevattānukkaṃsetī Na paraṃ vambhetī Yo hi tattha dakkho Analaso Sampajāno paṭissato Ariyavaṃse ṭhito Itarītarena piṇḍapātenāti yena kenaci piṇḍapātena. Etthāpi piṇḍapāto jānitabbo. Piṇḍapātakkhettaṃ jānitabbaṃ, piṇḍapātasantoso jānitabbo, piṇḍapātapaṭisaṃyuttaṃ dhutaṅgaṃ jānitabbaṃ. Tattha piṇḍapātoti ‘‘odano, kummāso, sattu, maccho, maṃsaṃ, khīraṃ, dadhi, sappi, navanītaṃ, telaṃ, madhu, phāṇitaṃ, yāgu, khādanīyaṃ, sāyanīyaṃ, lehanīya’’nti soḷasa piṇḍapātā. Itarītarena piṇḍapātenā piṇḍapāto Piṇḍapātakkhettanti saṅghabhattaṃ, uddesabhattaṃ, nimantanaṃ, salākabhattaṃ, pakkhikaṃ, uposathikaṃ, pāṭipadikaṃ, āgantukabhattaṃ, gamikabhattaṃ, gilānabhattaṃ, gilānupaṭṭhākabhattaṃ, dhurabhattaṃ, kuṭibhattaṃ, vārabhattaṃ, vihārabhattanti pannarasa piṇḍapātakkhettāni. Piṇḍapātakkhetta Piṇḍapātasantosoti piṇḍapāte vitakkasantoso, gamanasantoso, pariyesanasantoso paṭilābhasantoso, paṭiggahaṇasantoso, mattappaṭiggahaṇasantoso, loluppavivajjanasantoso, yathālābhasantoso, yathābalasantoso, yathāsāruppasantoso, upakārasantoso, parimāṇasantoso, paribhogasantoso, sannidhiparivajjanasantoso, vissajjanasantosoti pannarasa santosā. Piṇḍapātasantoso Tattha sādako bhikkhu mukhaṃ dhovitvā vitakketi. Piṇḍapātikena pana gaṇena saddhiṃ caratā sāyaṃ therūpaṭṭhānakāle ‘‘sve kattha piṇḍāya carissāmāti asukagāme, bhante’’ti, ettakaṃ cintetvā tato paṭṭhāya na vitakketabbaṃ. Ekacārikena vitakkamāḷake ṭhatvā vitakketabbaṃ. Tato paraṃ vitakkento ariyavaṃsā cuto hoti paribāhiro. Ayaṃ vitakkasantoso nāma. vitakkasantoso Piṇḍāya pavisantena ‘‘kuhiṃ labhissāmī’’ti acintetvā kammaṭṭhānasīsena gantabbaṃ. Ayaṃ gamanasantoso nāma. gamanasantoso Pariyesantena yaṃ vā taṃ vā agahetvā lajjiṃ pesalameva gahetvā pariyesitabbaṃ. Ayaṃ pariyesanasantoso nāma. pariyesanasantoso Dūratova āhariyamānaṃ disvā ‘‘etaṃ manāpaṃ, etaṃ amanāpa’’nti cittaṃ na uppādetabbaṃ. Ayaṃ paṭilābhasantoso nāma. paṭilābhasantoso ‘‘Imaṃ manāpaṃ gaṇhissāmi, imaṃ amanāpaṃ na gaṇhissāmī’’ti acintetvā yaṃkiñci yāpanamattaṃ gahetabbameva, ayaṃ paṭiggahaṇasantoso nāma. paṭiggahaṇasantoso Ettha pana deyyadhammo bahu, dāyako appaṃ dātukāmo, appaṃ gahetabbaṃ. Deyyadhammo bahu, dāyakopi bahuṃ dātukāmo, pamāṇeneva gahetabbaṃ. Deyyadhammo na bahu, dāyakopi appaṃ dātukāmo, appaṃ gahetabbaṃ. Deyyadhammo na bahu, dāyako pana bahuṃ dātukāmo, pamāṇena gahetabbaṃ. Paṭiggahaṇasmiñhi mattaṃ ajānanto manussānaṃ pasādaṃ makkheti, saddhādeyyaṃ vinipāteti, sāsanaṃ na karoti, vijātamātuyāpi cittaṃ gahetuṃ na sakkoti. Iti mattaṃ jānitvāva paṭiggahetabbanti ayaṃ mattappaṭiggahaṇasantoso nāma. mattappaṭiggahaṇasantoso Saddhakulāniyeva agantvā dvārappaṭipāṭiyā gantabbaṃ. Ayaṃ loluppavivajjanasantoso nāma. Yathālābhasantosādayo cīvare vuttanayā eva. loluppavivajjanasantoso Yathālābhasantosā Piṇḍapātaṃ paribhuñjitvā samaṇadhammaṃ anupālessāmīti evaṃ upakāraṃ ñatvā paribhuñjanaṃ upakārasantoso nāma. upakārasantoso Pattaṃ pūretvā ānītaṃ na paṭiggahetabbaṃ, anupasampanne sati tena gāhāpetabbaṃ, asati harāpetvā paṭiggahaṇamattaṃ gahetabbaṃ. Ayaṃ parimāṇasantoso nāma. parimāṇasantoso ‘‘Jighacchāya paṭivinodanaṃ idamettha nissaraṇa’’nti evaṃ paribhuñjanaṃ paribhogasantoso nāma. paribhogasantoso Nidahitvā na paribhuñjitabbanti ayaṃ sannidhiparivajjanasantoso nāma. sannidhiparivajjanasantoso Mukhaṃ anoloketvā sāraṇīyadhamme ṭhitena vissajjetabbaṃ. Ayaṃ vissajjanasantoso nāma. vissajjanasantoso Piṇḍapātapaṭisaṃyuttāni pana pañca dhutaṅgāni – piṇḍapātikaṅgaṃ, sapadānacārikaṅgaṃ, ekāsanikaṅgaṃ , pattapiṇḍikaṅgaṃ, khalupacchābhattikaṅganti. Tesaṃ vitthārakathā visuddhimagge vuttā. Iti piṇḍapātasantosamahāariyavaṃsaṃ pūrayamāno bhikkhu imāni pañca dhutaṅgāni gopeti. Imāni gopento piṇḍapātasantosamahāariyavaṃsena santuṭṭho hoti. ‘‘Vaṇṇavādī’’tiādīni vuttanayeneva veditabbāni. Piṇḍapātapaṭisaṃyuttāni Senāsanenāti idha senāsanaṃ jānitabbaṃ, senāsanakkhettaṃ jānitabbaṃ, senāsanasantoso jānitabbo, senāsanapaṭisaṃyuttaṃ dhutaṅgaṃ jānitabbaṃ. Tattha senāsananti mañco, pīṭhaṃ, bhisi, bimbohanaṃ, vihāro, aḍḍhayogo, pāsādo, hammiyaṃ, guhā, leṇaṃ, aṭṭo, māḷo , veḷugumbo, rukkhamūlaṃ, yattha vā pana bhikkhū paṭikkamantīti imāni pannarasa senāsanāni. Senāsanenā senāsana Senāsanakkhettanti ‘‘saṅghato vā gaṇato vā ñātito vā mittato vā attano vā dhanena paṃsukūlaṃ vā’’ti cha khettāni. Senāsanakkhetta Senāsanasantosoti senāsane vitakkasantosādayo pannarasa santosā. Te piṇḍapāte vuttanayeneva veditabbā. Senāsanapaṭisaṃyuttāni pana pañca dhutaṅgāni – āraññikaṅgaṃ, rukkhamūlikaṅgaṃ, abbhokāsikaṅgaṃ, sosānikaṅgaṃ, yathāsantatikaṅganti. Tesaṃ vitthārakathā visuddhimagge vuttā. Iti senāsanasantosamahāariyavaṃsaṃ pūrayamāno bhikkhu imāni pañca dhutaṅgāni gopeti. Imāni gopento senāsanasantosamahāariyavaṃsena santuṭṭho hoti. Senāsanasantoso Senāsanapaṭisaṃyuttāni Gilānapaccayo pana piṇḍapāteyeva paviṭṭho. Tattha yathālābhayathābalayathāsāruppasantoseneva santussitabbaṃ. Nesajjikaṅgaṃ bhāvanārāmaariyavaṃsaṃ bhajati. Vuttampi cetaṃ – ‘‘Pañca senāsane vuttā, pañca āhāranissitā; Eko vīriyasaṃyutto, dve ca cīvaranissitā’’ti. Iti āyasmā dhammasenāpati sāriputtatthero pathaviṃ pattharamāno viya sāgarakucchiṃ pūrayamāno viya ākāsaṃ vitthārayamāno viya ca paṭhamaṃ cīvarasantosaṃ ariyavaṃsaṃ kathetvā candaṃ uṭṭhāpento viya sūriyaṃ ullaṅghento viya ca dutiyaṃ piṇḍapātasantosaṃ kathetvā sineruṃ ukkhipento viya tatiyaṃ senāsanasantosaṃ ariyavaṃsaṃ kathetvā idāni sahassanayappaṭimaṇḍitaṃ catutthaṃ bhāvanārāmaṃ ariyavaṃsaṃ kathetuṃ puna caparaṃ āvuso bhikkhu pahānārāmo hotīti desanaṃ ārabhi. puna caparaṃ āvuso bhikkhu pahānārāmo hotī Tattha āramanaṃ ārāmo, abhiratīti attho. Pañcavidhe pahāne ārāmo assāti pahānārāmo. Kāmacchandaṃ pajahanto ramati, nekkhammaṃ bhāvento ramati, byāpādaṃ pajahanto ramati…pe… sabbakilese pajahanto ramati, arahattamaggaṃ bhāvento ramatīti evaṃ pahāne ratoti pahānarato. Vuttanayeneva bhāvanāya ārāmo assāti bhāvanārāmo. Bhāvanāya ratoti bhāvanārato. pahānārāmo. pahānarato bhāvanārāmo. bhāvanārato. Imesu pana catūsu ariyavaṃsesu purimehi tīhi terasannaṃ dhutaṅgānaṃ catupaccayasantosassa ca vasena sakalaṃ vinayapiṭakaṃ kathitaṃ hoti. Bhāvanārāmena avasesaṃ piṭakadvayaṃ. Imaṃ pana bhāvanārāmataṃ ariyavaṃsaṃ kathentena bhikkhunā paṭisambhidāmagge nekkhammapāḷiyā kathetabbo. Dīghanikāye dasuttarasuttantapariyāyena kathetabbo. Majjhimanikāye satipaṭṭhānasuttantapariyāyena kathetabbo. Abhidhamme niddesapariyāyena kathetabbo. Tattha paṭisambhidāmagge nekkhammapāḷiyāti so nekkhammaṃ bhāvento ramati, kāmacchandaṃ pajahanto ramati. Abyāpādaṃ byāpādaṃ. Ālokasaññaṃ, thinamiddhaṃ. Avikkhepaṃ uddhaccaṃ. Dhammavavatthānaṃ, vicikicchaṃ. Ñāṇaṃ, avijjaṃ. Pāmojjaṃ, aratiṃ. Paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ, pañca nīvaraṇe. Dutiyaṃ jhānaṃ, vitakkavicāre. Tatiyaṃ jhānaṃ, pītiṃ. Catutthaṃ jhānaṃ, sukhadukkhe. Ākāsānañcāyatanasamāpattiṃ bhāvento ramati, rūpasaññaṃ paṭighasaññaṃ nānattasaññaṃ pajahanto ramati. Viññāṇañcāyatanasamāpattiṃ…pe… nevasaññānāsaññāyatanasamāpattiṃ bhāvento ramati, ākiñcaññāyatanasaññaṃ pajahanto ramati. paṭisambhidāmagge nekkhammapāḷiyā Aniccānupassanaṃ bhāvento ramati, niccasaññaṃ pajahanto ramati. Dukkhānupassanaṃ, sukhasaññaṃ. Anattānupassanaṃ, attasaññaṃ. Nibbidānupassanaṃ, nandiṃ. Virāgānupassanaṃ, rāgaṃ. Nirodhānupassanaṃ, samudayaṃ. Paṭinissaggānupassanaṃ, ādānaṃ. Khayānupassanaṃ, ghanasaññaṃ. Vayānupassanaṃ, āyūhanaṃ. Vipariṇāmānupassanaṃ, dhuvasaññaṃ. Animittānupassanaṃ, nimittaṃ. Apaṇihitānupassanaṃ, paṇidhiṃ. Suññatānupassanaṃ abhinivesaṃ. Adhipaññādhammavipassanaṃ, sārādānābhinivesaṃ. Yathābhūtañāṇadassanaṃ, sammohābhinivesaṃ. Ādīnavānupassanaṃ, ālayābhinivesaṃ. Paṭisaṅkhānupassanaṃ, appaṭisaṅkhaṃ. Vivaṭṭānupassanaṃ, saṃyogābhinivesaṃ. Sotāpattimaggaṃ , diṭṭhekaṭṭhe kilese. Sakadāgāmimaggaṃ, oḷārike kilese. Anāgāmimaggaṃ, aṇusahagate kilese. Arahattamaggaṃ bhāvento ramati, sabbakilese pajahanto ramatīti evaṃ paṭisambhidāmagge nekkhammapāḷiyā kathetabbo. paṭisambhidāmagge Dīghanikāyedasuttarasuttantapariyāyenāti ekaṃ dhammaṃ bhāvento ramati, ekaṃ dhammaṃ pajahanto ramati…pe… dasa dhamme bhāvento ramati, dasa dhamme pajahanto ramati. Katamaṃ ekaṃ dhammaṃ bhāvento ramati? Kāyagatāsatiṃ sātasahagataṃ. Imaṃ ekaṃ dhammaṃ bhāvento ramati. Katamaṃ ekaṃ dhammaṃ pajahanto ramati? Asmimānaṃ. Imaṃ ekaṃ dhammaṃ pajahanto ramati. Katame dve dhamme…pe… katame dasa dhamme bhāvento ramati? Dasa kasiṇāyatanāni. Ime dasa dhamme bhāvento ramati. Katame dasa dhamme pajahanto ramati? Dasa micchatte. Ime dasa dhamme pajahanto ramati. Evaṃ kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu bhāvanārāmo hotīti evaṃ dīghanikāye dasuttarasuttantapariyāyena kathetabbo. Dīghanikāye dasuttarasuttantapariyāyenā Majjhimanikāye satipaṭṭhānasuttantapariyāyenāti ekāyano, bhikkhave, maggo sattānaṃ visuddhiyā, sokaparidevānaṃ samatikkamāya, dukkhadomanassānaṃ atthaṅgamāya, ñāyassa adhigamāya, nibbānassa sacchikiriyāya yadidaṃ cattāro satipaṭṭhānā. Katame cattāro? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu kāye kāyānupassī viharati… vedanāsu vedanānupassī… citte cittānupassī… dhammesu dhammānupassī… ‘atthi dhammā’ti vā panassa sati paccupaṭṭhitā hoti yāvadeva ñāṇamattāya paṭissatimattāya anissito ca viharati na ca kiñci loke upādiyati. Evampi, bhikkhave, bhikkhu bhāvanārāmo hoti bhāvanārato, pahānārāmo hoti pahānarato. Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu gacchanto vā gacchāmīti pajānāti…pe… puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu seyyathāpi passeyya sarīraṃ sivathikāya chaḍḍitaṃ…pe… pūtīni cuṇṇakajātāni. So imameva kāyaṃ upasaṃharati, ayampi kho kāyo evaṃdhammo evaṃbhāvī evaṃanatītoti. Iti ajjhattaṃ vā kāye kāyānupassī viharati…pe… evampi kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu bhāvanārāmo hotīti evaṃ majjhimanikāye satipaṭṭhānasuttantapariyāyena kathetabbo. Majjhimanikāye satipaṭṭhānasuttantapariyāyenā Abhidhamme niddesapariyāyenāti sabbepi saṅkhate aniccato dukkhato rogato gaṇḍato…pe… saṃkilesikadhammato passanto ramati. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu bhāvanārāmo hotīti evaṃ niddesapariyāyena kathetabbo. Abhidhamme niddesapariyāyenā Nevaattānukkaṃsetīti ajja me saṭṭhi vā sattati vā vassāni aniccaṃ dukkhaṃ anattāti vipassanāya kammaṃ karontassa, ko mayā sadiso atthīti evaṃ attukkaṃsanaṃ na karoti. Na paraṃ vambhetīti aniccaṃ dukkhanti vipassanāmattakampi natthi, kiṃ ime vissaṭṭhakammaṭṭhānā carantīti evaṃ paraṃ vambhanaṃ na karoti. Sesaṃ vuttanayameva. Neva attānukkaṃsetī Na paraṃ vambhetī
309. "ariyavaṃsā" means the lineages of the noble ones (ārya). Just as there is the lineage of the Kṣatriyas, the lineage of the Brahmins, the lineage of the Vaiśyas, the lineage of the Śūdras, the lineage of the Śramaṇas, the family lineage, the royal lineage, so too, this eighth lineage of the noble ones, which is noble, is called the noble tradition (ariyapaveṇī). And this lineage of the noble ones is called the foremost, like the kālānusārika-gandha, etc., from the root fragrance, etc., of these lineages. But who are these noble ones whose lineages these are? The noble ones are called Buddhas, Pratyekabuddhas, and disciples of the Tathāgata; the lineages of these noble ones are the "ariyavaṃsā." Before this, at the head of four asaṃkhyeyyas exceeding a hundred thousand kalpas, four Buddhas arose: Tṛṣṇaṃkara, Medhaṃkara, Saraṇaṃkara, and Dīpaṃkara; they are noble ones, the lineages of those noble ones are ariyavaṃsā. After the parinirvāṇa of those Buddhas, having passed asaṃkhyeyyas, a Buddha named Koṇḍañña arose...pe... In this kalpa, four Buddhas arose: Kakusandha, Koṇāgamana, Kassapa, and our Blessed One Gotama. The lineages of these noble ones are ariyavaṃsā. Moreover, the lineages of all Buddhas, Pratyekabuddhas, and Buddha disciples of the past, future, and present are ariyavaṃsā. These are to be known as "aggaññā," as foremost. "rattaññā" are to be known as long-lasting. "vaṃsaññā" are to be known as lineages.

"porāṇā" means not newly arisen. "asaṃkiṇṇā" means unmixed, not scattered, not removed. "asaṃkiṇṇapubbā" means not previously mixed by past Buddhas. "kiṃ imehī" means not previously rejected? "na saṅkīyantī" means even now they are not rejected. "na saṅkīyissantī" means even by future Buddhas they will not be rejected; those who are wise śramaṇas and brāhmaṇas in the world, they are unreproached, unblamed, unrejected by wise śramaṇas and brāhmaṇas.

porāṇā asaṃkiṇṇā asaṃkiṇṇapubbā na saṅkīyantī na saṅkīyissantī "santuṭṭho hoti" means he is content by way of contentment with the requisites. "itarītarena cīvarena" means with whatever, whether coarse, fine, rough, excellent, durable, or worn. But it means he is content with whatever, any kind of what is obtained, etc. For in regard to robes there are three kinds of contentment: contentment with whatever is obtained, contentment with whatever strength, contentment with whatever is suitable. The same method applies to piṇḍapāta, etc. Their detailed explanation should be understood in the same way as stated in the Sāmaññaphala [Sutta]. With reference to these three kinds of contentment it is said, "santuṭṭho hoti, itarītarena, with whatever is obtained, etc., he is content with whatever robe."

santuṭṭho hoti itarītarena cīvarena Here, the robe should be known, the robe field should be known, the rag robe (pāṃśukūla) should be known, contentment with the robe should be known, and the dhutaṅgas connected with the robe should be known. There, "the robe should be known" means the six robes of khoma, etc., and the six conforming robes of dukūla, etc., should be known. These twelve are allowable robes. But the unallowable robes are: robes of kusa grass, robes of bark, robes of fruit fibers, robes of human hair, robes of horsehair, blankets, hides, owl wings, tree bark cloth, creeper cloth, eraka cloth, kadali cloth, veḷu cloth, and so on. "cīvarakkhetta" means the six fields from which they arise: "from the Saṅgha, from the group, from relatives, from friends, or from one's own wealth, or a rag robe," and the eight fields should be known in terms of the eight matrices. "paṃsukūla" means: from the cemetery, from the market, from the road, from the heap of rubbish, from the lying-in place, from the bathing place, from the ford, gone and returned, burnt by fire, eaten by cows, eaten by white ants, eaten by rats, cut at the edge, torn at the edge, brought by a flag, from a stupa, a śramaṇa's robe, from the sea, for anointing, from the road, brought by the wind, made by magic, given by Devadatta; these twenty-three pāṃsukūlas should be known.

cīvaraṃ jānitabba cīvarakkhetta aṭṭha khettāni paṃsukūla Here, "sotthiya" means removing the impurities of the womb. "gatapaccāgata" means a robe that is covered over a dead body, taken to the cemetery, and brought back. "dhajāhaṭa" means brought from there, having raised a flag. "thūpa" means a robe worshiped in an anthill.
0175"/>.
sāmuddiyanti: (That which is) brought to shore by the waves of the sea.
panthikanti: A robe torn and worn by those traveling on the road due to fear of thieves, having struck it with stones.
iddhimayanti: A robe (obtained) by the ehibhikkhu method.
The rest is but clear.

sotthiya gatapaccāgata dhajāhaṭa thūpa sāmuddiya panthika iddhimaya
The contentment with robes (cīvarasaṃtoso) is twentyfold: contentment with thought (vitakkasaṃtoso), contentment with going (gamanaṃsaṃtoso), contentment with seeking (pariyesanasaṃtoso), contentment with gain (paṭilābhasaṃtoso), contentment with receiving the right amount (mattappaṭiggahaṇasaṃtoso), contentment with avoiding greed (loluppavivajjanasaṃtoso), contentment with what is obtained (yathālābhasaṃtoso), contentment with one's strength (yathābalasaṃtoso), contentment with suitability (yathāsāruppasaṃtoso), contentment with water (udakasaṃtoso), contentment with washing (dhovanasaṃtoso), contentment with making (karaṇasaṃtoso), contentment with the measure (parimāṇasaṃtoso), contentment with thread (suttasaṃtoso), contentment with sewing (sibbanasaṃtoso), contentment with dyeing (rajanasaṃtoso), contentment with cutting (kappasaṃtoso), contentment with use (paribhogasaṃtoso), contentment with avoiding accumulation (sannidhiparivajjanasaṃtoso), contentment with giving away (vissajjanasaṃtoso).

In that regard, a bhikkhu who is a learner (sādakabhikkhunā) should dwell for three months in fixed residence and it is fitting to reflect for about one month.
For he, having concluded the pavāraṇā, makes a robe during the robe-month (cīvaramāse).
A rag-robe wearer (paṃsukūliko) makes (it) in just half a month.
Thus, reflecting for a month or half a month is contentment with reflection (vitakkasantoso).
A bhikkhu who is content by means of contentment with reflection (vitakkasantosena) should be like the Elder Rag-robe wearer residing in Pācīnakkhanda (pācīnakkhaṇḍarājivāsikapaṃsukūlikattherasadisena).

The Elder (thero), it is said, having come to the Cetiyapabbata monastery thinking, “I will worship the cetiya,” having worshipped the cetiya, thought, “My robe is worn out; I will obtain (a new one) in a place where many robes are.”
He, having gone to the Great Monastery (mahāvihāraṃ), having seen the Elder of the Saṅgha (saṅghattheraṃ), having asked about a place for robes, having dwelt there, on the following day, having taken a robe, having come back, worshiped the Elder.
The Elder said, “What is it, friend (āvuso)?”
“I will go to the village gate, venerable sir (bhante).”
“I, too, friend (āvuso), will go.”
“Good, venerable sir (bhante)” Going, having stood in the gateway of the Great Bodhi tree (mahābodhidvārakoṭṭhake), thinking, “I will obtain a pleasing (robe) in a place of meritorious ones (puññavantānaṃ vasanaṭṭhāne)”, (and) thinking, “My thought is not pure (aparisuddho me vitakko)”, he turned back from there.
On the following day, near the mango grove (ambaṅgaṇasamīpato), on the following day, from the northern gate of the Great cetiya (mahācetiyassa uttaradvārato), just so, having turned back, on the fourth day, he went to the Elder's presence (therassa santikaṃ agamāsi).
The Elder, thinking, “The thought of this bhikkhu is not pure (parisuddho bhavissati)”, having taken a robe, while asking a question along with him, entered the village.
And that night, a certain man, overwhelmed by the urge to defecate, having relieved himself right on (his) cloak (sāṭake), threw that (cloak) in the refuse heap (saṅkāraṭṭhāne).
The Elder Rag-robe wearer (paṃsukūlikatthero), having seen that (cloak) swarming with blue flies (nīlamakkhikāhi samparikiṇṇaṃ), extended his joined palms (añjaliṃ paggahesi).
The Great Elder (mahāthero) (asked), “Why, friend (āvuso), do you extend your joined palms (añjaliṃ paggaṇhāsī) to the refuse heap (saṅkāraṭṭhānassa)?” “I do not, venerable sir (bhante), extend my joined palms (añjaliṃ paggaṇhāmi) to the refuse heap (saṅkāraṭṭhānassa); I extend (them) to my father, the Ten-Powered One (dasabalassa), venerable sir, (because it is) rag-robe (paṃsukūlaṃ) cast off (after) covering the body of Puṇṇadāsī, (and) it was difficult to do (dukkaraṃ kataṃ) by (me) shaking out the worms (pāṇake) to the measure of a tumba from the cemetery (susānato gaṇhantena)".
The Great Elder (mahāthero) thought, “The thought of the Rag-robe wearer (paṃsukūlikassa) is pure (parisuddho vitakko)”.
The Elder Rag-robe wearer (paṃsukūlikattheropi), having stood right there in that place, having developed insight (vipassanaṃ vaḍḍhetvā), having attained the three fruits (tīṇi phalāni patto), having taken that cloak (taṃ sāṭakaṃ gahetvā), having made a robe (cīvaraṃ katvā) (and) having worn (it) (pārupitvā), having gone to Pācīnakkhandarājiṃ, attained the highest fruit, Arahantship (aggaphalaṃ arahattaṃ pāpuṇi).

The Elder, the Rag-robe wearer (thero paṃsukūlikatthero).
For one going for the sake of a robe (cīvaratthāya), the going itself (gamanam) is contentment with going (gamanasaṃtoso), not thinking, “Where shall I obtain (it) (kattha labhissāmī?)”, (but going) just with mindfulness of the meditation subject (kammaṭṭhānasīsena).

For one seeking, taking a modest and amiable bhikkhu, without seeking just with whomever or whatever, the seeking is contentment with seeking (pariyesanasantoso).

Thus, for one seeking, seeing from afar a robe being brought, without thinking, “This will be pleasing (manāpaṃ bhavissati), this (will be) unpleasing (amanāpa)”, but just being content (santussanaṃ) with whatever has been obtained (yathāladdheneva) among the coarse, fine, etc., is contentment with gain (paṭilābhasantoso).

Thus, even when taking what has been obtained, the contentment with just the amount that is sufficient for oneself, (thinking,) "This much will be for the double-layered one (dupaṭṭassa bhavissati), this much for the single-layered one (ekapaṭṭassa)”, is called contentment with receiving the right amount (mattappaṭiggahaṇasantoso).

For one seeking a robe, not thinking “At the door of such-and-such a house I will obtain a pleasing one (manāpaṃ labhissāmi)”, but going in the order of the doorways is contentment with avoiding greed (loluppavivajjanasantoso).
Loluppavivajjanasantosa: Contentment with avoiding greed.

Lūkhapaṇītesu yena kenaci yāpetuṃ sakkontassa yathāladdheneva yāpanaṃ yathālābhasantoso nāma: The ability to subsist on whatever coarse or fine food one receives, and subsisting only on what has been received, is called "contentment with whatever is obtained" (yathālābhasantosa).

Yathālābhasantoso: Contentment with whatever is obtained.

Attano thāmaṃ jānitvā yena yāpetuṃ sakkoti, tena yāpanaṃ yathābalasantoso nāma: Knowing one's own strength, and subsisting in a way that one is capable of, is called "contentment with appropriate strength" (yathābalasantosa).

Yathābalasantoso: Contentment with appropriate strength.

Manāpaṃ aññassa datvā attano yena kenaci yāpanaṃ yathāsāruppasantoso nāma: Giving what is pleasing to others, and subsisting on whatever one has, is called "contentment with suitability" (yathāsāruppasantosa).

Yathāsāruppasantoso: Contentment with suitability.

“Kattha udakaṃ manāpaṃ, kattha amanāpa”nti avicāretvā yena kenaci dhovanupagena udakena dhovanaṃ udakasantoso nāma: Not considering “Where is the water pleasing, where is it unpleasing?”, and washing with whatever water that is fit for washing, is called "contentment with water" (udakasantosa).

But water that is stained with dirt, clay, geruka (red ochre), putrid leaves, or that is slimy, should be avoided.

Udakasantoso: Contentment with water.

Dhovantassa pana muggarādīhi apaharitvā hatthehi madditvā dhovanaṃ dhovanasantoso nāma: For one who is washing, removing [dirt] with a mallet and such, and washing by rubbing with the hands, is called "contentment with washing" (dhovanasantosa).

Likewise, if [the robe] is not clean, it is proper to wash it by putting leaves in and using heated water.

Dhovanasantoso: Contentment with washing.

Evaṃ dhovitvā karontassa idaṃ thūlaṃ, idaṃ sukhumanti akopetvā pahonakanīhāreneva karaṇaṃ karaṇasantoso nāma: Thus, for one who is washing, not being angry, thinking “This is coarse, this is fine,” but doing it with sufficient effort, is called "contentment with doing" (karaṇasantosa).

Karaṇasantoso: Contentment with doing.

Timaṇḍalappaṭicchādanamattasseva karaṇaṃ parimāṇasantoso nāma: Doing only what is needed to cover the three robes is called "contentment with measure" (parimāṇasantosa).

Parimāṇasantoso: Contentment with measure.

Cīvarakaraṇatthāya pana manāpasuttaṃ pariyesissāmīti avicāretvā rathikādīsu vā devaṭṭhāne vā āharitvā pādamūle vā ṭhapitaṃ yaṃkiñcideva suttaṃ gahetvā karaṇaṃ suttasantoso nāma: Without considering, "I will seek thread that is pleasing for the purpose of making robes," but bringing it from roadsides, or from a shrine, or taking whatever thread has been placed at the foot of a tree, and doing it, is called "contentment with thread" (suttasantosa).

Suttasantoso: Contentment with thread.

Kusibandhanakāle pana aṅgulamatte sattavāre na vijjhitabbaṃ, evaṃ karontassa hi yo bhikkhu sahāyo na hoti, tassa vattabhedopi natthi: At the time of tying the hem, it should not be pierced seven times in the space of a finger-breadth. For if a bhikṣu is not a companion when one does this, there is no difference in his behavior.

Tivaṅgulamatte pana sattavāre vijjhitabbaṃ, evaṃ karontassa maggapaṭipannenāpi sahāyena bhavitabbaṃ: But it should be pierced seven times in the space of three finger-breadths. When one does this, one should be a companion even to one who has set out on the path.

If one is not, there is a difference in behavior.

Ayaṃ sibbanasantoso nāma: This is called "contentment with sewing" (sibbanasantosa).

Sibbanasantoso: Contentment with sewing.

Rajantena pana kāḷakacchakādīni pariyesantena na rajitabbaṃ: But when dyeing, one should not dye [the robes] while seeking kāḷakacchaka and other dyes.

Somavakkalādīsu yaṃ labhati, tena rajitabbaṃ: One should dye [the robes] with whatever one obtains from somavakkala and other [sources].

Alabhantena pana manussehi araññe vākaṃ gahetvā chaḍḍitarajanaṃ vā bhikkhūhi pacitvā chaḍḍitakasaṭaṃ vā gahetvā rajitabbaṃ, ayaṃ rajanasantoso nāma: But if one does not obtain [dyes], one should dye [the robes] by taking the bark discarded by people in the forest, or by taking the sediment discarded by the bhikṣus after boiling [dyes]. This is called "contentment with dye" (rajanasantosa).

Rajanasantoso: Contentment with dye.

Nīlakaddamakāḷasāmesu yaṃkiñci gahetvā hatthipiṭṭhe nisinnassa paññāyamānakapakaraṇaṃ kappasantoso nāma: Taking whatever [dye] is available from blue mud, black soil, and such, and having the apparent means of dyeing while sitting on an elephant's back, is called "contentment with preparation" (kappasantosa).

Kappasantoso: Contentment with preparation.

Hirikopīnapaṭicchādanamattavasena paribhuñjanaṃ paribhogasantoso nāma: Using [robes] merely to cover the shame and the private parts is called "contentment with use" (paribhogasantosa).

Paribhogasantoso: Contentment with use.

Dussaṃ pana labhitvā suttaṃ vā sūciṃ vā kārakaṃ vā alabhantena ṭhapetuṃ vaṭṭati, labhantena na vaṭṭati: But if one obtains cloth, if one does not obtain thread, a needle, or a worker, it is proper to put it away; it is not proper for one who obtains [these things to put the cloth away].

Katampi sace antevāsikādīnaṃ dātukāmo hoti, te ca asannihitā yāva āgamanā ṭhapetuṃ vaṭṭati: If one intends to give a Kathinacloth to one's students and such, and they are not present, it is proper to put it away until they arrive.

Āgatamattesu dātabbaṃ: It should be given as soon as they have arrived.

Dātuṃ asakkontena adhiṭṭhātabbaṃ: One who is not able to give should resolve [to do so].

Aññasmiṃ cīvare sati paccattharaṇampi adhiṭṭhātuṃ vaṭṭati: If there is another robe, it is proper to resolve to use even a paccattharaṇa (a spread or covering).

Anadhiṭṭhitameva hi sannidhi hoti: For what is not resolved [for use] is indeed [subject to the rule of] storing up.

Adhiṭṭhitaṃ na hotīti Mahāsīvatthero āha: The Venerable Mahāsīva said, “What is resolved is not subject to this rule.”

Ayaṃ sannidhiparivajjanasantoso nāma: This is called "contentment with avoiding storing up" (sannidhiparivajjanasantosa).

Mahāsīvatthero Sannidhiparivajjanasantoso: The Venerable Mahāsīva: Contentment with avoiding storing up.

Vissajjantena pana na mukhaṃ oloketvā dātabbaṃ: But when giving [a robe away], one should not give it away after looking at [the receiver's] face.

Sāraṇīyadhamme ṭhatvā vissajjitabbanti ayaṃ vissajjanasantoso nāma: Thinking "I will give it away while abiding in qualities that create amiability", this is called "contentment with giving away" (vissajjanasantosa).

Vissajjanasantoso: Contentment with giving away.

Cīvarapaṭisaṃyuttāni dhutaṅgāni nāma paṃsukūlikaṅgañceva tecīvarikaṅgañca: The dhutaṅgas connected with robes are called the paṃsukūlikaṅga (the practice of wearing robes made of rags collected from dust heaps) and the tecīvarikaṅga (the practice of possessing only three robes).

Tesaṃ vitthārakathā visuddhimaggato veditabbā: The detailed explanation of these should be understood from the Visuddhimagga.

Iti cīvarasantosamahāariyavaṃsaṃ pūrayamāno bhikkhu imāni dve dhutaṅgāni gopeti: Thus, a bhikṣu who fulfills this great lineage of the noble ones, characterized by contentment with robes, protects these two dhutaṅgas.
Protecting these, he is content with the great lineage of the noble one, being content with robes ( cīvara ).

The dhutaṅgas related to robes, from the Visuddhimagga:

"A praiser of virtue (vaṇṇavādī)": one is content and does not speak of the virtue of contentment; one is not content and speaks of the virtue of contentment; one is neither content nor speaks of the virtue of contentment; one is content and speaks of the virtue of contentment; to show that, it is said, "A praiser of virtue through contentment with any kind of robe".

"Non-seeking (anesana)": non-seeking of various kinds, such as different kinds of sending as a messenger, going as a go-between, or pressing a claim.

"Unsuitable (appatirūpa)": improper.

"Not obtained (aladdhā ca )": without obtaining.

Just as someone might think, "How shall I obtain a robe?".

While one is making an effort, in company with virtuous bhikkhus, doing flattery, he trembles and is agitated; the content bhikkhu, even without obtaining a robe in this way, is not agitated.

"Having obtained (laddhā ca)": having obtained through the Dharma in a proper way.

"Unattached (agadhito)": devoid of greed and craving.

"Uninfatuated (amucchito)": not subject to bewilderment from excessive craving (taṇhā).

"Not addicted (anajjhāpanno)": not overwhelmed or completely bound by craving (taṇhā).

"Seeing danger (ādīnavadassāvī)": seeing danger in both non-seeking and greedy use.

"Having wisdom of escape (nissaraṇapañño)": knowing only the escape, as it was said, "Merely for protection from the cold".

"By contentment with any kind of robe whatsoever (itarītaracīvarasantuṭṭhiyā)".

"He does not exalt himself (nevattānukkaṃsetī)": he does not exalt himself by saying, "I am a rag-robe wearer (paṃsukūlika), I have undertaken the practice of wearing rag-robes (paṃsukūlikaṅga) since my ordination; who is like me?".

"He does not disparage others (na paraṃ vambhetī)": he does not disparage others by saying, "These other bhikkhus are not rag-robe wearers" or "They do not even have the smallest part of the rag-robe practice (paṃsukūlikaṅgamattampi)".

"He who is skilled therein (yo hi tattha dakkho)": he who is skilled, clever, and expert in that contentment with robes, or in praising virtue, etc.

"Not lazy (analaso)": unseparated from constant action.

"Mindful and recollecting (sampajāno paṭissato)": endowed with both mindful wisdom (sampajāno paññāya) and recollection (satiyā).

"Established in the noble lineage (ariyavaṃse ṭhito)": established in the noble lineage (ariyavaṃse patiṭṭhito).

"By contentment with any kind of alms-food whatsoever (itarītarena piṇḍapātenā)".

Here also, alms-food (piṇḍapāta) should be understood.

The field of alms-food (piṇḍapātakkhetta) should be understood, contentment with alms-food (piṇḍapātasantoso) should be understood, the dhutaṅga connected with alms-food should be understood.

Therein, "Piṇḍapāta (alms-food)": rice, porridge, barley-meal, fish, meat, milk, curd, ghee, fresh butter, oil, honey, molasses, gruel, hard food, soft food, and licking food—these are the sixteen alms-foods (piṇḍapātā).

"The field of alms-food (piṇḍapātakkhetta)": food for the saṅgha, food assigned, invitation, food by lot, fortnightly food, uposatha food, first-day food, food for guests, food for travelers, food for the sick, food for attendants of the sick, regular food, hut food, turn-food, monastery food—these are the fifteen fields of alms-food (piṇḍapātakkhettāni).

"Piṇḍapātasantoso (contentment with alms-food)": in alms-food, contentment with deliberation, contentment with going, contentment with seeking, contentment with obtaining, contentment with receiving, contentment with moderate receiving, contentment with avoiding greed, contentment with what is obtained, contentment with strength, contentment with suitability, contentment with assistance, contentment with measure, contentment with use, contentment with avoiding accumulation, contentment with giving up—these are the fifteen contentments (santosā).

Therein, the striving bhikkhu, having washed his face, deliberates.

The piṇḍapātika, however, when walking in company with a group, in the evening at the time of attending upon the elders, having thought, "Where shall we go for alms-food tomorrow, bhante, in such-and-such a village?", from then on, no deliberation should be done.

By one who dwells alone, deliberation should be done while standing in the deliberation-enclosure.

Deliberating further than that, he falls away from the noble lineage (ariyavaṃsa) and becomes an outsider (paribāhiro).

This is called "contentment with deliberation" (vitakkasantoso).

He who is entering for alms-food, without thinking "Where shall I obtain it?"
1015"/> It should be approached with the head of meditation subject.
This is called gamanasantoso (satisfaction with going).
Gamanasantoso The seeker, without taking this or that, having taken only what is proper and modest, should seek.
This is called pariyesanasantoso (satisfaction with seeking).
Pariyesanasantoso Seeing it being brought from afar , the thought, “This is pleasing, this is displeasing,” should not be produced in the mind.
This is called paṭilābhasantoso (satisfaction with gain).
Paṭilābhasantoso Without thinking, "I will take this that is pleasing, I will not take this that is displeasing," whatever is just enough to keep alive should be taken. This is called paṭiggahaṇasantoso (satisfaction with acceptance).
Paṭiggahaṇasantoso Here, however, if the alms-food is plentiful, [but] the giver wants to give little, little should be taken.
If the alms-food is plentiful, and the giver also wants to give much, it should be taken according to measure.
If the alms-food is not plentiful, and the giver also wants to give little, little should be taken.
If the alms-food is not plentiful, but the giver wants to give much, it should be taken according to measure.
Indeed, in accepting alms , one who does not know moderation spoils people's faith, ruins the gift of faith, does not do the teaching, and is not able to win over even a wise mother’s mind.
Having known moderation in this way, since it should be accepted, this is called mattappaṭiggahaṇasantoso (satisfaction with accepting in moderation).
Mattappaṭiggahaṇasantoso Without going to houses of faith alone, one should go in order from door to door.
This is called loluppavivajjanasantoso (satisfaction with avoiding greed).
Yathālābhasantosā (satisfaction with whatever is received), etc., are in the same way as stated regarding robes.
Loluppavivajjanasantoso yathālābhasantosā Having eaten alms-food, thinking, "I will protect the ascetic's practice," thus knowing the benefit and eating, this is called upakārasantoso (satisfaction with benefit).
Upakārasantoso Food brought filling the bowl should not be accepted; if there is one who is not fully ordained, it should be made to be taken by him; if there is not one, having had it removed, only the act of accepting should be taken.
This is called parimāṇasantoso (satisfaction with measure).
Parimāṇasantoso “This is a means of escape from hunger,” thus eating, this is called paribhogasantoso (satisfaction with use).
Paribhogasantoso It should not be stored up and eaten. This is called sannidhiparivajjanasantoso (satisfaction with avoiding hoarding).
Sannidhiparivajjanasantoso Without looking at the face, it should be given away by one standing in the memorable qualities.
This is called vissajjanasantoso (satisfaction with giving away).
Vissajjanasantoso Piṇḍapātapaṭisaṃyuttāni (the practices connected with alms-food), however, are five ascetic practices: piṇḍapātikaṅgaṃ (the practice of being an alms-food eater), sapadānacārikaṅgaṃ (the practice of going from house to house for alms), ekāsanikaṅgaṃ (the practice of eating at one sitting) , pattapiṇḍikaṅgaṃ (the practice of eating only from the bowl), khalupacchābhattikaṅgaṃ (the practice of refusing food offered after one has begun eating).
Their detailed discussion is stated in the Visuddhimagga (Path of Purification).
Thus, the bhikkhu, fulfilling the great lineage of the noble ones, which is satisfaction with alms-food, protects these five ascetic practices.
Protecting these, he is satisfied with the great lineage of the noble ones, which is satisfaction with alms-food.
"One who speaks well," etc., should be understood in the way stated above.
Piṇḍapātapaṭisaṃyuttāni Senāsanenā (by a dwelling place): here, senāsana (dwelling place) should be known, the area of the dwelling place should be known, satisfaction with the dwelling place should be known, and the ascetic practice connected with the dwelling place should be known.
There, senāsana (dwelling place) means a couch, a seat, a mat, a pillow, a vihāra (monastery), a lean-to, a palace, a mansion, a cave, a lodging, a garret, a hall , a bamboo grove, the foot of a tree, or wherever bhikkhus depart. These are fifteen dwelling places.
Senāsanenā Senāsana
Senāsanakkhetta means "six fields: from the saṃgha or from a group or from relatives or from friends or with one's own wealth or with rags from a dust heap."
Senāsanakkhetta
Senāsanasantoso means fifteen kinds of contentment, such as contentment with thoughts in lodging.
These should be understood in the same way as stated in [the section on] alms food.

However, the five ascetic practices (dhutaṅga) associated with lodging are: dwelling in the forest (āraññikaṅga), dwelling at the foot of a tree (rukkhamūlikaṅga), dwelling in the open air (abbhokāsikaṅga), dwelling in a charnel ground (sosānikaṅga), using whatever lodging is assigned (yathāsantatikaṅga).
The detailed discussion of these is stated in the Visuddhimagga.
Thus, a bhikkhu fulfilling the Great Lineage of the Noble Ones with contentment in lodging protects these five dhutaṅgas.
Protecting these, he is content with the Great Lineage of the Noble Ones with contentment in lodging.

Senāsanasantoso Senāsanapaṭisaṃyuttāni
However, the requisites for the sick are included in alms food itself.
There, one should be content with contentment with whatever is obtained, whatever strength [one has], whatever is suitable.
The practice of sitting and not lying down (nesajjikaṅga) resorts to the Noble Lineage delighting in meditation.
And this was said:

"Five are stated regarding lodging, five depend on food;
One is joined with vigor, and two depend on robes."

Thus, Venerable Dhammasenāpati Sāriputta, like one spreading out the earth, like one filling the ocean's belly, like one expanding the sky, having first told of the Noble Lineage of contentment with robes, like one raising the moon, like one surpassing the sun, having told of the second contentment with alms food, like one lifting up Mount Sineru, having told of the third Noble Lineage of contentment with lodging, now, adorned with a thousand methods, to tell of the fourth Noble Lineage delighting in meditation, again, furthermore, āvusos, a bhikkhu is one who delights in abandoning, began the teaching.

Again, furthermore, āvusos, a bhikkhu is one who delights in abandoning
There, an object is delight, meaning delighting in.
He has delight in the fivefold abandoning, thus one who delights in abandoning.
He delights abandoning sensual desire, he delights cultivating renunciation, he delights abandoning ill-will…pe… he delights abandoning all defilements, he delights cultivating the path of arhatship, thus he delights in abandoning.
In the same way as stated, he has delight in meditation, thus one who delights in meditation.
He delights in meditation, thus one who delights in meditation.

Pahānārāmo. Pahānarato Bhāvanārāmo. Bhāvanārato.
However, in these four Noble Lineages, with the first three, the entire Vinaya Piṭaka is spoken of by way of the thirteen dhutaṅgas and contentment with the four supports.
With delighting in meditation [is spoken of] the remaining two Piṭakas.
However, this Noble Lineage of delighting in meditation should be told by a bhikkhu telling the Nekkhammapāḷi in the Paṭisambhidāmagga.
It should be told by way of the Dasuttarasuttantapariyāya in the Dīghanikāya.
It should be told by way of the Satipaṭṭhānasuttantapariyāya in the Majjhimanikāya.
It should be told by way of the Niddesapariyāya in the Abhidhamma.

There, in the Paṭisambhidāmagga in the Nekkhammapāḷi, he delights cultivating renunciation, he delights abandoning sensual desire.
Non-ill-will [abandons] ill-will.
Perception of light [abandons] dullness and drowsiness (thinamiddha).
Non-distraction [abandons] restlessness (uddhacca).
Definition of ideas [abandons] doubt (vicikicchā).
Knowledge [abandons] ignorance (avijjā).
Joy [abandons] discontent.
The first jhāna [absorption], the five hindrances (pañca nīvaraṇe).
The second jhāna, initial and sustained thought (vitakkavicāre).
The third jhāna, rapture (pītiṃ).
The fourth jhāna, pleasure and pain (sukhadukkhe).

Dwelling while cultivating the attainment of the sphere of infinite space (ākāsānañcāyatanasamāpattiṃ), he delights, abandoning the perception of form (rūpasaññaṃ), the perception of repulsion (paṭighasaññaṃ), the perception of diversity (nānattasaññaṃ).
Dwelling while cultivating the attainment of the sphere of infinite consciousness (viññāṇañcāyatanasamāpattiṃ)…pe… dwelling while cultivating the attainment of the sphere of neither perception nor non-perception (nevasaññānāsaññāyatanasamāpattiṃ), he delights, abandoning the perception of the sphere of nothingness (ākiñcaññāyatanasaññaṃ).

In the Paṭisambhidāmagga, in the chapter on Renunciation Dwelling while cultivating contemplation of impermanence (aniccānupassanaṃ), he delights, abandoning the perception of permanence (niccasaññaṃ).
Contemplation of suffering (dukkhānupassanaṃ), the perception of pleasure (sukhasaññaṃ).
Contemplation of not-self (anattānupassanaṃ), the perception of self (attasaññaṃ).
Contemplation of disenchantment (nibbidānupassanaṃ), delight (nandiṃ).
Contemplation of detachment (virāgānupassanaṃ), greed (rāgaṃ).
Contemplation of cessation (nirodhānupassanaṃ), origination (samudayaṃ).
Contemplation of relinquishment (paṭinissaggānupassanaṃ), grasping (ādānaṃ).
Contemplation of dissolution (khayānupassanaṃ), the perception of solidity (ghanasaññaṃ).
Contemplation of decline (vayānupassanaṃ), accumulation (āyūhanaṃ).
Contemplation of change (vipariṇāmānupassanaṃ), the perception of permanence (dhuvasaññaṃ).
Contemplation of the signless (animittānupassanaṃ), the sign (nimittaṃ).
Contemplation of the desireless (apaṇihitānupassanaṃ), desire (paṇidhiṃ).
Contemplation of emptiness (suññatānupassanaṃ) misinterpretation (abhinivesaṃ).
Insight into ideas that is higher understanding (adhipaññādhammavipassanaṃ), misinterpretation due to grasping at a core (sārādānābhinivesaṃ).
Correct knowledge and seeing (yathābhūtañāṇadassanaṃ), misinterpretation due to delusion (sammohābhinivesaṃ).
Contemplation of danger (ādīnavānupassanaṃ), misinterpretation due to attachment (ālayābhinivesaṃ).
Contemplation of reflexion (paṭisaṅkhānupassanaṃ), non-reflexion (appaṭisaṅkhaṃ).
Contemplation of turning away (vivaṭṭānupassanaṃ), misinterpretation due to clinging (saṃyogābhinivesaṃ).
The path of stream-entry (sotāpattimaggaṃ), defilements fixed on a view (diṭṭhekaṭṭhe kilese).
The path of once-returner (sakadāgāmimaggaṃ), gross defilements (oḷārike kilese).
The path of non-returner (anāgāmimaggaṃ), subtle defilements (aṇusahagate kilese).
Dwelling while cultivating the path of arahatship (arahattamaggaṃ), he delights, abandoning all defilements (sabbakilese pajahanto ramatīti evaṃ)—thus should it be told in the Paṭisambhidāmagga, in the chapter on Renunciation.
In the Paṭisambhidāmagga in the Dīgha Nikāyaby the Dasuttarasuttanta—dwelling while cultivating one idea, he delights, abandoning one idea, he delights…pe… dwelling while cultivating ten ideas, he delights, abandoning ten ideas, he delights.
What one idea does he delight cultivating? Mindfulness of the body (kāyagatāsatiṃ) accompanied by pleasure (sātasahagataṃ).
This one idea he delights cultivating.
What one idea does he delight abandoning? The conceit 'I am' (asmimānaṃ).
This one idea he delights abandoning.
What two ideas…pe… What ten ideas does he delight cultivating? The ten kasiṇa-spheres (dasa kasiṇāyatanāni).
These ten ideas he delights cultivating.
What are the ten qualities that, when abandoned, one finds delight? The ten wrong qualities.

These are the ten qualities that, when abandoned, one finds delight.

Thus, monks, the monk is one who delights in cultivation. In this way, it should be discussed according to the Dasuttara Sutta discourse series in the Dīgha Nikāya.

Dīgha Nikāya

Dasuttara Sutta Discourse Series

Majjhima Nikāya, Satipaṭṭhāna Sutta Discourse Series: Monks, this is the one way, for the purification of beings, for the overcoming of sorrow and lamentation, for the cessation of suffering and displeasure, for the attainment of the right path, for the realization of nirvāṇa, namely, the four smṛti-upasthānas (foundations of mindfulness).

What are the four? Here, monks, a monk dwells contemplating the body as a body…contemplating feelings as feelings…contemplating the mind as the mind…contemplating dharmas as dharmas. Or his mindfulness is present to the extent necessary for knowledge and remembrance, and he dwells independent, and does not cling to anything in the world.

Even so, monks, the monk is one who delights in cultivation, one who rejoices in cultivation, one who delights in abandonment, one who rejoices in abandonment.

And furthermore, monks, a monk, while walking, knows, "I am walking"…pe… And furthermore, monks, a monk might see a corpse cast away in a charnel ground…pe…rotten, crumbled to dust.

He compares this very body with that, thinking: "This body too is of the same nature, it will become like that, it is not exempt from that fate."

Thus, either inwardly, he dwells contemplating the body as a body…pe… Even so, monks, the monk is one who delights in cultivation. In this way, it should be discussed according to the Satipaṭṭhāna Sutta discourse series in the Majjhima Nikāya.

Majjhima Nikāya, Satipaṭṭhāna Sutta Discourse Series

Abhidhamma, Niddesa Discourse Series: Seeing all conditioned things as impermanent, as suffering, as disease, as a tumor…pe…he delights in viewing the saṃkilesika-dharmas.

This monk, monks, is one who delights in cultivation. In this way, it should be discussed according to the Niddesa discourse series.

Abhidhamma, Niddesa Discourse Series

Never exalting himself: "Today I am sixty or seventy years old and am doing the work of insight (vipaśyanā) on impermanence, suffering, and no-self. Who is like me?" Thus, he does not exalt himself.

Not disparaging others: "There is not even the slightest insight into impermanence and suffering! What are these people doing wandering around with scattered objects of meditation?" Thus, he does not disparage others.

The rest is as already stated.

exalting himself, not disparaging others.
