310. ‘‘Cattāri padhānāni. Saṃvarapadhānaṃ pahānapadhānaṃ bhāvanāpadhānaṃ bhāvanāppadhānaṃ (syā.) anurakkhaṇāpadhānaṃ anurakkhanāppadhānaṃ (syā.). Katamañcāvuso, saṃvarapadhānaṃ? Idhāvuso, bhikkhu cakkhunā rūpaṃ disvā na nimittaggāhī hoti nānubyañjanaggāhī. Yatvādhikaraṇamenaṃ cakkhundriyaṃ asaṃvutaṃ viharantaṃ abhijjhādomanassā pāpakā akusalā dhammā anvāssaveyyuṃ, tassa saṃvarāya paṭipajjati, rakkhati cakkhundriyaṃ, cakkhundriye saṃvaraṃ āpajjati. Sotena saddaṃ sutvā… ghānena gandhaṃ ghāyitvā… jivhāya rasaṃ sāyitvā… kāyena phoṭṭhabbaṃ phusitvā… manasā dhammaṃ viññāya na nimittaggāhī hoti nānubyañjanaggāhī. Yatvādhikaraṇamenaṃ manindriyaṃ asaṃvutaṃ viharantaṃ abhijjhādomanassā pāpakā akusalā dhammā anvāssaveyyuṃ, tassa saṃvarāya paṭipajjati, rakkhati manindriyaṃ, manindriye saṃvaraṃ āpajjati. Idaṃ vuccatāvuso, saṃvarapadhānaṃ. ‘‘Katamañcāvuso, pahānapadhānaṃ? Idhāvuso, bhikkhu uppannaṃ kāmavitakkaṃ nādhivāseti pajahati vinodeti byantiṃ karoti byantī karoti (syā. kaṃ.) anabhāvaṃ gameti. Uppannaṃ byāpādavitakkaṃ…pe… uppannaṃ vihiṃsāvitakkaṃ… uppannuppanne pāpake akusale dhamme nādhivāseti pajahati vinodeti byantiṃ karoti anabhāvaṃ gameti. Idaṃ vuccatāvuso, pahānapadhānaṃ. byantī karoti (syā. kaṃ.) ‘‘Katamañcāvuso , bhāvanāpadhānaṃ? Idhāvuso, bhikkhu satisambojjhaṅgaṃ bhāveti vivekanissitaṃ virāganissitaṃ nirodhanissitaṃ vossaggapariṇāmiṃ. Dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgaṃ bhāveti… vīriyasambojjhaṅgaṃ bhāveti… pītisambojjhaṅgaṃ bhāveti… passaddhisambojjhaṅgaṃ bhāveti… samādhisambojjhaṅgaṃ bhāveti… upekkhāsambojjhaṅgaṃ bhāveti vivekanissitaṃ virāganissitaṃ nirodhanissitaṃ vossaggapariṇāmiṃ. Idaṃ vuccatāvuso, bhāvanāpadhānaṃ. ‘‘Katamañcāvuso, anurakkhaṇāpadhānaṃ? Idhāvuso, bhikkhu uppannaṃ bhadrakaṃ bhaddakaṃ (syā. kaṃ. pī.) samādhinimittaṃ anurakkhati – aṭṭhikasaññaṃ, puḷuvakasaññaṃ puḷavakasaññaṃ (sī. pī.), vinīlakasaññaṃ, vicchiddakasaññaṃ, uddhumātakasaññaṃ. 310. "Four exertions (pradhana): the exertion of restraint (samvarapradhana), the exertion of abandonment (pahanapradhana), the exertion of development (bhavanapradhana), and the exertion of maintaining (anurakkhanapradhana).

And what, venerable friends, is the exertion of restraint? Here, venerable friends, a bhikshu on seeing a form with the eye, does not grasp at its major signs or secondary characteristics. Since, if he left the eye faculty unrestrained, evil, unwholesome qualities such as greed or distress might assail him, he practices restraint over it, he guards the eye faculty, he undertakes the restraint of the eye faculty. Having heard a sound with the ear… Having smelled an odor with the nose … Having tasted a taste with the tongue … Having felt a tactile object with the body … Having cognized a mental phenomenon with the mind, he does not grasp its marks and features. Since, if he left the mind faculty unrestrained, bad unwholesome states of longing and dejection might invade him, he practices restraint over it; he guards the mind faculty, he undertakes the restraint of the mind faculty. This is called the exertion to guard.

“And what, venerable friends, is the exertion to abandon? Here, venerable friends, a bhikshu does not tolerate an arisen sensual thought; he abandons it, dispels it, terminates it, and obliterates it. He does not tolerate an arisen thought of ill will… an arisen thought of harming . .. bad unwholesome states whenever they arise; he abandons them, dispels them , terminates them, and obliterates them . This is called the exertion to abandon.

“And what, venerable friends, is the exertion to develop? Here, a bhikshu develops the mindfulness enlightenment factor (sati-sambojjhanga), which is based upon seclusion (viveka-nissita), dispassion (viraga-nissita), and cessation (nirodha-nissita), maturing in release (vossagga-parinami). He develops the investigation-of-states enlightenment factor (dhamma-vicaya-sambojjhanga) .. the energy enlightenment factor (viriya-sambojjhanga) .. the rapture enlightenment factor (piti-sambojjhanga). .. the tranquility enlightenment factor (passaddhi-sambojjhanga). .. the concentration enlightenment factor (samadhi-sambojjhanga). .. the equanimity enlightenment factor (upekkha-sambojjhanga), which is based upon seclusion, dispassion, and cessation, maturing in release. This is called the exertion to develop.

“And what, venerable friends, is the exertion to maintain? Here, a bhikshu protects an arisen excellent object of concentration: the perception of a skeleton (atthika-sañña), the perception of a worm-infested corpse (puluvaka-sañña), the perception of a livid corpse (vinilaka-sañña), the perception of a festering corpse (vicchiddaka-sañña), the perception of a fissured corpse, the perception of a bloated corpse (uddhumataka-sañña). This is called the exertion to maintain.

“Four kinds of knowledge (jnana): knowledge of the dharma (dharme jnana), knowledge of inference (anvaye jnana), knowledge of…
), one should consult the commentary on paricchede (in Syā, Pī, Ka); knowledge, conventional knowledge.

Knowledges – one should consult the commentary on paricce (in Sī, Ka), paricchede (in Syā, Pī, Ka)

‘‘Furthermore, there are these four knowledges: knowledge of suffering (duḥkha), knowledge of the origin of suffering (duḥkhasamudaya), knowledge of the cessation of suffering (duḥkhanirodha), and knowledge of the path leading to the cessation of suffering (duḥkhanirodhagāminī pratipad).

Anvaye ñāṇanti cattāri saccāni paccakkhato disvā yathā idāni, evaṃ atītepi anāgatepi imeva pañcakkhandhā dukkhasaccaṃ, ayameva taṇhā samudayasaccaṃ, ayameva nirodho nirodhasaccaṃ, ayameva maggo maggasaccanti evaṃ tassa ñāṇassa anugatiyaṃ ñāṇaṃ. Tenāha – ‘‘so iminā dhammena ñātena diṭṭhena pattena viditena pariyogāḷhena atītānāgatena nayaṃ netī’’ti. Pariye ñāṇanti paresaṃ cittaparicchede ñāṇaṃ. Yathāha – ‘‘tattha katamaṃ pariye ñāṇaṃ? Idha bhikkhu parasattānaṃ parapuggalānaṃ cetasā ceto paricca jānātī’’ti (vibha. 796) vitthāretabbaṃ. Ṭhapetvā pana imāni tīṇi ñāṇāni avasesaṃ sammutiñāṇaṃ nāma. Yathāha – ‘‘tattha katamaṃ sammutiñāṇaṃ? Ṭhapetvā dhamme ñāṇaṃ ṭhapetvā anvaye ñāṇaṃ ṭhapetvā paricchede ñāṇaṃ avasesaṃ sammutiñāṇa’’nti (vibha. 796). Dhamme ñāṇa Anvaye ñāṇa Pariye ñāṇa sammutiñāṇaṃ Dukkhe ñāṇādīhi arahattaṃ pāpetvā ekassa bhikkhuno niggamanaṃ catusaccakammaṭṭhānaṃ kathitaṃ. Tattha dve saccāni vaṭṭaṃ, dve vivaṭṭaṃ, vaṭṭe abhiniveso hoti, no vivaṭṭe. Dvīsu saccesu ācariyasantike pariyattiṃ uggahetvā kammaṃ karoti, dvīsu saccesu ‘‘nirodhasaccaṃ nāma iṭṭhaṃ kantaṃ manāpaṃ, maggasaccaṃ nāma iṭṭhaṃ kantaṃ manāpa’’nti savanavasena kammaṃ karoti. Dvīsu saccesu uggahaparipucchāsavanadhāraṇasammasanapaṭivedho vaṭṭati, dvīsu savanapaṭivedho vaṭṭati. Tīṇi kiccavasena paṭivijjhati, ekaṃ ārammaṇavasena. Dve saccāni duddasattā gambhīrāni, dve gambhīrattā duddasāni. Dukkhe ñāṇā Sotāpattiyaṅgādicatukkavaṇṇanā
310. Padhānāni means uttamavīriyāni, highest effort.

Saṃvarapadhāna means the effort that arises in one who restrains the sense faculties beginning with the eye (cakṣus).

Pahānapadhāna means the effort that arises in one who abandons thoughts of sensual pleasure (kāma), etc.

Bhāvanāpadhāna means the effort that arises in one who cultivates the factors of enlightenment (bojjhaṅga).

Anurakkhaṇāpadhāna means the effort that arises in one who protects the object of concentration (samādhi-nimitta).

Vivekanissita and so on: viveka (discrimination), virāga (detachment), and nirodha (cessation) are three names for nirvāṇa.

Indeed, nirvāṇa is viveka because it is the discrimination of the upadhi (defilements).

Because upon approaching it, passions and the like become detached, it is called virāga, detachment.

Because [defilements] are suppressed, it is called nirodha, cessation.

Therefore, in vivekanissita, etc., the meaning is dependent on nirvāṇa either by way of object or by way of what is to be attained.

Vossaggapariṇāmi means here two kinds of abandonment (vossaggā): abandonment by relinquishment (pariccāgavossaggo) and abandonment by expulsion (pakkhandanavossaggo).

There, vipassanā (insight) by way of its factors, relinquishes defilements (kilesa) and aggregates (khandha); therefore it is abandonment by relinquishment (pariccāgavossaggo).

The path (magga) by way of its object, expels towards nirvāṇa; therefore it is abandonment by expulsion (pakkhandanavossaggo).

Therefore, vossaggapariṇāmin means just as the mindfulness factor of enlightenment (satisambojjhaṅga), being cultivated, develops for the purpose of abandonment, attains the nature of insight (vipassanābhāva) and the nature of the path (maggabhāva), thus one cultivates it; this is the meaning here.

The same method of explanation applies to the other clauses too.

Vivekanissita. Viveko.

Virāgo.

Nirodho.

Vossaggapariṇāmi

Bhadraka means auspicious.

Samādhinimittaṃ is said to be the concentration itself that is attained by way of the skeleton meditation (aṭṭhikasaññā), etc.

Anurakkhati means protecting by purifying the dharma (qualities) that obstruct samādhi, such as greed, hatred, and delusion.

Here, only five perceptions beginning with the skeleton meditation (aṭṭhikasaññā) are mentioned.

However, in this place, even ten impurities (asubha) should be explained in detail.

Their detailed explanation is indeed given in the Visuddhimagga.

Bhadraka Samādhinimittaṃ Anurakkhati

Dhamme ñāṇa means knowledge of the four noble truths (catusaccadhamma) by way of a single penetration, and knowledge of the dharma (qualities) in the truth of cessation (nirodhasacce) within the four noble truths.

As it was said: “What there is knowledge of the dharma? Knowledge in the four paths (magga) and knowledge in the four fruits (phala)” (vibha. 796).

Anvaye ñāṇa means having seen the four truths directly, just as now, so also in the past and future, these five aggregates (pañcakkhandha) are the truth of suffering (dukkhasacca), this craving (taṇhā) is the truth of origin (samudayasacca), this cessation (nirodha) is the truth of cessation (nirodhasacca), this path (magga) is the truth of the path (maggasacca); thus, knowledge in accordance with that knowledge.

Therefore it was said: “He, with this dharma (quality) known, seen, attained, understood, penetrated, leads the conduct of the past and future.”

Pariye ñāṇa means knowledge in the discernment of the minds of others.

As it was said: “What there is knowledge of others' minds? Here, a bhikkhu (monk) discerns with his mind the mind of other beings, other individuals.” (vibha. 796) To be explained in detail.

Having set aside these three knowledges, the remainder is called conventional knowledge (sammutiñāṇaṃ).

As it was said: “What there is conventional knowledge (sammutiñāṇaṃ)? Having set aside knowledge of the dharma, having set aside knowledge in accordance, having set aside knowledge in discernment, the remainder is conventional knowledge (sammutiñāṇaṃ)” (vibha. 796).
dhamme ñāṇa, anvaye ñāṇa, pariye ñāṇa, sammutiñāṇaṃ.

Having brought a monk to Arhatship by means of knowledge of suffering (dukkhe ñāṇa) etc., the going forth of a single monk is said to be the practice of the Four Truths ( catusaccakammaṭṭhānaṃ).

Therein, two truths are cyclical (vaṭṭaṃ), two are non-cyclical (vivaṭṭaṃ); there is attachment to the cyclical, but not to the non-cyclical.

In two truths, having grasped the teachings from a teacher, one does the practice; in two truths, one does the practice by way of hearing, thinking, "Cessation (nirodhasaccaṃ) is desirable, lovely, and pleasing, the path (maggasaccaṃ) is desirable, lovely, and pleasing."

In relation to two truths, learning, questioning, hearing, retaining, pondering, and penetrating occur; in relation to two, only hearing and penetrating occur.

One penetrates three by way of function (kiccavasena), and one by way of object (ārammaṇavasena).

Two truths are difficult to see and profound (duddasattā gambhīrāni), two are difficult to see because of their profundity (gambhīrattā duddasāni).

Knowledge of suffering (dukkhe ñāṇā).

Explanation of the four factors beginning with the limbs of stream-entry (sotāpattiyaṅgādicatukkavaṇṇanā).

311. ‘‘Cattāri sotāpattiyaṅgāni – sappurisasaṃsevo, saddhammassavanaṃ, yonisomanasikāro, dhammānudhammappaṭipatti. ‘‘Cattāri sotāpannassa aṅgāni. Idhāvuso, ariyasāvako buddhe aveccappasādena samannāgato hoti – ‘itipi so bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho vijjācaraṇasampanno sugato lokavidū anuttaro purisadammasārathi satthā devamanussānaṃ buddho, bhagavā’ti. Dhamme aveccappasādena samannāgato hoti – ‘svākkhāto bhagavatā dhammo sandiṭṭhiko akāliko ehipassiko opaneyyiko opanayiko (syā. kaṃ.) paccattaṃ veditabbo viññūhī’ti. Saṅghe aveccappasādena samannāgato hoti – ‘suppaṭipanno bhagavato sāvakasaṅgho ujuppaṭipanno bhagavato sāvakasaṅgho ñāyappaṭipanno bhagavato sāvakasaṅgho sāmīcippaṭipanno bhagavato sāvakasaṅgho yadidaṃ cattāri purisayugāni aṭṭha purisapuggalā, esa bhagavato sāvakasaṅgho āhuneyyo pāhuneyyo dakkhiṇeyyo añjalikaraṇīyo anuttaraṃ puññakkhettaṃ lokassā’ti. Ariyakantehi sīlehi samannāgato hoti akhaṇḍehi acchiddehi asabalehi akammāsehi bhujissehi viññuppasatthehi aparāmaṭṭhehi samādhisaṃvattanikehi. sotāpannassa aṅgāni. opanayiko (syā. kaṃ.) ‘‘Cattāri sāmaññaphalāni – sotāpattiphalaṃ, sakadāgāmiphalaṃ, anāgāmiphalaṃ, arahattaphalaṃ. sāmaññaphalāni – ‘‘Catasso dhātuyo – pathavīdhātu, āpodhātu, tejodhātu, vāyodhātu. dhātuyo – ‘‘Cattāro āhārā – kabaḷīkāro āhāro oḷāriko vā sukhumo vā, phasso dutiyo, manosañcetanā tatiyā, viññāṇaṃ catutthaṃ. āhārā – ‘‘Catasso viññāṇaṭṭhitiyo. Rūpūpāyaṃ vā, āvuso, viññāṇaṃ tiṭṭhamānaṃ tiṭṭhati rūpārammaṇaṃ rūpāramaṇaṃ (?) rūpappatiṭṭhaṃ nandūpasecanaṃ vuddhiṃ virūḷhiṃ vepullaṃ āpajjati; vedanūpāyaṃ vā āvuso…pe… saññūpāyaṃ vā, āvuso…pe… saṅkhārūpāyaṃ vā, āvuso, viññāṇaṃ tiṭṭhamānaṃ tiṭṭhati saṅkhārārammaṇaṃ saṅkhārappatiṭṭhaṃ nandūpasecanaṃ vuddhiṃ virūḷhiṃ vepullaṃ āpajjati. viññāṇaṭṭhitiyo. rūpāramaṇaṃ (?) ‘‘Cattāri agatigamanāni – chandāgatiṃ gacchati, dosāgati gacchati, mohāgatiṃ gacchati, bhayāgatiṃ gacchati. agatigamanāni – ‘‘Cattāro taṇhuppādā – cīvarahetu vā, āvuso, bhikkhuno taṇhā uppajjamānā uppajjati; piṇḍapātahetu vā, āvuso, bhikkhuno taṇhā uppajjamānā uppajjati; senāsanahetu vā, āvuso, bhikkhuno taṇhā uppajjamānā uppajjati; itibhavābhavahetu vā, āvuso, bhikkhuno taṇhā uppajjamānā uppajjati. taṇhuppādā – ‘‘Catasso paṭipadā – dukkhā paṭipadā dandhābhiññā, dukkhā paṭipadā khippābhiññā, sukhā paṭipadā dandhābhiññā, sukhā paṭipadā khippābhiññā. paṭipadā – ‘‘Aparāpi catasso paṭipadā – akkhamā paṭipadā, khamā paṭipadā, damā paṭipadā, samā paṭipadā. paṭipadā – ‘‘Cattāri dhammapadāni – anabhijjhā dhammapadaṃ, abyāpādo dhammapadaṃ, sammāsati dhammapadaṃ, sammāsamādhi dhammapadaṃ. dhammapadāni – ‘‘Cattāri dhammasamādānāni – atthāvuso, dhammasamādānaṃ paccuppannadukkhañceva āyatiñca dukkhavipākaṃ. Atthāvuso, dhammasamādānaṃ paccuppannadukkhaṃ āyatiṃ sukhavipākaṃ. Atthāvuso, dhammasamādānaṃ paccuppannasukhaṃ āyatiṃ dukkhavipākaṃ. Atthāvuso, dhammasamādānaṃ paccuppannasukhañceva āyatiñca sukhavipākaṃ. dhammasamādānāni – ‘‘Cattāro dhammakkhandhā – sīlakkhandho, samādhikkhandho, paññākkhandho, vimuttikkhandho. dhammakkhandhā – ‘‘Cattāri balāni – vīriyabalaṃ, satibalaṃ, samādhibalaṃ, paññābalaṃ. balāni – ‘‘Cattāri adhiṭṭhānāni – paññādhiṭṭhānaṃ, saccādhiṭṭhānaṃ, cāgādhiṭṭhānaṃ, upasamādhiṭṭhānaṃ. adhiṭṭhānāni –
311. “There are four factors of stream-entry (sotāpattiyaṅgāni): associating with good people (sappurisasaṃsevo), listening to the true Dharma (saddhammassavanaṃ), wise reflection (yonisomanasikāro), and practicing the Dharma in accordance with the Dharma (dhammānudhammappaṭipatti).

“There are four qualities of a stream-enterer (sotāpannassa aṅgāni). Here, a noble disciple is endowed with verified confidence in the Buddha: ‘Indeed, the Blessed One is worthy and rightly self-awakened, consummate in knowledge & conduct, well-gone, an expert with regard to the world, unexcelled as a trainer for those people fit to be tamed, the Teacher of divine & human beings, awakened, blessed.’

He is endowed with verified confidence in the Dhamma: ‘The Dhamma is well taught by the Blessed One, to be seen here & now, timeless, inviting verification, pertinent, to be experienced by the observant for themselves.’

He is endowed with verified confidence in the Saṅgha: ‘The Saṅgha of the Blessed One’s disciples who have practiced well… who have practiced straight-forwardly… who have practiced methodically… who have practiced masterfully—in other words, the four types of noble disciples when taken as pairs, the eight when taken as individual types—they are the Saṅgha of the Blessed One’s disciples: worthy of gifts, worthy of hospitality, worthy of offerings, worthy of respect, the incomparable field of merit for the world.’

He is endowed with virtues that are appealing to the noble ones: untorn, unbroken, unspotted, unsplattered, liberating, praised by the wise, untarnished, leading to concentration.

Qualities of a stream-enterer (sotāpannassa aṅgāni).

“There are four fruits of the contemplative life (sāmaññaphalāni): the fruit of stream-entry (sotāpattiphalaṃ), the fruit of once-returning (sakadāgāmiphalaṃ), the fruit of non-returning (anāgāmiphalaṃ), the fruit of arahantship (arahattaphalaṃ).

Fruits of the contemplative life (sāmaññaphalāni).

“There are four elements (dhātuyo): the earth element (pathavīdhātu), the water element (āpodhātu), the fire element (tejodhātu), and the air element (vāyodhātu).

Elements (dhātuyo).

“There are four nutriments (āhārā): physical food (kabaḷīkāro āhāro), gross or refined; contact (phasso) as the second; intellectual intention (manosañcetanā) the third; and consciousness (viññāṇaṃ) the fourth.

Nutriments (āhārā).

“There are four supports of consciousness (viññāṇaṭṭhitiyo). Consciousness, when standing, stands attached to form (rūpa), supported by form , landing on form, watered with delight, and exhibits growth, increase, & proliferation. When standing, it stands attached to feeling… to perception… to fabrications, supported by fabrications, landing on fabrications, watered with delight, and exhibits growth, increase, & proliferation.

Supports of consciousness (viññāṇaṭṭhitiyo).

“There are four ways of going off course (agatigamanāni): One goes off course through desire (chandāgatiṃ gacchati). One goes off course through aversion (dosāgati gacchati). One goes off course through delusion (mohāgatiṃ gacchati). One goes off course through fear (bhayāgatiṃ gacchati).

Ways of going off course (agatigamanāni).

“There are four origins of craving (taṇhuppādā): Craving arises in a bhikkhu because of robes; craving arises in a bhikkhu because of alms food; craving arises in a bhikkhu because of lodging; craving arises in a bhikkhu because of this-being or not-being.

Origins of craving (taṇhuppādā).

“There are four modes of practice (paṭipadā): painful practice with slow intuition (dukkhā paṭipadā dandhābhiññā), painful practice with quick intuition (dukkhā paṭipadā khippābhiññā), pleasant practice with slow intuition (sukhā paṭipadā dandhābhiññā), and pleasant practice with quick intuition (sukhā paṭipadā khippābhiññā).

Modes of practice (paṭipadā).

“There are also four other modes of practice (paṭipadā): intolerant practice, tolerant practice, taming practice, and calming practice.

Modes of practice (paṭipadā).

“There are four foundations of Dharma (dhammapadāni): non-covetousness, non-ill will, right mindfulness, right concentration.

Foundations of Dharma (dhammapadāni).

“There are four ways of undertaking things (dhammasamādānāni): There is the taking on of a practice that is painful in the present and results in pain in the future. There is the taking on of a practice that is painful in the present but results in pleasure in the future. There is the taking on of a practice that is pleasant in the present and results in pain in the future.
Friend, there is the undertaking of a practice (dhammasamādānaṃ) that is pleasant in the present (paccuppannasukhañceva) and results in pleasure in the future (āyatiñca sukhavipākaṃ).
311.Sotāpattiyaṅgānīti sotāpattiyā aṅgāni, sotāpattimaggassa paṭilābhakāraṇānīti attho. Sappurisasaṃsevoti buddhādīnaṃ sappurisānaṃ upasaṅkamitvā sevanaṃ. Saddhammassavananti sappāyassa tepiṭakadhammassa savanaṃ. Yonisomanasikāroti aniccādivasena manasikāro. Dhammānudhammappaṭipattīti lokuttaradhammassa anudhammabhūtāya pubbabhāgapaṭipattiyā paṭipajjanaṃ. Aveccappasādenāti acalappasādena. ‘‘Itipi so bhagavā’’tiādīni visuddhimagge vitthāritāni. Phaladhātuāhāracatukkāni uttānatthāneva. Apicettha lūkhapaṇītavatthuvasena oḷārikasukhumatā veditabbā. Aveccappasādenā Viññāṇaṭṭhitiyoti viññāṇaṃ etāsu tiṭṭhatīti viññāṇaṭṭhitiyo. Ārammaṇaṭṭhitivasenetaṃ vuttaṃ. Rūpūpāyanti rūpaṃ upagataṃ hutvā. Pañcavokārabhavasmiñhi abhisaṅkhāraviññāṇaṃ rūpakkhandhaṃ nissāya tiṭṭhati. Taṃ sandhāyetaṃ vuttaṃ. Rūpārammaṇanti rūpakkhandhagocaraṃ rūpapatiṭṭhitaṃ hutvā. Nandūpasecananti lobhasahagataṃ sampayuttanandiyāva upasittaṃ hutvā. Itaraṃ upanissayakoṭiyā. Vuddhiṃ virūḷhiṃ vepullaṃ āpajjatīti saṭṭhipi sattatipi vassāni evaṃ pavattamānaṃ vuddhiṃ virūḷhiṃ vepullaṃ āpajjati. Vedanūpāyādīsupi eseva nayo. Imehi pana tīhi padehi catuvokārabhave abhisaṅkhāraviññāṇaṃ vuttaṃ. Tassa yāvatāyukaṃ pavattanavasena vuddhiṃ virūḷhiṃ vepullaṃ āpajjanā veditabbā. Catukkavasena pana desanāya āgatattā viññāṇūpāyanti na vuttaṃ. Evaṃ vuccamāne ca ‘‘katamaṃ nu kho ettha kammaviññāṇaṃ, katamaṃ vipākaviññāṇa’’nti sammoho bhaveyya, tasmāpi na vuttaṃ. Agatigamanāni vitthāritāneva. Viññāṇaṭṭhitiyo Rūpūpāya Rūpārammaṇa Nandūpasecana Vuddhiṃ virūḷhiṃ vepullaṃ āpajjatī Vedanūpāyādīsupi Agatigamanāni Cīvarahetūti tattha manāpaṃ cīvaraṃ labhissāmīti cīvarakāraṇā uppajjati. Iti bhavābhavahetūti ettha itīti nidassanatthe nipāto. Yathā cīvarādihetu, evaṃ bhavābhavahetūpīti attho. Bhavābhavoti cettha paṇītapaṇītatarāni telamadhuphāṇitādīni adhippetāni. Imesaṃ pana catunnaṃ taṇhuppādānaṃ pahānatthāya paṭipāṭiyāva cattāro ariyavaṃsā desitāti veditabbā. Paṭipadācatukkaṃ heṭṭhā vuttameva. Akkhamādīsu padhānakaraṇakāle sītādīni na khamatīti akkhamā. Khamatīti khamā. Indriyadamanaṃ damā. ‘‘Uppannaṃ kāmavitakkaṃ nādhivāsetī’’tiādinā nayena vitakkasamanaṃ samā. Cīvarahetū Iti bhavābhavahetū itī Bhavābhavo Paṭipadācatukkaṃ akkhamā. khamā. damā. samā. Dhammapadānīti dhammakoṭṭhāsāni. Anabhijjhā dhammapadaṃ nāma alobho vā alobhasīsena adhigatajjhānavipassanāmaggaphalanibbānāni vā. Abyāpādo dhammapadaṃ nāma akopo vā mettāsīsena adhigatajjhānādīni vā. Sammāsati dhammapadaṃ nāma suppaṭṭhitasati vā satisīsena adhigatajjhānādīni vā. Sammāsamādhi dhammapadaṃ nāma samāpatti vā aṭṭhasamāpattivasena adhigatajjhānavipassanāmaggaphalanibbānāni vā. Dasāsubhavasena vā adhigatajjhānādīni anabhijjhā dhammapadaṃ. Catubrahmavihāravasena adhigatāni abyāpādo dhammapadaṃ. Dasānussatiāhārepaṭikūlasaññāvasena adhigatāni sammāsati dhammapadaṃ. Dasakasiṇaānāpānavasena adhigatāni sammāsamādhi dhammapadanti. Dhammapadānī Dhammasamādānesu paṭhamaṃ acelakapaṭipadā. Dutiyaṃ tibbakilesassa arahattaṃ gahetuṃ asakkontassa assumukhassāpi rudato parisuddhabrahmacariyacaraṇaṃ. Tatiyaṃ kāmesu pātabyatā. Catutthaṃ cattāro paccaye alabhamānassāpi jhānavipassanāvasena sukhasamaṅgino sāsanabrahmacariyaṃ. Dhammakkhandhāti ettha guṇaṭṭho khandhaṭṭho. Sīlakkhandhoti sīlaguṇo. Ettha ca phalasīlaṃ adhippetaṃ. Sesapadesupi eseva nayo. Iti catūsupi ṭhānesu phalameva vuttaṃ. Dhammakkhandhā Sīlakkhandho Balānīti upatthambhanaṭṭhena akampiyaṭṭhena ca balāni. Tesaṃ paṭipakkhehi kosajjādīhi akampaniyatā veditabbā. Sabbānipi samathavipassanāmaggavasena lokiyalokuttarāneva kathitāni. Balānī Adhiṭṭhānānīti ettha adhīti upasaggamattaṃ. Atthato pana tena vā tiṭṭhanti, tattha vā tiṭṭhanti, ṭhānameva vā taṃtaṃguṇādhikānaṃ purisānaṃ adhiṭṭhānaṃ, paññāva adhiṭṭhānaṃ paññādhiṭṭhānaṃ. Ettha ca paṭhamena aggaphalapaññā. Dutiyena vacīsaccaṃ. Tatiyena āmisapariccāgo. Catutthena kilesūpasamo kathitoti veditabbo. Paṭhamena ca kammassakatapaññaṃ vipassanāpaññaṃ vā ādiṃ katvā phalapaññā kathitā. Dutiyena vacīsaccaṃ ādiṃ katvā paramatthasaccaṃ nibbānaṃ. Tatiyena āmisapariccāgaṃ ādiṃ katvā aggamaggena kilesapariccāgo. Catutthena samāpattivikkhambhite kilese ādiṃ katvā aggamaggena kilesavūpasamo. Paññādhiṭṭhānena vā ekena arahattaphalapaññā kathitā. Sesehi paramatthasaccaṃ. Saccādhiṭṭhānena vā ekena paramatthasaccaṃ kathitaṃ. Sesehi arahattapaññāti mūsikābhayatthero āha. Adhiṭṭhānānī adhī paññādhiṭṭhānaṃ. mūsikābhayatthero Pañhabyākaraṇādicatukkavaṇṇanā
311. sotāpattiyaṅgānī: the factors of stream-entry, that is, factors of stream-entry, are causes for obtaining the path of stream-entry— such is the meaning.

sappurisasaṃsevo: associating with good people, that is, approaching and attending upon good people such as the Buddha and others.

saddhammassavana: hearing the true Dharma, that is, hearing the Dharma of the Tipiṭaka that is conducive [to liberation].

yonisomanasikāro: proper attention, that is, attention in terms of impermanence and so forth.

dhammānudhammappaṭipattī: practicing the Dharma in accordance with the Dharma, that is, practicing in accordance with the preliminary practice that is in accordance with the transcendental Dharma.

aveccappasādenā: unwavering confidence, acalappasādena: and immovable confidence.

‘‘itipi so bhagavā’’ tiādīni visuddhimagge vitthāritāni: ‘‘Thus indeed, the Blessed One…’’ and so on, is extensively explained in the Path of Purification (Visuddhimagga).

phaladhātuāhāracatukkāni uttānatthāneva: the four factors of fruit, element, and nutriment are easy to understand.

apicettha lūkhapaṇītavatthuvasena oḷārikasukhumatā veditabbā: Moreover, here, coarseness and refinement should be understood in terms of coarse and fine objects.

aveccappasādenā, viññāṇaṭṭhitiyo: stations of consciousness, viññāṇaṃ etāsu tiṭṭhatīti viññāṇaṭṭhitiyo: that is, because consciousness abides in these, they are stations of consciousness.

ārammaṇaṭṭhitivasenetaṃ vuttaṃ: This was said in terms of the abiding of an object.

rūpūpāya: approaching form, rūpaṃ upagataṃ hutvā: that is, having approached form.

pañcavokārabhavasmiñhi abhisaṅkhāraviññāṇaṃ rūpakkhandhaṃ nissāya tiṭṭhati: In the realm of five aggregates, volitional consciousness depends on the aggregate of form and abides.

taṃ sandhāyetaṃ vuttaṃ: This was said with reference to that.

rūpārammaṇa: form as object, rūpakkhandhagocaraṃ rūpapatiṭṭhitaṃ hutvā: that is, having form as its range, having form as its basis.

nandūpasecana: delight in nourishment, lobhasahagataṃ sampayuttanandiyāva upasittaṃ hutvā: that is, having delighted and associated with covetousness, it is sprinkled, so to speak.

itaraṃ upanissayakoṭiyā: The other [condition] is by way of decisive support.

vuddhiṃ virūḷhiṃ vepullaṃ āpajjatī: it attains growth, increase, and abundance, that is, even if it is happening for sixty or seventy years, it attains growth, increase, and abundance.

vedanūpāyādīsupi eseva nayo: The same method applies to feeling, approaching, and so forth.

imehi pana tīhi padehi catuvokārabhave abhisaṅkhāraviññāṇaṃ vuttaṃ: However, in the realm of four aggregates, volitional consciousness is described by these three terms.

tassa yāvatāyukaṃ pavattanavasena vuddhiṃ virūḷhiṃ vepullaṃ āpajjanā veditabbā: Its attainment of growth, increase, and abundance should be understood in terms of its continuation as long as life lasts.

catukkavasena pana desanāya āgatattā viññāṇūpāyanti na vuttaṃ: However, because the teaching comes in terms of a tetrad, it is not said "consciousness approaching."

evaṃ vuccamāne ca ‘‘katamaṃ nu kho ettha kammaviññāṇaṃ, katamaṃ vipākaviññāṇa’’nti sammoho bhaveyya, tasmāpi na vuttaṃ: And if it were said thus, there would be confusion as to ‘‘which here is karmic consciousness, which is resultant consciousness,’’ therefore it was not said.

agatigamanāni vitthāritāneva: The ways of not going [to the four destinations] are explained in detail.

viññāṇaṭṭhitiyo, rūpūpāya, rūpārammaṇa, nandūpasecana, vuddhiṃ virūḷhiṃ vepullaṃ āpajjatī, vedanūpāyādīsupi agatigamanāni: stations of consciousness, approaching form, form as object, delight in nourishment, it attains growth, increase, and abundance, the same method applies to feeling, approaching, and so forth, the ways of not going [to the four destinations].

cīvarahetū: the cause of robes, that is, there the thought arises, “I will obtain a desirable robe,” because of the cause of a robe.

iti bhavābhavahetū: thus, the causes of existence and non-existence, that is, here, itī: thus, is a particle in the sense of example.

yathā cīvarādihetu, evaṃ bhavābhavahetūpīti attho: Just as the cause of a robe and so forth, so also the causes of existence and non-existence—such is the meaning.

bhavābhavo: existence and non-existence, paṇītapaṇītatarāni telamadhuphāṇitādīni adhippetāni: that is, here, excellent and still more excellent oil, honey, molasses, and so forth are intended.

imesaṃ pana catunnaṃ taṇhuppādānaṃ pahānatthāya paṭipāṭiyāva cattāro ariyavaṃsā desitāti veditabbā: However, it should be understood that the four noble lineages have been taught in order to abandon these four kinds of craving-production, successively.

paṭipadācatukkaṃ heṭṭhā vuttameva: The fourfold practice has already been described below.

akkhamādīsu padhānakaraṇakāle sītādīni na khamatīti akkhama: At the time of undertaking effort in things such as endurance, cold, etc., are not tolerated, thus, non-endurance.

khamatīti khamā: Tolerating, thus, endurance.

indriyadamanaṃ damā: Subduing the senses is self-control.
"...not dwelling on arisen thoughts of desire" and so forth, the subsiding of thoughts [is] samā.

cīvarahetū (robe-cause), bhavābhavahetū (causes of existence and non-existence), bhavābhavo (existence and non-existence), paṭipadācatukkaṃ (the fourfold path), akkhamā (impatience), khamā (patience), damā (self-control), samā (concentration).

dhammapadānī means sections of the Dharma.
anabhijjhā dhammapadaṃ means absence of covetousness, or non-greed, or the attainments of jhāna, vipassanā, path, fruition, and nirvāṇa with non-greed as the chief.
abyāpādo dhammapadaṃ means absence of ill-will, or non-anger, or the attainments of jhāna, etc., with loving-kindness as the chief.
sammāsati dhammapadaṃ means well-established mindfulness, or the attainments of jhāna, etc., with mindfulness as the chief.
sammāsamādhi dhammapadaṃ means concentration, or the attainments of jhāna, vipassanā, path, fruition, and nirvāṇa by way of the eight attainments.

Or, the attainments of jhāna, etc., by way of the ten kinds of foulness [are] anabhijjhā dhammapadaṃ; that which is attained by way of the four Brahmā-vihāras [is] abyāpādo dhammapadaṃ; that which is attained by way of the ten recollections and perception of loathsomeness in food [is] sammāsati dhammapadaṃ; that which is attained by way of the ten kasinas and mindfulness of breathing [is] sammāsamādhi dhammapadaṃ.

dhammapadānī

Among Dharma practices, firstly, the practice of naked ascetics.
Secondly, the perfectly pure conduct of the holy life, even for one who, being unable to take hold of arahantship due to intense defilements, [is] with tearful face, even weeping.
Thirdly, the habit of drinking in sensual pleasures.
Fourthly, even without obtaining the four conditions, the teaching and holy life together with happiness by way of jhāna and vipassanā.

dhammakkhandhā—here, khandha has the meaning of quality.
sīlakkhandho means the quality of virtue.
And here, the virtue of fruition is intended.
The same method [applies] in the remaining clauses as well.
Thus, in all four places, only fruition is stated.

dhammakkhandhā, sīlakkhandho

balānī means strengths by way of supporting and by way of being unshakeable.
Their unshakeability should be understood by [their] opponents, such as laziness, etc.
All of them are said in terms of mundane and supramundane [qualities] by way of calm, insight, and the path.

balānī

adhiṭṭhānānī—here, adhi is merely a prefix.
But in meaning, either they stand by that, or they stand in that, or the place itself is the basis of those persons who are superior in various qualities, or wisdom itself is the basis; paññādhiṭṭhānaṃ (wisdom-basis).
And here, with the first, the wisdom of the highest fruition [is discussed].
With the second, truthfulness of speech.
With the third, the giving up of material things.
With the fourth, the subsiding of defilements is said; [this] should be understood.
And with the first, the wisdom of fruition is said, having made the wisdom of ownership of karma or the wisdom of insight the beginning.
With the second, having made truthfulness of speech the beginning, ultimate truth [or] nirvāṇa [is discussed].
With the third [adhiṭṭhāna], making the renunciation of material things the beginning, the abandonment of defilements [occurs] by the highest path. With the fourth, making the defilements suppressed by attainment the beginning, the subsiding of defilements [occurs] by the highest path. Or, with one adhiṭṭhāna that is based on wisdom, the wisdom of the fruit of Arahatship is spoken of. With the remainder, the ultimate truth. Or, with one adhiṭṭhāna that is based on truth, the ultimate truth is spoken of. With the remainder, the wisdom of Arahatship—so says Mūsikābhaya Thero. Adhiṭṭhānānī. Adhī. Paññādhiṭṭhānaṃ. Mūsikābhaya Thero. Explanation of the fourfold beginning with questions and answers.

312. ‘‘Cattāri pañhabyākaraṇāni –cattāro pañhābyākaraṇā (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) ekaṃsabyākaraṇīyo pañho, paṭipucchābyākaraṇīyo pañho, vibhajjabyākaraṇīyo pañho, ṭhapanīyo pañho. ‘‘Cattāri kammāni – atthāvuso, kammaṃ kaṇhaṃ kaṇhavipākaṃ; atthāvuso, kammaṃ sukkaṃ sukkavipākaṃ; atthāvuso, kammaṃ kaṇhasukkaṃ kaṇhasukkavipākaṃ; atthāvuso, kammaṃ akaṇhaasukkaṃ akaṇhaasukkavipākaṃ kammakkhayāya saṃvattati. kammāni – ‘‘Cattāro sacchikaraṇīyā dhammā – pubbenivāso satiyā sacchikaraṇīyo; sattānaṃ cutūpapāto cakkhunā sacchikaraṇīyo; aṭṭha vimokkhā kāyena sacchikaraṇīyā; āsavānaṃ khayo paññāya sacchikaraṇīyo. sacchikaraṇīyā dhammā – ‘‘Cattāro oghā – kāmogho, bhavogho, diṭṭhogho, avijjogho. oghā – ‘‘Cattāro yogā – kāmayogo, bhavayogo, diṭṭhiyogo, avijjāyogo. yogā – ‘‘Cattāro visaññogā – kāmayogavisaññogo, bhavayogavisaññogo, diṭṭhiyogavisaññogo, avijjāyogavisaññogo. visaññogā – ‘‘Cattāro ganthā – abhijjhā kāyagantho, byāpādo kāyagantho, sīlabbataparāmāso kāyagantho, idaṃsaccābhiniveso kāyagantho. ganthā – ‘‘Cattāri upādānāni – kāmupādānaṃ kāmūpādānaṃ (sī. pī.) evamitaresupi, diṭṭhupādānaṃ, sīlabbatupādānaṃ, attavādupādānaṃ. upādānāni – kāmūpādānaṃ (sī. pī.) evamitaresupi ‘‘Catasso yoniyo – aṇḍajayoni, jalābujayoni, saṃsedajayoni, opapātikayoni. yoniyo – ‘‘Catasso gabbhāvakkantiyo. Idhāvuso, ekacco asampajāno mātukucchiṃ okkamati, asampajāno mātukucchismiṃ ṭhāti, asampajāno mātukucchimhā nikkhamati, ayaṃ paṭhamā gabbhāvakkanti. Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, idhekacco sampajāno mātukucchiṃ okkamati, asampajāno mātukucchismiṃ ṭhāti, asampajāno mātukucchimhā nikkhamati, ayaṃ dutiyā gabbhāvakkanti. Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, idhekacco sampajāno mātukucchiṃ okkamati, sampajāno mātukucchismiṃ ṭhāti, asampajāno mātukucchimhā nikkhamati, ayaṃ tatiyā gabbhāvakkanti. Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, idhekacco sampajāno mātukucchiṃ okkamati, sampajāno mātukucchismiṃ ṭhāti, sampajāno mātukucchimhā nikkhamati, ayaṃ catutthā gabbhāvakkanti. gabbhāvakkantiyo. ‘‘Cattāro attabhāvapaṭilābhā. Atthāvuso, attabhāvapaṭilābho, yasmiṃ attabhāvapaṭilābhe attasañcetanāyeva kamati, no parasañcetanā. Atthāvuso, attabhāvapaṭilābho, yasmiṃ attabhāvapaṭilābhe parasañcetanāyeva kamati, no attasañcetanā. Atthāvuso, attabhāvapaṭilābho, yasmiṃ attabhāvapaṭilābhe attasañcetanā ceva kamati parasañcetanā ca. Atthāvuso, attabhāvapaṭilābho, yasmiṃ attabhāvapaṭilābhe neva attasañcetanā kamati, no parasañcetanā. attabhāvapaṭilābhā.
312. "Four ways of answering questions (pañhabyākaraṇāni): the question to be answered with a definite reply (ekaṃsabyākaraṇīyo pañho), the question to be answered with a counter-question (paṭipucchābyākaraṇīyo pañho), the question to be answered with an analysis (vibhajjabyākaraṇīyo pañho), the question to be set aside (ṭhapanīyo pañho).

"Four kinds of karma (kammāni): Friends, there is karma that is dark with dark result (kaṇhaṃ kaṇhavipākaṃ); friends, there is karma that is bright with bright result (sukkaṃ sukkavipākaṃ); friends, there is karma that is dark & bright with dark & bright result (kaṇhasukkaṃ kaṇhasukkavipākaṃ); friends, there is karma that is neither dark nor bright with neither dark nor bright result, leading to the ending of karma (akaṇhaasukkaṃ akaṇhaasukkavipākaṃ kammakkhayāya saṃvattati).

"Four things to be realized (sacchikaraṇīyā dhammā): past lives are realized through mindfulness (pubbenivāso satiyā sacchikaraṇīyo); the passing away and rebirth of beings are realized through the eye (sattānaṃ cutūpapāto cakkhunā sacchikaraṇīyo); the eight liberations are realized through the body (aṭṭha vimokkhā kāyena sacchikaraṇīyā); the destruction of the influxes is realized through wisdom (āsavānaṃ khayo paññāya sacchikaraṇīyo).

"Four floods (oghā): the flood of sensual desire (kāmogho), the flood of becoming (bhavogho), the flood of views (diṭṭhogho), the flood of ignorance (avijjogho).

"Four yokes (yogā): the yoke of sensuality (kāmayogo), the yoke of becoming (bhavayogo), the yoke of views (diṭṭhiyogo), the yoke of ignorance (avijjāyogo).

"Four unfetterings (visaññogā): unfettering from the yoke of sensuality (kāmayogavisaññogo), unfettering from the yoke of becoming (bhavayogavisaññogo), unfettering from the yoke of views (diṭṭhiyogavisaññogo), unfettering from the yoke of ignorance (avijjāyogavisaññogo).

"Four ties (ganthā): covetousness is a bodily tie (abhijjhā kāyagantho), ill-will is a bodily tie (byāpādo kāyagantho), obsession with rules and observances is a bodily tie (sīlabbataparāmāso kāyagantho), insistence that only this is true is a bodily tie (idaṃsaccābhiniveso kāyagantho).

"Four clingings (upādānāni): clinging to sensual pleasures (kāmupādānaṃ), clinging to views (diṭṭhupādānaṃ), clinging to rules and observances (sīlabbatupādānaṃ), and clinging to a doctrine of self (attavādupādānaṃ).

"Four modes of generation (yoniyo): egg-born generation (aṇḍajayoni), placenta-born generation (jalābujayoni), slime-born generation (saṃsedajayoni), spontaneous generation (opapātikayoni).

"Four descents into the womb (gabbhāvakkantiyo): Here, friends, a certain person descends into the mother’s womb unalert, stays in the mother’s womb unalert, & leaves the mother’s womb unalert. This is the first descent of the embryo (paṭhamā gabbhāvakkanti). Furthermore, friends, here a certain person descends into the mother’s womb alert, stays in the mother’s womb unalert, & leaves the mother’s womb unalert. This is the second descent of the embryo (dutiyā gabbhāvakkanti). Furthermore, friends, here a certain person descends into the mother’s womb alert, stays in the mother’s womb alert, & leaves the mother’s womb unalert. This is the third descent of the embryo (tatiyā gabbhāvakkanti). Furthermore, friends, here a certain person descends into the mother’s womb alert, stays in the mother’s womb alert, & leaves the mother’s womb alert. This is the fourth descent of the embryo (catutthā gabbhāvakkanti).

"Four acquisitions of self-state (attabhāvapaṭilābhā): Friends, there is the acquisition of a self-state where one goes to a self-state through one’s own intention & not through the intention of another (attasañcetanāyeva kamati, no parasañcetanā). Friends, there is the acquisition of a self-state where one goes to a self-state through the intention of another & not through one’s own intention (parasañcetanāyeva kamati, no attasañcetanā).
There is, friend, the attainment of a self-state (attabhāva-paṭilābha), in which, in the attainment of that self-state, one proceeds by one's own intention and also by the intention of another.

There is, friend, the attainment of a self-state (attabhāva-paṭilābha), in which, in the attainment of that self-state, one proceeds neither by one's own intention nor by the intention of another.

Attainments of a self-state (attabhāva-paṭilābhā).
312.Pañhabyākaraṇāni mahāpadesakathāya vitthāritāneva. Kaṇhanti kāḷakaṃ dasaakusalakammapathakammaṃ. Kaṇhavipākanti apāye nibbattanato kāḷakavipākaṃ. Sukkanti paṇḍaraṃ kusalakammapathakammaṃ. Sukkavipākanti sagge nibbattanato paṇḍaravipākaṃ. Kaṇhasukkanti missakakammaṃ. Kaṇhasukkavipākanti sukhadukkhavipākaṃ. Missakakammañhi katvā akusalena tiracchānayoniyaṃ maṅgalahatthiṭṭhānādīsu uppanno kusalena pavatte sukhaṃ vedayati. Kusalena rājakulepi nibbatto akusalena pavatte dukkhaṃ vedayati. Akaṇhaasukkanti kammakkhayakaraṃ catumaggañāṇaṃ adhippetaṃ. Tañhi yadi kaṇhaṃ bhaveyya, kaṇhavipākaṃ dadeyya. Yadi sukkaṃ bhaveyya, sukkavipākaṃ dadeyya. Ubhayavipākassa pana adānato akaṇhāsukkavipākattā akaṇhaṃ asukkanti ayamettha attho. Kaṇha Kaṇhavipāka Sukka Sukkavipāka Kaṇhasukka Kaṇhasukkavipāka Akaṇhaasukka Sacchikaraṇīyāti paccakkhakaraṇena ceva paṭilābhena ca sacchikātabbā. Cakkhunāti dibbacakkhunā. Kāyenāti sahajātanāmakāyena. Paññāyāti arahattaphalañāṇena. Sacchikaraṇīyā Cakkhunā Kāyenā Paññāyā Oghāti vaṭṭasmiṃ satte ohananti osīdāpentīti oghā. Tattha pañcakāmaguṇiko rāgo kāmogho. Rūpārūpabhavesu chandarāgo bhavogho. Tathā jhānanikanti sassatadiṭṭhisahagato ca rāgo. Dvāsaṭṭhi diṭṭhiyo diṭṭhogho. Oghā kāmogho. bhavogho. diṭṭhogho. Vaṭṭasmiṃ yojentīti yogā. Te oghā viya veditabbā. yogā. Visaṃyojentīti visaññogā. Tattha asubhajjhānaṃ kāmayogavisaṃyogo. Taṃ pādakaṃ katvā adhigato anāgāmimaggo ekanteneva kāmayogavisaññogo nāma. Arahattamaggo bhavayogavisaññogo nāma. Sotāpattimaggo diṭṭhiyogavisaññogo nāma. Arahattamaggo avijjāyogavisaññogo nāma. visaññogā. kāmayogavisaññogo bhavayogavisaññogo diṭṭhiyogavisaññogo avijjāyogavisaññogo Ganthanavasena ganthā. Vaṭṭasmiṃ nāmakāyañceva rūpakāyañca ganthati bandhati palibundhatīti kāyagantho.Idaṃsaccābhinivesoti idameva saccaṃ, moghamaññanti evaṃ pavatto diṭṭhābhiniveso. ganthā. kāyagantho. Idaṃsaccābhiniveso Upādānānīti ādānaggahaṇāni. Kāmoti rāgo, soyeva gahaṇaṭṭhena upādānanti kāmupādānaṃ. Diṭṭhīti micchādiṭṭhi, sāpi gahaṇaṭṭhena upādānanti diṭṭhupādānaṃ. Iminā suddhīti evaṃ sīlavatānaṃ gahaṇaṃ sīlabbatupādānaṃ. Attāti etena vadati ceva upādiyati cāti attavādupādānaṃ. Upādānānī kāmupādānaṃ. diṭṭhupādānaṃ. sīlabbatupādānaṃ. attavādupādānaṃ. Yoniyoti koṭṭhāsā. Aṇḍe jātāti aṇḍajā. Jalābumhi jātāti jalābujā. Saṃsede jātāti saṃsedajā. Sayanasmiṃ pūtimacchādīsu ca nibbattānametaṃ adhivacanaṃ. Vegena āgantvā upapatitā viyāti opapātikā. Tattha devamanussesu saṃsedajaopapātikānaṃ ayaṃ viseso. Saṃsedajā mandā daharā hutvā nibbattanti. Opapātikā soḷasavassuddesikā hutvā. Manussesu hi bhummadevesu ca imā catassopi yoniyo labbhanti. Tathā tiracchānesu supaṇṇanāgādīsu. Vuttañhetaṃ – ‘‘tattha, bhikkhave, aṇḍajā supaṇṇā aṇḍajeva nāge haranti, na jalābuje na saṃsedaje na opapātike’’ti (saṃ. ni. 3.393). Cātumahārājikato paṭṭhāya uparidevā opapātikāyeva . Tathā nerayikā. Petesu catassopi labbhanti. Gabbhāvakkantiyo sampasādanīye kathitā eva. Yoniyo aṇḍajā. jalābujā. saṃsedajā. opapātikā. Gabbhāvakkantiyo Attabhāvapaṭilābhesu paṭhamo khiḍḍāpadosikavasena veditabbo. Dutiyo orabbhikādīhi ghātiyamānaurabbhādivasena. Tatiyo manopadosikāvasena. Catuttho cātumahārājike upādāya uparisesadevatāvasena. Te hi devā neva attasañcetanāya maranti, na parasañcetanāya. Attabhāvapaṭilābhesu Dakkhiṇāvisuddhādicatukkavaṇṇanā
312. pañhabyākaraṇāni (questions and explanations) have been extensively explained in the Mahāpadesakathā.

kaṇha means black, the ten unwholesome paths of action (akusalakammapatha).
kaṇhavipāka means having a black result, because it causes rebirth in the lower realms (apāya).
sukka means white, the wholesome paths of action (kusalakammapatha).
sukkavipāka means having a white result, because it causes rebirth in the heavens (sagga).
kaṇhasukka means mixed karma.
kaṇhasukkavipāka means having a result of pleasure and pain.
Having done mixed karma, by unwholesome karma, one is born in the animal realm, in places such as the elephant stables during festivals, and by wholesome karma, one experiences pleasure.
By wholesome karma, one is born even in a royal family, and by unwholesome karma, one experiences pain.
akaṇhaasukka means the four knowledges of the paths (catumaggañāṇa) that destroy karma are intended.
If that were black (kaṇha), it would give a black result (kaṇhavipāka).
If that were white (sukka), it would give a white result (sukkavipāka).
But since it does not give either result, due to having a result that is neither black nor white (akaṇhāsukkavipāka), akaṇhaṃ asukkaṃ means this here.

sacchikaraṇīyā means to be realized by direct experience (paccakkhakaraṇena) and by attainment (paṭilābhena).
cakkhunā means by the divine eye (dibbacakkhunā).
kāyenā means by the body born together [with consciousness] (sahajātanāmakāyena).
paññāyā means by the knowledge of the fruit of Arahatship (arahattaphalañāṇena).

oghā means floods, because they sweep away and submerge beings in the round of existence (vaṭṭa).
There, the desire associated with the five sense pleasures is the kāmogho (flood of sensual desire).
The desire and lust (chandarāgo) in the realms of form and formlessness (rūpārūpabhavesu) is the bhavogho (flood of becoming).
Likewise, attachment to meditation (jhānanikanti) and the lust associated with eternalistic views is also [the flood of becoming].
The sixty-two views are the diṭṭhogho (flood of views).

yogā means that which binds to the round of existence (vaṭṭa).
These should be understood like floods (oghā).

visaññogā means that which unbinds.
There, the meditation on the unattractive (asubhajjhānaṃ) is the kāmayogavisaṃyoga (unbinding from the yoke of sensual desire).
Having made that the basis, the path of the non-returner (anāgāmimaggo) attained is entirely called kāmayogavisaññogo.
The path of Arahatship (arahattamaggo) is called bhavayogavisaññogo (unbinding from the yoke of becoming).
The path of stream-entry (sotāpattimaggo) is called diṭṭhiyogavisaññogo (unbinding from the yoke of views).
The path of Arahatship (arahattamaggo) is called avijjāyogavisaññogo (unbinding from the yoke of ignorance avijjā).
Disconnection from the union with desire (kāmayogavisaṃyoga), disconnection from the union with becoming (bhavayogavisaṃyoga), disconnection from the union with views (dṛṣṭiyogavisaṃyoga), disconnection from the union with ignorance (avidyāyogavisaṃyoga).

Due to the nature of ties (grantha).

Ties (granthā).
Because it ties, binds, and completely binds the name-body (nāmakāya) and the form-body (rūpakāya in a round, therefore, [it is called] body-tie (kāyagrantha).

"Grasping at this as the truth" (idaṃsaccābhiniveso) means: Only this is the truth, and they consider the rest worthless; thus, the adherence to views proceeds.

Ties (granthā).
Body-tie (kāyagrantha).
Grasping at this as the truth (idaṃsaccābhiniveso).

"Grasping" (upādānāni) means acts of taking up (ādānaggahaṇāni).

Desire (kāma) is attachment (rāga), and that itself is grasping because of its characteristic of seizing; therefore, [it is called] grasping at desire (kāmupādānaṃ).

View (dṛṣṭi) is wrong view (micchādiṭṭhi), and that itself is grasping because of its characteristic of seizing; therefore, [it is called] grasping at views (dṛṣṭhupādānaṃ).

Through this, [there is] purification; thus, the seizing of those possessing morality (śīla) is grasping at morality and vows (śīlabbatupādānaṃ).

Self (ātman) is declared and taken up through this; therefore, [it is called] grasping at the doctrine of self (attavādupādānaṃ).

Grasping (upādānāni). Grasping at desire (kāmupādānaṃ).
Grasping at views (dṛṣṭhupādānaṃ).
Grasping at morality and vows (śīlabbatupādānaṃ).
Grasping at the doctrine of self (attavādupādānaṃ).

"Wombs" (yoniyo) means compartments (koṭṭhāsā).

Born from an egg (aṇḍa) means egg-born (aṇḍajā).

Born from a womb (jalābu) means womb-born (jalābujā).

Born from moisture (saṃseda) means moisture-born (saṃsedajā).

This is a designation for beings who arise in beds and among rotten fish, and so forth.

Having come swiftly, they arise suddenly; therefore, [they are called] spontaneously born (opapātikā).

There, among gods and humans, this is the distinction for the moisture-born and spontaneously born.

Moisture-born beings arise having become dull and young.

Spontaneously born beings become sixteen years old instantly.

Indeed, these four wombs are found among humans and earth-bound deities.

Likewise, among animals, such as Garuḍas (suparṇa), Nāgas (nāga), and so forth.

It was said, "There, monks, egg-born Garuḍas seize egg-born Nāgas, but not womb-born, moisture-born, or spontaneously born Nāgas" (Saṃyutta Nikāya 3.393).

From the Realm of the Four Great Kings (cātumahārājika) upward, the higher gods are only spontaneously born.

Likewise, hell-beings (nerayika).

All four are found among the departed spirits (preta).

Entries into the womb (gabbhāvakkanti) have already been described in detail.

Wombs (yoniyo). Egg-born (aṇḍajā).
Womb-born (jalābujā).
Moisture-born (saṃsedajā).
opapātikā. gabbhāvakkantiyo

In the gaining of individual existence (attabhāvapaṭilābhesu): The first should be understood as being through the fault of play (khiḍḍāpadosikavasena); the second, as sheep etc. being slaughtered by butchers etc. (orabbhikādīhi ghātiyamānaurabbhādivasena); the third, as being through the fault of mind (manopadosikāvasena); the fourth, as deities beginning with the Cātumahārājika deities and the remaining deities (cātumahārājike upādāya uparisesadevatāvasena). For those gods neither die by their own intention (attasañcetanāya), nor by the intention of another (parasañcetanāya). For they are apparitional only (opapātikā eva hi te honti).

attabhāvapaṭilābhesu: Explanation of the tetrad beginning with purity of offerings (dakkhiṇāvisuddhādicatukkavaṇṇanā).

313. ‘‘Catasso dakkhiṇāvisuddhiyo. Atthāvuso, dakkhiṇā dāyakato visujjhati no paṭiggāhakato. Atthāvuso, dakkhiṇā paṭiggāhakato visujjhati no dāyakato. Atthāvuso, dakkhiṇā neva dāyakato visujjhati no paṭiggāhakato. Atthāvuso, dakkhiṇā dāyakato ceva visujjhati paṭiggāhakato ca. ‘‘Cattāri saṅgahavatthūni – dānaṃ, peyyavajjaṃ piyavajjaṃ (syā. kaṃ. ka.), atthacariyā, samānattatā. saṅgahavatthūni – piyavajjaṃ (syā. kaṃ. ka.) ‘‘Cattāro anariyavohārā – musāvādo, pisuṇāvācā, pharusāvācā, samphappalāpo. anariyavohārā – ‘‘Cattāro ariyavohārā – musāvādā veramaṇī veramaṇi (ka.), pisuṇāya vācāya veramaṇī, pharusāya vācāya veramaṇī, samphappalāpā veramaṇī. ariyavohārā – veramaṇi (ka.) ‘‘Aparepi cattāro anariyavohārā – adiṭṭhe diṭṭhavāditā, assute sutavāditā, amute mutavāditā, aviññāte viññātavāditā. anariyavohārā – ‘‘Aparepi cattāro ariyavohārā – adiṭṭhe adiṭṭhavāditā, assute assutavāditā, amute amutavāditā, aviññāte aviññātavāditā. ariyavohārā – ‘‘Aparepi cattāro anariyavohārā – diṭṭhe adiṭṭhavāditā, sute assutavāditā, mute amutavāditā, viññāte aviññātavāditā. anariyavohārā – ‘‘Aparepi cattāro ariyavohārā – diṭṭhe diṭṭhavāditā, sute sutavāditā, mute mutavāditā, viññāte viññātavāditā. ariyavohārā –
313. “There are four kinds of purification of offerings (dakkhiṇāvisuddhiyo). There is, friends, the offering that is purified by the donor (dāyakato), but not by the recipient (paṭiggāhakato). There is, friends, the offering that is purified by the recipient, but not by the donor. There is, friends, the offering that is purified neither by the donor nor by the recipient. There is, friends, the offering that is purified both by the donor and by the recipient.

“There are four grounds of sympathy (saṅgahavatthūni): giving (dānaṃ), kind speech (peyyavajjaṃ), beneficial service (atthacariyā), and impartiality (samānattatā).

“There are four ignoble modes of speech (anariyavohārā): lying (musāvādo), malicious speech (pisuṇāvācā), harsh speech (pharusāvācā), and idle chatter (samphappalāpo).

“There are four noble modes of speech (ariyavohārā): abstaining from lying (musāvādā veramaṇī), abstaining from malicious speech (pisuṇāya vācāya veramaṇī), abstaining from harsh speech (pharusāya vācāya veramaṇī), and abstaining from idle chatter (samphappalāpā veramaṇī).

“There are four more ignoble modes of speech (anariyavohārā): saying that what has not been seen has been seen (adiṭṭhe diṭṭhavāditā), saying that what has not been heard has been heard (assute sutavāditā), saying that what has not been sensed has been sensed (amute mutavāditā), saying that what has not been cognized has been cognized (aviññāte viññātavāditā).

“There are four more noble modes of speech (ariyavohārā): saying that what has not been seen has not been seen (adiṭṭhe adiṭṭhavāditā), saying that what has not been heard has not been heard (assute assutavāditā), saying that what has not been sensed has not been sensed (amute amutavāditā), saying that what has not been cognized has not been cognized (aviññāte aviññātavāditā).

“There are four more ignoble modes of speech (anariyavohārā): saying that what has been seen has not been seen (diṭṭhe adiṭṭhavāditā), saying that what has been heard has not been heard (sute assutavāditā), saying that what has been sensed has not been sensed (mute amutavāditā), saying that what has been cognized has not been cognized (viññāte aviññātavāditā).

“There are four more noble modes of speech (ariyavohārā): saying that what has been seen has been seen (diṭṭhe diṭṭhavāditā), saying that what has been heard has been heard (sute sutavāditā), saying that what has been sensed has been sensed (mute mutavāditā), saying that what has been cognized has been cognized (viññāte viññātavāditā).
313.Dakkhiṇāvisuddhiyoti dānasaṅkhātā dakkhiṇā visujjhanti mahapphalā honti etāhīti dakkhiṇāvisuddhiyo. Dāyakato visujjhati, no paṭiggāhakatoti yattha dāyako sīlavā hoti, dhammenuppannaṃ deyyadhammaṃ deti, paṭiggāhako dussīlo. Ayaṃ dakkhiṇā vessantaramahārājassa dakkhiṇāsadisā. Paṭiggāhakatovisujjhati, no dāyakatoti yattha paṭiggāhako sīlavā hoti, dāyako dussīlo, adhammenuppannaṃ deti, ayaṃ dakkhiṇā coraghātakassa dakkhiṇāsadisā. Neva dāyakato visujjhati, no paṭiggāhakatoti yattha ubhopi dussīlā deyyadhammopi adhammena nibbatto. Vipariyāyena catutthā veditabbā. Dāyakato visujjhati, no paṭiggāhakato Paṭiggāhakato visujjhati, no dāyakato Neva dāyakato visujjhati, no paṭiggāhakato Saṅgahavatthūnīti saṅgahakāraṇāni. Tāni heṭṭhā vibhattāneva. Saṅgahavatthūnī Anariyavohārāti anariyānaṃ lāmakānaṃ vohārā. Anariyavohārā Ariyavohārāti ariyānaṃ sappurisānaṃ vohārā. Ariyavohārā Diṭṭhavāditāti diṭṭhaṃ mayāti evaṃ vāditā. Ettha ca taṃtaṃsamuṭṭhāpakacetanāvasena attho veditabbo. Diṭṭhavāditā Attantapādicatukkavaṇṇanā
313. Dakkhiṇāvisuddhiyo means dakkhiṇā, which is synonymous with giving, are purified, become greatly fruitful; therefore, [they are called] dakkhiṇāvisuddhiyo.

Where the giver is virtuous, gives the gift-item produced in accordance with dhamma, [but] the receiver is immoral, [this] dakkhiṇā is similar to the dakkhiṇā of King Vessantara.

Where the receiver is virtuous, [but] the giver is immoral, gives [something] produced not in accordance with dhamma, [this] dakkhiṇā is similar to the dakkhiṇā of a robber-killer.

Where both are immoral, [and] also the gift-item is produced not in accordance with dhamma, the fourth should be understood by reversal.

Dāyakato visujjhati, no paṭiggāhakato paṭiggāhakato

Visujjhati, no dāyakato neva dāyakato visujjhati, no paṭiggāhakato

Saṅgahavatthūnī means causes of integration. These have been explained below.

Saṅgahavatthūnī

Anariyavohārā means speech of the ignoble, the base.

Anariyavohārā

Ariyavohārā means speech of the noble, the good persons.

Ariyavohārā

Diṭṭhavāditā means having the doctrine that "it has been seen by me." And here, the meaning should be understood in terms of the consciousness that gives rise to the respective thing.

314. ‘‘Cattāro puggalā. Idhāvuso, ekacco puggalo attantapo hoti attaparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto. Idhāvuso, ekacco puggalo parantapo hoti paraparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto. Idhāvuso, ekacco puggalo attantapo ca hoti attaparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto, parantapo ca paraparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto. Idhāvuso, ekacco puggalo neva attantapo hoti na attaparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto na parantapo na paraparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto. So anattantapo aparantapo diṭṭheva dhamme nicchāto nibbuto sītībhūto sītibhūto (ka.) sukhappaṭisaṃvedī brahmabhūtena attanā viharati. ‘‘Aparepi cattāro puggalā. Idhāvuso, ekacco puggalo attahitāya paṭipanno hoti no parahitāya. Idhāvuso, ekacco puggalo parahitāya paṭipanno hoti no attahitāya. Idhāvuso , ekacco puggalo neva attahitāya paṭipanno hoti no parahitāya. Idhāvuso, ekacco puggalo attahitāya ceva paṭipanno hoti parahitāya ca. ‘‘Aparepi cattāro puggalā – tamo tamaparāyano, tamo jotiparāyano, joti tamaparāyano, joti jotiparāyano. puggalā – ‘‘Aparepi cattāro puggalā – samaṇamacalo, samaṇapadumo, samaṇapuṇḍarīko, samaṇesu samaṇasukhumālo. puggalā – ‘‘Ime kho, āvuso, tena bhagavatā jānatā passatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena cattāro dhammā sammadakkhātā; tattha sabbeheva saṅgāyitabbaṃ…pe… atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ. jānatā passatā Paṭhamabhāṇavāro niṭṭhito. Pañcakaṃ
314. "Four individuals. Here, friends, a certain individual torments himself and is devoted to the practice of torturing himself. Here, friends, a certain individual torments others and is devoted to the practice of torturing others. Here, friends, a certain individual both torments himself and is devoted to the practice of torturing himself, and also torments others and is devoted to the practice of torturing others. Here, friends, a certain individual neither torments himself nor is devoted to the practice of torturing himself, neither torments others nor is devoted to the practice of torturing others. He, not tormenting himself nor tormenting others, in this very life, is without craving, extinguished, cooled, experiencing pleasure, abides with a mind become like Brahmā.

"Again, four individuals. Here, friends, a certain individual practices for his own benefit but not for the benefit of others. Here, friends, a certain individual practices for the benefit of others but not for his own benefit. Here, friends, a certain individual practices neither for his own benefit nor for the benefit of others. Here, friends, a certain individual practices both for his own benefit and for the benefit of others.

"Again, four individuals: one who is in darkness and is devoted to darkness, one who is in darkness and is devoted to light, one who is in light and is devoted to darkness, one who is in light and is devoted to light.

"Again, four individuals: a śramaṇa who is unmoving (samaṇamacala), a śramaṇa like a lotus (samaṇapaduma), a śramaṇa like a white lotus (samaṇapuṇḍarīka), a śramaṇa who is exceedingly delicate among śramaṇas (samaṇesu samaṇasukhumāla).

"These, friends, are the four dharmas well-proclaimed by that Blessed One, the one who knows (jānatā), the one who sees (passatā), the worthy one, the perfectly self-awakened one; there all should recite together...for the benefit and happiness of gods and humans."

The first recitation section is finished.

314.Attantapādīsu paṭhamo acelako. Dutiyo orabbhikādīsu aññataro. Tatiyo yaññayājako. Catuttho sāsane sammāpaṭipanno. Attahitāya paṭipannādīsu paṭhamo yo sayaṃ sīlādisampanno, paraṃ sīlādīsu na samādapeti āyasmā vakkalitthero viya. Dutiyo yo attanā na sīlādisampanno, paraṃ sīlādīsu samādapeti āyasmā upanando viya. Tatiyo yo nevattanā sīlādisampanno, paraṃ sīlādīsu na samādapeti devadatto viya. Catuttho yo attanā ca sīlādisampanno parañca sīlādīsu samādapeti āyasmā mahākassapo viya. Attahitāya paṭipannā Tamādīsu tamoti andhakārabhūto. Tamaparāyaṇoti tamameva paraṃ ayanaṃ gati assāti tamaparāyaṇo. Evaṃ sabbapadesu attho veditabbo. Ettha ca paṭhamo nīce caṇḍālādikule dujjīvite hīnattabhāve nibbattitvā tīṇi duccaritāni paripūreti. Dutiyo tathāvidho hutvā tīṇi sucaritāni paripūreti. Tatiyo uḷāre khattiyakule bahuannapāne sampannattabhāve nibbattitvā tīṇi duccaritāni paripūreti. Catuttho tādisova hutvā tīṇi sucaritāni paripūreti. tamo Tamaparāyaṇo Samaṇamacaloti samaṇaacalo. Rāgadosānaṃ abhāvā khippameva pupphissatīti anāgāmī samaṇapuṇḍarīko nāma. Tenāha – ‘‘katamo ca puggalo samaṇapuṇḍarīko? 314. The first of these is the naked ascetic.
The second is one of those who begin with butchers.
The third is a sacrificer.
The fourth is one who has rightly entered the teaching.

The first of those who have entered for their own benefit is the venerable Vakkali, who, though himself endowed with morality etc., does not encourage others in morality etc.
The second is the venerable Upananda, who, though himself not endowed with morality etc., encourages others in morality etc.
The third is Devadatta, who neither himself is endowed with morality etc., nor encourages others in morality etc.
The fourth is the venerable Mahākassapa, who both himself is endowed with morality etc., and encourages others in morality etc.

"Darkness" means being shrouded in darkness.
"Having darkness as the final end" means that darkness is the ultimate goal and destination for that being.
In this way, the meaning should be understood in all instances.
And here, the first one, having been born in a low family such as that of an outcaste, in a miserable state, and in a state of inferior selfhood, completely fulfills the three kinds of bad conduct.
The second, having become like that, completely fulfills the three kinds of good conduct.
The third, having been born in an eminent aristocratic family, in a state of abundant food and drink, and in a state of accomplished selfhood, completely fulfills the three kinds of bad conduct.
The fourth, having become just like that, completely fulfills the three kinds of good conduct.

"Unshakable recluse" means a recluse who is unshakable.
The letter "m" is merely the junction of words.
That one should be understood as a stream-enterer ( srotāpanna).
Because the stream-enterer is unshaken by the attacks of others, like an indrakhīla pillar by the four winds.
Because he is endowed with unshakable faith, he is an "unshakable recluse".
It was also said, "Which person is an unshakable recluse? Here, a certain person, through the destruction of the three fetters..." (Pu.
Pa.
190) in detail.
Because the passion and hatred have become thin, the once-returner (sakadāgāmī) is called "lotus-like recluse."

Therefore, it is said: “Which person is a lotus-like recluse? Here, a certain person, having come once to this world, makes an end of suffering; this person is called a lotus-like recluse” (Pu.
Pa.
190).
Because of the absence of passion and hatred, the non-returner (anāgāmī) will quickly bloom and is called "lotus-like recluse."

Therefore, it is said: “Which person is a lotus-like recluse? Here, a certain person, through the destruction of the five lower fetters… this person is called a lotus-like recluse” (Pu.
Pa.
190).
Because of the absence of all the bonds of sensual craving and defilements, the arhat among recluses is called "most delicate recluse".

Therefore, it is said: "Which person among recluses is the most delicate recluse? Here, a certain person, through the destruction of the influxes... having attained full ordination, dwells.
This person is called the most delicate recluse among recluses."
1026"/> samaṇapuṇḍarīko samaṇasukhumālo "These, friend," etc., should be connected in the manner already stated.
Thus, while explaining two hundred questions in terms of groups of four with equal wisdom, the elder showed the flavor of concord.
The commentary on the groups of four is finished.

315. ‘‘Atthi kho, āvuso, tena bhagavatā jānatā passatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena pañca dhammā sammadakkhātā. Tattha sabbeheva saṅgāyitabbaṃ…pe… atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ. Katame pañca? ‘‘Pañcakkhandhā. Rūpakkhandho vedanākkhandho saññākkhandho saṅkhārakkhandho viññāṇakkhandho. ‘‘Pañcakkhandhā. ‘‘Pañcupādānakkhandhā. Rūpupādānakkhandho rūpūpādānakkhandho (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) evamitaresupi vedanupādānakkhandho saññupādānakkhandho saṅkhārupādānakkhandho viññāṇupādānakkhandho. ‘‘Pañcupādānakkhandhā. rūpūpādānakkhandho (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.) evamitaresupi ‘‘Pañca kāmaguṇā. Cakkhuviññeyyā rūpā iṭṭhā kantā manāpā piyarūpā kāmūpasañhitā rajanīyā , sotaviññeyyā saddā… ghānaviññeyyā gandhā… jivhāviññeyyā rasā… kāyaviññeyyā phoṭṭhabbā iṭṭhā kantā manāpā piyarūpā kāmūpasañhitā rajanīyā. kāmaguṇā. ‘‘Pañca gatiyo – nirayo, tiracchānayoni, pettivisayo, manussā, devā. gatiyo – ‘‘Pañca macchariyāni – āvāsamacchariyaṃ, kulamacchariyaṃ, lābhamacchariyaṃ, vaṇṇamacchariyaṃ, dhammamacchariyaṃ. macchariyāni – ‘‘Pañca nīvaraṇāni – kāmacchandanīvaraṇaṃ, byāpādanīvaraṇaṃ, thinamiddhanīvaraṇaṃ, uddhaccakukkuccanīvaraṇaṃ, vicikicchānīvaraṇaṃ. nīvaraṇāni – ‘‘Pañca orambhāgiyāni saññojanāni – sakkāyadiṭṭhi, vicikicchā, sīlabbataparāmāso, kāmacchando, byāpādo. orambhāgiyāni saññojanāni – ‘‘Pañca uddhambhāgiyāni saññojanāni – rūparāgo, arūparāgo, māno, uddhaccaṃ, avijjā. uddhambhāgiyāni saññojanāni – ‘‘Pañca sikkhāpadāni – pāṇātipātā veramaṇī, adinnādānā veramaṇī, kāmesumicchācārā veramaṇī, musāvādā veramaṇī, surāmerayamajjappamādaṭṭhānā veramaṇī. sikkhāpadāni –
“There are, friends, five dhammas that are rightly expounded by the Blessed One—the one who knows, the one who sees, the Worthy One, rightly self-awakened. There, all should chant together…for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of gods and humans. What five?

“The five aggregates (pañcakkhandhā). The form aggregate (rūpakkhandha), the feeling aggregate (vedanākkhandha), the perception aggregate (saññākkhandha), the fabrication aggregate (saṅkhārakkhandha), the consciousness aggregate (viññāṇakkhandha).

“The five aggregates (pañcakkhandhā).

“The five aggregates subject to clinging (pañcupādānakkhandhā). The form aggregate subject to clinging (rūpupādānakkhandha), the feeling aggregate subject to clinging (vedanupādānakkhandha), the perception aggregate subject to clinging (saññupādānakkhandha), the volitional formations aggregate subject to clinging (saṅkhārupādānakkhandha), the consciousness aggregate subject to clinging (viññāṇupādānakkhandha).

“The five aggregates subject to clinging (pañcupādānakkhandhā).

“The five sense pleasures (pañca kāmaguṇā). Forms cognizable via the eye that are wished for, desired, agreeable, and likeable, connected with sensual desire and provocative of lust (cakkhuviññeyyā rūpā iṭṭhā kantā manāpā piyarūpā kāmūpasañhitā rajanīyā), sounds cognizable via the ear… aromas cognizable via the nose… flavors cognizable via the tongue… tactile sensations cognizable via the body that are wished for, desired, agreeable, and likeable, connected with sensual desire and provocative of lust (kāyaviññeyyā phoṭṭhabbā iṭṭhā kantā manāpā piyarūpā kāmūpasañhitā rajanīyā).

Sense pleasures (kāmaguṇā).

“The five destinations (pañca gatiyo) – hell (nirayo), the animal realm (tiracchānayoni), the realm of the departed spirits (pettivisayo), humans (manussā), gods (devā).

The destinations (gatiyo).

“The five kinds of stinginess (pañca macchariyāni) – stinginess about one’s dwelling (āvāsamacchariyaṃ), stinginess about one’s family (kulamacchariyaṃ), stinginess about gains (lābhamacchariyaṃ), stinginess about one’s reputation (vaṇṇamacchariyaṃ), stinginess about the Dhamma (dhammamacchariyaṃ).

Stinginess (macchariyāni).

“The five hindrances (pañca nīvaraṇāni) – the hindrance of sensual desire (kāmacchandanīvaraṇaṃ), the hindrance of ill will (byāpādanīvaraṇaṃ), the hindrance of sloth and torpor (thinamiddhanīvaraṇaṃ), the hindrance of restlessness and remorse (uddhaccakukkuccanīvaraṇaṃ), the hindrance of doubt (vicikicchānīvaraṇaṃ).

Hindrances (nīvaraṇāni).

“The five lower fetters (pañca orambhāgiyāni saññojanāni) – belief in a self (sakkāyadiṭṭhi), doubt (vicikicchā), clinging to rules and rituals (sīlabbataparāmāso), sensual desire (kāmacchando), ill will (byāpādo).

Lower fetters (orambhāgiyāni saññojanāni).

“The five higher fetters (pañca uddhambhāgiyāni saññojanāni) – desire for form (rūparāgo), desire for the formless (arūparāgo), conceit (māno), restlessness (uddhaccaṃ), ignorance (avijjā).

Higher fetters (uddhambhāgiyāni saññojanāni).

“The five precepts (pañca sikkhāpadāni) – abstaining from killing living beings (pāṇātipātā veramaṇī), abstaining from taking what is not given (adinnādānā veramaṇī), abstaining from sexual misconduct (kāmesumicchācārā veramaṇī), abstaining from false speech (musāvādā veramaṇī), abstaining from intoxicants that cause heedlessness (surāmerayamajjappamādaṭṭhānā veramaṇī).

Precepts (sikkhāpadāni).
315. Iti catukkavasena sāmaggirasaṃ dassetvā idāni pañcakavasena dassetuṃ puna desanaṃ ārabhi. Tattha pañcasu khandhesu rūpakkhandho lokiyo. Sesā lokiyalokuttarā. Upādānakkhandhā lokiyāva. Vitthārato pana khandhakathā visuddhimagge vuttā. Kāmaguṇā heṭṭhā vitthāritāva. Sukatadukkaṭādīhi gantabbāti gatiyo. Nirayoti nirassādo. Sahokāsena khandhā kathitā. Tato paresu tīsu nibbattā khandhāva vuttā. Catutthe okāsopi. gatiyo. Nirayo Āvāse macchariyaṃ āvāsamacchariyaṃ. Tena samannāgato bhikkhu āgantukaṃ disvā ‘‘ettha cetiyassa vā saṅghassa vā parikkhāro ṭhapito’’tiādīni vatvā saṅghikampi āvāsaṃ nivāreti. So kālaṅkatvā peto vā ajagaro vā hutvā nibbattati. Kule macchariyaṃ kulamacchariyaṃ. Tena samannāgato bhikkhu tehi kāraṇehi attano upaṭṭhākakule aññesaṃ pavesanampi nivāreti. Lābhe macchariyaṃ lābhamacchariyaṃ. Tena samannāgato bhikkhu saṅghikampi lābhaṃ maccharāyanto yathā aññe na labhanti, evaṃ karoti. Vaṇṇe macchariyaṃ vaṇṇamacchariyaṃ. Vaṇṇoti cettha sarīravaṇṇopi guṇavaṇṇopi veditabbo. Pariyattidhamme macchariyaṃ dhammamacchariyaṃ. Tena samannāgato bhikkhu ‘‘imaṃ dhammaṃ pariyāpuṇitvā eso maṃ abhibhavissatī’’ti aññassa na deti. Yo pana dhammānuggahena vā puggalānuggahena vā na deti, na taṃ macchariyaṃ. āvāsamacchariyaṃ. kulamacchariyaṃ. lābhamacchariyaṃ. vaṇṇamacchariyaṃ. Vaṇṇo dhammamacchariyaṃ. Cittaṃ nivārenti pariyonandhantīti nīvaraṇāni. Kāmacchando nīvaraṇapatto arahattamaggavajjho. Kāmarāgānusayo kāmarāgasaṃyojanapatto anāgāmimaggavajjho. Thinaṃ cittagelaññaṃ . Middhaṃ khandhattayagelaññaṃ. Ubhayampi arahattamaggavajjhaṃ. Tathā uddhaccaṃ. Kukkuccaṃ anāgāmimaggavajjhaṃ. Vicikicchā paṭhamamaggavajjhā. nīvaraṇāni. Saṃyojanānīti bandhanāni. Tehi pana baddhesu puggalesu rūpārūpabhave nibbattā sotāpannasakadāgāmino antobaddhā bahisayitā nāma. Tesañhi kāmabhave bandhanaṃ. Kāmabhave anāgāmino bahibaddhā antosayitā nāma. Tesañhi rūpārūpabhave bandhanaṃ. Kāmabhave sotāpannasakadāgāmino antobaddhā antosayitā nāma. Rūpārūpabhave anāgāmino bahibaddhā bahisayitā nāma. Khīṇāsavo sabbattha abandhano. Saṃyojanānī Sikkhitabbaṃ padaṃ sikkhāpadaṃ, sikkhākoṭṭhāsoti attho. Sikkhāya vā padaṃ sikkhāpadaṃ, adhicittaadhipaññāsikkhāya adhigamupāyoti attho. Ayamettha saṅkhepo. Vitthārato pana sikkhāpadakathā vibhaṅgappakaraṇe sikkhāpadavibhaṅge āgatā eva. sikkhāpadaṃ, Abhabbaṭṭhānādipañcakavaṇṇanā
315. Thus, having shown the essence of harmony in terms of a tetrad, now, in order to show it in terms of a pentad, [the text] again begins the teaching.

There, among the five aggregates (pañcakkhandhā), the aggregate of form (rūpakkhandha) is mundane (lokiyo). The remaining [aggregates] are mundane and supramundane (lokiyalokuttarā). The aggregates of clinging (upādānakkhandhā) are only mundane (lokiyāva). The detailed discussion of the aggregates (khandhakathā) is stated in the Visuddhimagga. The sensual qualities (kāmaguṇā) have already been explained extensively below.

Because one goes by means of good deeds, bad deeds, etc., [these are called] destinies (gatiyo). Niraya means without enjoyment. The aggregates have been spoken of along with their location. After that, in the following three, only the aggregates that have arisen are mentioned. In the fourth, the location is also [mentioned].

Jealousy concerning dwellings (āvāsamacchariyaṃ) is avarice regarding dwellings. A monk endowed with that, seeing a newcomer, and saying things such as, "Here, the requisites for a shrine or for the sangha have been placed," prevents [him from using] even a communal dwelling. Having died, he is reborn as a ghost or a python. Jealousy concerning families (kulamacchariyaṃ) is avarice regarding families. A monk endowed with that, for those reasons, prevents others from even entering his patron families. Jealousy concerning gains (lābhamacchariyaṃ) is avarice regarding gains. A monk endowed with that, being avaricious even regarding communal gains, acts in such a way that others do not receive [them]. Jealousy concerning appearance (vaṇṇamacchariyaṃ) is avarice regarding appearance. Here, "appearance" (vaṇṇo) should be understood as both bodily appearance and the appearance of good qualities. Jealousy concerning the doctrine of learning (dhammamacchariyaṃ) is avarice regarding the doctrine. A monk endowed with that does not give [the doctrine] to another, [thinking], "Having learned this doctrine, this one will surpass me." However, if one does not give out of consideration for the doctrine or out of consideration for individuals, that is not avarice.

Because they obstruct and completely bind the mind, [they are called] hindrances (nīvaraṇāni). Sensual desire that has attained the state of a hindrance is overcome by the path of arhatship. The underlying tendency of sensual desire that has attained the state of a fetter of sensual desire is overcome by the path of a non-returner (anāgāmi). Thina is sickness of the mind. Middha is sickness of the three aggregates.
Both are excluded by the path of Arhatship.

Likewise, agitatedness (uddhaccaṃ).

Worry (kukkuccaṃ) is excluded by the path of non-returning.

Uncertainty (vicikicchā) is excluded by the first path.

Hindrances (nīvaraṇāni).

Fetters (saṃyojanāni) are bondages (bandhanāni).

Now, among those individuals bound by them, those reborn in the realm of form and the formless realm, stream-enterers and once-returners are called inwardly-bound and outwardly-reclining.

For them, there is bondage in the realm of desire (kāmabhave bandhanaṃ).

In the realm of desire, non-returners are called outwardly-bound and inwardly-reclining.

For them, there is bondage in the realm of form and the formless realm.

In the realm of desire, stream-enterers and once-returners are called inwardly-bound and inwardly-reclining.

In the realm of form and the formless realm, non-returners are called outwardly-bound and outwardly-reclining.

A perfected one (khīṇāsavo) is unbound in every way.

Fetters (saṃyojanāni).

A thing to be trained in is a precept of training (sikkhāpadaṃ); that is, it is a section of training.

Or, a step of training (sikkhāpadaṃ) is a means to achieve higher mind and higher wisdom training.

This is a brief account (ayamettha saṅkhepo).

But, in detail, the discussion of the training precepts comes in the Sikkhāpadavibhaṅga in the Vibhaṅga treatise.

Precept of training (sikkhāpadaṃ),

Explanation of the five states of being incapable, etc.

316. ‘‘Pañca abhabbaṭṭhānāni. Abhabbo, āvuso, khīṇāsavo bhikkhu sañcicca pāṇaṃ jīvitā voropetuṃ. Abhabbo khīṇāsavo bhikkhu adinnaṃ theyyasaṅkhātaṃ ādiyituṃ ādātuṃ (syā. kaṃ. pī.). Abhabbo khīṇāsavo bhikkhu methunaṃ dhammaṃ paṭisevituṃ. Abhabbo khīṇāsavo bhikkhu sampajānamusā bhāsituṃ. Abhabbo khīṇāsavo bhikkhu sannidhikārakaṃ kāme paribhuñjituṃ, seyyathāpi pubbe āgārikabhūto. ‘‘Pañca byasanāni – ñātibyasanaṃ, bhogabyasanaṃ, rogabyasanaṃ, sīlabyasanaṃ, diṭṭhibyasanaṃ. Nāvuso, sattā ñātibyasanahetu vā bhogabyasanahetu vā rogabyasanahetu vā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā apāyaṃ duggatiṃ vinipātaṃ nirayaṃ upapajjanti. Sīlabyasanahetu vā, āvuso, sattā diṭṭhibyasanahetu vā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā apāyaṃ duggatiṃ vinipātaṃ nirayaṃ upapajjanti. byasanāni – ‘‘Pañca sampadā – ñātisampadā, bhogasampadā, ārogyasampadā, sīlasampadā, diṭṭhisampadā. Nāvuso, sattā ñātisampadāhetu vā bhogasampadāhetu vā ārogyasampadāhetu vā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā sugatiṃ saggaṃ lokaṃ upapajjanti. Sīlasampadāhetu vā, āvuso, sattā diṭṭhisampadāhetu vā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā sugatiṃ saggaṃ lokaṃ upapajjanti. sampadā – ‘‘Pañca ādīnavā dussīlassa sīlavipattiyā. Idhāvuso , dussīlo sīlavipanno pamādādhikaraṇaṃ mahatiṃ bhogajāniṃ nigacchati, ayaṃ paṭhamo ādīnavo dussīlassa sīlavipattiyā. Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, dussīlassa sīlavipannassa pāpako kittisaddo abbhuggacchati, ayaṃ dutiyo ādīnavo dussīlassa sīlavipattiyā. Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, dussīlo sīlavipanno yaññadeva parisaṃ upasaṅkamati yadi khattiyaparisaṃ yadi brāhmaṇaparisaṃ yadi gahapatiparisaṃ yadi samaṇaparisaṃ, avisārado upasaṅkamati maṅkubhūto, ayaṃ tatiyo ādīnavo dussīlassa sīlavipattiyā. Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, dussīlo sīlavipanno sammūḷho kālaṃ karoti, ayaṃ catuttho ādīnavo dussīlassa sīlavipattiyā. Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, dussīlo sīlavipanno kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā apāyaṃ duggatiṃ vinipātaṃ nirayaṃ upapajjati, ayaṃ pañcamo ādīnavo dussīlassa sīlavipattiyā. ādīnavā dussīlassa sīlavipattiyā. ‘‘Pañca ānisaṃsā sīlavato sīlasampadāya. Idhāvuso, sīlavā sīlasampanno appamādādhikaraṇaṃ mahantaṃ bhogakkhandhaṃ adhigacchati, ayaṃ paṭhamo ānisaṃso sīlavato sīlasampadāya. Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, sīlavato sīlasampannassa kalyāṇo kittisaddo abbhuggacchati, ayaṃ dutiyo ānisaṃso sīlavato sīlasampadāya. Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, sīlavā sīlasampanno yaññadeva parisaṃ upasaṅkamati yadi khattiyaparisaṃ yadi brāhmaṇaparisaṃ yadi gahapatiparisaṃ yadi samaṇaparisaṃ, visārado upasaṅkamati amaṅkubhūto, ayaṃ tatiyo ānisaṃso sīlavato sīlasampadāya. Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, sīlavā sīlasampanno asammūḷho kālaṃ karoti, ayaṃ catuttho ānisaṃso sīlavato sīlasampadāya. Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, sīlavā sīlasampanno kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā sugatiṃ saggaṃ lokaṃ upapajjati, ayaṃ pañcamo ānisaṃso sīlavato sīlasampadāya. ānisaṃsā sīlavato sīlasampadāya. ‘‘Codakena , āvuso, bhikkhunā paraṃ codetukāmena pañca dhamme ajjhattaṃ upaṭṭhapetvā paro codetabbo. Kālena vakkhāmi no akālena, bhūtena vakkhāmi no abhūtena, saṇhena vakkhāmi no pharusena, atthasaṃhitena vakkhāmi no anatthasaṃhitena, mettacittena mettācittena (katthaci) vakkhāmi no dosantarenāti. Codakena, āvuso, bhikkhunā paraṃ codetukāmena ime pañca dhamme ajjhattaṃ upaṭṭhapetvā paro codetabbo. mettācittena (katthaci)
316.
“Five impossible things.
It is impossible, friends, for a monk whose effluents are ended to intentionally deprive a living being of life.
It is impossible for a monk whose effluents are ended to take, in the manner of stealing, what is not given.
It is impossible for a monk whose effluents are ended to engage in sexual intercourse.
It is impossible for a monk whose effluents are ended to tell a conscious lie.
It is impossible for a monk whose effluents are ended to consume stored-up sensual things as he did before, when he was a householder.

“Five kinds of loss: loss of relatives ( jñātivyasana), loss of wealth (bhogavyasana), loss through disease (rogavyasana), loss in terms of virtue (śīlavyasana), loss in terms of views (dṛṣṭivyasanāni).
Friends, it’s not by reason of loss of relatives, loss of wealth, or loss through disease that beings—with the break-up of the body, after death—reappear in a plane of deprivation, a bad destination, a lower realm, hell.
It’s by reason of loss in terms of virtue (śīlavyasana) and loss in terms of views (dṛṣṭivyasanāni) that beings—with the break-up of the body, after death—reappear in a plane of deprivation, a bad destination, a lower realm, hell.

“Five ways of being consummate: being consummate in terms of relatives (jñātisampadā), being consummate in terms of wealth (bhogasampadā), being consummate in terms of freedom from disease (ārogyasampadā), being consummate in terms of virtue (śīlasampadā), being consummate in terms of views (dṛṣṭhisampadā).
Friends, it’s not by reason of being consummate in terms of relatives, being consummate in terms of wealth, or being consummate in terms of freedom from disease that beings—with the break-up of the body, after death—reappear in a good destination, a heavenly world.
