Aṭṭhānametaṃ, āvuso, anavakāso, yaṃ upekkhāya cetovimuttiyā bhāvitāya bahulīkatāya yānīkatāya vatthukatāya anuṭṭhitāya paricitāya susamāraddhāya, atha ca panassa rāgo cittaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassati netaṃ ṭhānaṃ vijjati. Nissaraṇaṃ hetaṃ, āvuso, rāgassa, yadidaṃ upekkhā cetovimuttī’ti. ‘‘Idha panāvuso, bhikkhu evaṃ vadeyya – ‘animittā hi kho me cetovimutti bhāvitā bahulīkatā yānīkatā vatthukatā anuṭṭhitā paricitā susamāraddhā. Atha ca pana me nimittānusāri viññāṇaṃ hotī’ti. So ‘mā hevaṃ’ tissa vacanīyo ‘māyasmā evaṃ avaca, mā bhagavantaṃ abbhācikkhi, na hi sādhu bhagavato abbhakkhānaṃ, na hi bhagavā evaṃ vadeyya. Aṭṭhānametaṃ, āvuso, anavakāso, yaṃ animittāya cetovimuttiyā bhāvitāya bahulīkatāya yānīkatāya vatthukatāya anuṭṭhitāya paricitāya susamāraddhāya, atha ca panassa nimittānusāri viññāṇaṃ bhavissati, netaṃ ṭhānaṃ vijjati. Nissaraṇaṃ hetaṃ, āvuso, sabbanimittānaṃ, yadidaṃ animittā cetovimuttī’ti. ‘‘Idha panāvuso, bhikkhu evaṃ vadeyya – ‘asmīti kho me vigataṃ vighātaṃ (sī. pī.), vigate (syā. ka.), ayamahamasmīti na samanupassāmi, atha ca pana me vicikicchākathaṅkathāsallaṃ cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhatī’ti. So ‘mā hevaṃ’ tissa vacanīyo ‘māyasmā evaṃ avaca, mā bhagavantaṃ abbhācikkhi, na hi sādhu bhagavato abbhakkhānaṃ, na hi bhagavā evaṃ vadeyya. Aṭṭhānametaṃ, āvuso, anavakāso, yaṃ asmīti vigate vighāte (sī. pī.) ayamahamasmīti asamanupassato, atha ca panassa vicikicchākathaṅkathāsallaṃ cittaṃ pariyādāya ṭhassati, netaṃ ṭhānaṃ vijjati. Nissaraṇaṃ hetaṃ, āvuso, vicikicchākathaṅkathāsallassa, yadidaṃ asmimānasamugghāto’ti. vighātaṃ (sī. pī.), vigate (syā. ka.) vighāte (sī. pī.)
326. “There are these six elements of escape.

Here, friends, a bhikkhu might say, ‘Goodwill (mettā) as an awareness-release (cetovimutti) has been developed, pursued, handed the reins, taken as a basis, steadied, consolidated, & well-undertaken by me. Still, ill will (byāpādo) keeps overpowering my mind.’

He should be told, ‘Don’t say that. You shouldn’t speak in that way. Don’t misrepresent the Blessed One, for it’s not right to misrepresent the Blessed One, and the Blessed One wouldn’t say that. It’s impossible, there is no way that—when goodwill as an awareness-release has been developed, pursued, handed the reins, taken as a basis, steadied, consolidated, & well-undertaken— ill will would still keep overpowering the mind. That possibility doesn’t exist, for this is the escape from ill will: goodwill as an awareness-release.’

“And further, friends, a bhikkhu might say, ‘Compassion (karuṇā) as an awareness-release has been developed, pursued, handed the reins, taken as a basis, steadied, consolidated, & well-undertaken by me. Still, harmfulness (vihesā) keeps overpowering my mind.’ He should be told, ‘Don’t say that. You shouldn’t speak in that way. Don’t misrepresent the Blessed One, for it’s not right to misrepresent the Blessed One, and the Blessed One wouldn’t say that. It’s impossible, there is no way that—when compassion as an awareness-release has been developed, pursued, handed the reins, taken as a basis, steadied, consolidated, & well-undertaken— harmfulness would still keep overpowering the mind. That possibility doesn’t exist, for this is the escape from harmfulness: compassion as an awareness-release.’

“And further, friends, a bhikkhu might say, ‘Empathetic joy (muditā) as an awareness-release has been developed, pursued, handed the reins, taken as a basis, steadied, consolidated, & well-undertaken by me. Still, resentment (arati) keeps overpowering my mind.’ He should be told, ‘Don’t say that. You shouldn’t speak in that way. Don’t misrepresent the Blessed One, for it’s not right to misrepresent the Blessed One, and the Blessed One wouldn’t say that. It’s impossible, there is no way that—when empathetic joy as an awareness-release has been developed, pursued, handed the reins, taken as a basis, steadied, consolidated, & well-undertaken— resentment would still keep overpowering the mind. That possibility doesn’t exist, for this is the escape from resentment: empathetic joy as an awareness-release.’

“And further, friends, a bhikkhu might say, ‘Equanimity (upekkhā) as an awareness-release has been developed, pursued, handed the reins, taken as a basis, steadied, consolidated, & well undertaken by me, still passion (rāga) keeps overpowering my mind.’ He should be told, ‘Don’t say that. You shouldn’t speak in that way. Don’t misrepresent the Blessed One, for it’s not right to misrepresent the Blessed One, and the Blessed One wouldn’t say that. It’s impossible, there is no way that—when equanimity as an awareness-release has been developed, pursued, handed the reins, taken as a basis, steadied, consolidated, & well-undertaken— passion would still keep overpowering the mind. That possibility doesn’t exist, for this is the escape from passion: equanimity as an awareness-release.’

“And further, friends, a bhikkhu might say, ‘The signless (animittā) as an awareness-release has been developed, pursued, handed the reins, taken as a basis, steadied, consolidated, & well undertaken by me, still my consciousness (viññāṇa) follows the drift of signs.’ He should be told, ‘Don’t say that. You shouldn’t speak in that way. Don’t misrepresent the Blessed One, for it’s not right to misrepresent the Blessed One, and the Blessed One wouldn’t say that. It’s impossible, there is no way that—when the signless as an awareness-release has been developed, pursued, handed the reins, taken as a basis, steadied, consolidated, & well-undertaken— consciousness would follow the drift of signs. That possibility doesn’t exist, for this is the escape from all signs: the signless as an awareness-release.’

“And further, friends, a bhikkhu might say, ‘The notion “I am” has been eliminatedpī.
), vigate (syā.
ka.

He should be told, 'Don't say that. Venerable, you shouldn’t speak in that way. Don’t misrepresent the Blessed One, for it’s not right to misrepresent the Blessed One, and the Blessed One would never say such a thing.

326.Nissaraṇiyā dhātuyoti nissaṭadhātuyova. Pariyādāya tiṭṭhatīti pariyādiyitvā hāpetvā tiṭṭhati. ‘Mā hevantissa vacanīyo’ti yasmā abhūtaṃ byākaraṇaṃ byākaroti, tasmā mā evaṃ bhaṇīti vattabbo. Yadidaṃ mettācetovimuttīti yā ayaṃ mettācetovimutti, idaṃ nissaraṇaṃ byāpādassa, byāpādato nissaṭāti attho. Yo pana mettāya tikacatukkajjhānato vuṭṭhito saṅkhāre sammasitvā tatiyamaggaṃ patvā ‘‘puna byāpādo natthī’’ti tatiyaphalena nibbānaṃ passati, tassa cittaṃ accantaṃ nissaraṇaṃ byāpādassa. Etenupāyena sabbattha attho veditabbo. Animittācetovimuttīti arahattaphalasamāpatti. Sā hi rāganimittādīnañceva rūpanimittādīnañca niccanimittādīnañca abhāvā ‘‘animittā’’ti vuttā. Nimittānusārīti vuttappabhedaṃ nimittaṃ anusaratīti nimittānusārī. Animittā cetovimuttī Nimittānusārī Asmīti asmimāno. Ayamahamasmīti pañcasu khandhesu ayaṃ nāma ahaṃ asmīti ettāvatā arahattaṃ byākataṃ hoti. Vicikicchākathaṃkathāsallanti vicikicchābhūtaṃ kathaṃkathāsallaṃ. ‘Mā hevantissa vacanīyo’ti sace te paṭhamamaggavajjhā vicikicchā uppajjati, arahattabyākaraṇaṃ micchā hoti, tasmā mā abhūtaṃ bhaṇīti vāretabbo. Asmimānasamugghātoti arahattamaggo. Arahattamaggaphalavasena hi nibbāne diṭṭhe puna asmimāno natthīti arahattamaggo asmimānasamugghātoti vutto. Asmī Ayamahamasmī Vicikicchākathaṃkathāsalla Asmimānasamugghāto Anuttariyādichakkavaṇṇanā
326.
“Nissaraṇīyā dhātuyo” means nissaṭadhātuyova.
"Pariyādāya tiṭṭhatī" means pariyādiyitvā hāpetvā tiṭṭhati.
“'He should not be told, mā hevantissa vacanīyo'” means because he declares an untruthful declaration, therefore he should be prevented by saying, "Do not speak thus," p. 3.
0219 Vattabbo.
“Yadidaṃ mettācetovimuttī” means that which is loving-kindness, liberation of mind, this is escape from malice, the meaning is: escaped from malice.
Moreover, whoever, having arisen from loving-kindness, the triple and quadruple jhānas, having contemplated the saṅkhāras, having attained the third path, sees nirvāṇa with the fruit of the third [path], [thinking,] “Again, there is no malice,” that person's mind is utterly free from malice.
By this method, the meaning should be understood everywhere.

"Animittā," p. 3.
0200 "cetovimuttī" means the attainment of the fruit of arhatship.
For, since there is an absence of signs of passion, etc., and of signs of form, etc., and of signs of permanence, etc., therefore it is said to be "animittā."
"Nimittānusārī" means one who follows the sign of the aforesaid kind; therefore, [he is called] nimittānusārī.

Animittā, p. 3.
0200 cetovimuttī nimittānusārī
"Asmī" means asmimāna.
"Ayamahamasmī" means that by this much, “This name is I,” in the five aggregates, arhatship is declared.
"Vicikicchākathaṃkathāsalla" means doubt that has become questioning-doubt.
“'He should not be told, mā hevantissa vacanīyo'” means if doubt arises in you, the destroyer of the first path, the declaration of arhatship is false; therefore, he should be prevented by saying, “Do not speak what is untrue.”
"Asmimānasamugghāto" means the path of arhatship.
Indeed, because upon seeing nirvāṇa through the power of the path and fruit of arhatship, there is no more self-conceit, therefore the path of arhatship is said to be asmimānasamugghāto.

Asmī ayamahamasmī vicikicchākathaṃkathāsalla asmimānasamugghāto
Explanation of the sixfold unsurpassed, etc.
327
327. ‘‘Cha anuttariyāni – dassanānuttariyaṃ, savanānuttariyaṃ, lābhānuttariyaṃ, sikkhānuttariyaṃ, pāricariyānuttariyaṃ, anussatānuttariyaṃ. ‘‘Cha anussatiṭṭhānāni – buddhānussati, dhammānussati, saṅghānussati, sīlānussati, cāgānussati, devatānussati. anussatiṭṭhānāni
327. Six unsurpassed things (anuttariyāni): unsurpassed vision (dassanānuttariyaṃ), unsurpassed hearing (savanānuttariyaṃ), unsurpassed gains (lābhānuttariyaṃ), unsurpassed training (sikkhānuttariyaṃ), unsurpassed service (pāricariyānuttariyaṃ), unsurpassed objects of recollection (anussatānuttariyaṃ).

Six objects of recollection (anussatiṭṭhānāni): recollection of the Buddha (buddhānussati), recollection of the Dharma (dhammānussati), recollection of the Saṅgha (saṅghānussati), recollection of virtue (sīlānussati), recollection of generosity (cāgānussati), recollection of the deities (devatānussati).
327.Anuttariyānīti anuttarāni jeṭṭhakāni. Dassanesu anuttariyaṃ dassanānuttariyaṃ. Sesapadesupi eseva nayo. Tattha hatthiratanādīnaṃ dassanaṃ na dassanānuttariyaṃ, niviṭṭhasaddhassa pana niviṭṭhapemavasena dasabalassa vā bhikkhusaṅghassa vā kasiṇāsubhanimittādīnaṃ vā aññatarassa dassanaṃ dassanānuttariyaṃ nāma. Khattiyādīnaṃ guṇakathāsavanaṃ na savanānuttariyaṃ, niviṭṭhasaddhassa pana niviṭṭhapemavasena tiṇṇaṃ vā ratanānaṃ guṇakathāsavanaṃ tepiṭakabuddhavacanasavanaṃ vā savanānuttariyaṃ nāma. Maṇiratanādilābho na lābhānuttariyaṃ, sattavidhaariyadhanalābho pana lābhānuttariyaṃ nāma. Hatthisippādisikkhanaṃ na sikkhānuttariyaṃ, sikkhattayapūraṇaṃ pana sikkhānuttariyaṃ nāma. Khattiyādīnaṃ pāricariyā na pāricariyānuttariyaṃ, tiṇṇaṃ pana ratanānaṃ pāricariyā pāricariyānuttariyaṃ nāma. Khattiyādīnaṃ guṇānussaraṇaṃ nānussatānuttariyaṃ, tiṇṇaṃ pana ratanānaṃ guṇānussaraṇaṃ anussatānuttariyaṃ nāma. Anussatiyova anussatiṭṭhānāni nāma. Buddhānussatīti buddhassa guṇānussaraṇaṃ. Evaṃ anussarato hi pīti uppajjati. So taṃ pītiṃ khayato vayato paṭṭhapetvā arahattaṃ pāpuṇāti. Upacārakammaṭṭhānaṃ nāmetaṃ gihīnampi labbhati, esa nayo sabbattha. Vitthārakathā panettha visuddhimagge vuttanayeneva veditabbā. anussatiṭṭhānāni Buddhānussatī Satatavihārachakkavaṇṇanā
327. " Anuttariyānī " thus. /Page 3.1037/ Highest, pre-eminent. In regard to sights, the highest sight, dassanānuttariyaṃ. This same method applies to the remaining terms as well. Therein, the sight of elephants, jewels, and so on, is not the highest sight. But for one whose faith is firmly established, due to firm affection, the sight of the Ten-Powered One (Daśabala), or of the monastic community (bhikkhusaṅgha), or of one of the kasiṇa signs, the impure signs (asubhanimitta), and so on, is called the highest sight, dassanānuttariyaṃ . Hearing tales of the virtues of Kṣatriyas and so forth is not the highest hearing. But for one whose faith is firmly established, due to firm affection, hearing tales of the virtues of the Three Jewels (triratna), or hearing the words of the Buddha in the Tipiṭaka, is called the highest hearing, savanānuttariyaṃ. Gaining jewels, gems, and so on is not the highest gain. But gaining the seven kinds of wealth of the noble ones (sattavidhaariyadhana) is called the highest gain, lābhānuttariyaṃ. Learning elephant lore and so on is not the highest learning. But perfecting the three trainings (sikkhattaya) is called the highest learning, sikkhānuttariyaṃ. Service to Kṣatriyas and so forth is not the highest service. But service to the Three Jewels is called the highest service, pāricariyānuttariyaṃ. Recollecting the good qualities of Kṣatriyas and so forth is not the highest recollection. But recollecting the good qualities of the Three Jewels is called the highest recollection, anussatānuttariyaṃ.

The foundations of mindfulness (anussatiṭṭhānāni) are mindfulness itself. /Page 3.0220/ Buddhānussatī means the recollection of the Buddha’s qualities (buddhasya guṇānussaraṇaṃ). When one recollects thus, joy (pīti) arises. Having established that joy in terms of its arising and passing away (khayato vayato paṭṭhapetvā), he attains Arhatship (arahattaṃ pāpuṇāti). This practice of approach (upacārakammaṭṭhānaṃ) is attainable even by householders (gihīnampi labbhati); this is the method everywhere (esa nayo sabbattha). The detailed exposition (vitthārakathā) here should be understood according to the method stated in the Visuddhimagga (visuddhimagge vuttanayeneva veditabbā).

/Page 3.0220/ The Foundations of Mindfulness (anussatiṭṭhānāni), Mindfulness of the Buddha (buddhānussatī). Explanation of the Six Causes of Constant Dwelling (satatavihārachakkavaṇṇanā).
328
328. ‘‘Cha satatavihārā. Idhāvuso, bhikkhu cakkhunā rūpaṃ disvā neva sumano hoti na dummano, upekkhako upekkhako ca (syā. ka.) viharati sato sampajāno. Sotena saddaṃ sutvā…pe… manasā dhammaṃ viññāya neva sumano hoti na dummano, upekkhako viharati sato sampajāno.
328.
’’Six constant abidings.
Here, friend, a bhikṣu, having seen a form with the eye (cakṣunā rūpaṃ disvā), is neither gladdened (sumano hoti) nor saddened (dummano), but abides in equanimity (upekkhako), mindful (sato) and fully aware (sampajāno).
Having heard a sound with the ear… Having cognized a mental phenomenon with the mind (manasā dhammaṃ viññāya), he is neither joyful nor saddened, but dwells equanimous (upekkhako), mindful (sato) and clearly comprehending (sampajāno).
328.Satatavihārāti khīṇāsavassa niccavihārā. Cakkhunā rūpaṃ disvāti cakkhudvārārammaṇe āpāthagate taṃ rūpaṃ cakkhuviññāṇena disvā javanakkhaṇe iṭṭhe arajjanto neva sumano hoti, aniṭṭhe adussanto na dummano. Asamapekkhane mohaṃ anuppādento upekkhako viharati majjhatto, satiyā yuttattā sato, sampajaññena yuttattā sampajāno. Sesapadesupi eseva nayo. Iti chasupi dvāresu upekkhako viharatīti iminā chaḷaṅgupekkhā kathitā. Sampajānoti vacanato pana cattāri ñāṇasampayuttacittāni labbhanti. Satatavihārāti vacanato aṭṭhapi mahācittāni labbhanti arajjanto adussantoti vacanato dasapi cittāni labbhanti. Somanassaṃ kathaṃ labbhatīti ce āsevanato labbhati. Abhijātichakkavaṇṇanā
328.
"Satatavihārāti" khīṇāsava's constant dwelling.
"Cakkhunā rūpaṃ disvāti" Having seen a form with the eye, at the moment of swiftness, not being attached to what is desirable, "neva sumano hoti," not being hateful towards what is undesirable, "na dummano."
Not producing delusion in relation to asamapekkhane, "upekkhako viharati" he dwells with equanimity (majjhatta), endowed with mindfulness (satiyā yuttattā), "sato," endowed with clear comprehension (sampajaññena yuttattā), "sampajāno."
And so with the remaining words.
Thus, with these six doors, it is said that dwelling with equanimity is chaḷaṅgupekkhā.
From the saying "sampajānoti," four consciousnesses associated with knowledge (jñāna) are obtained.
From the saying "satatavihārāti," eight great consciousnesses (mahācitta) are obtained, and from the saying "arajjanto adussantoti," ten consciousnesses (citta) are obtained.
How is joy (somanassa) obtained? If it is, it is obtained from cultivation (āsevanato).

Abhijātichakkavaṇṇanā
329
329.‘‘Chaḷābhijātiyo. Idhāvuso, ekacco kaṇhābhijātiko samāno kaṇhaṃ dhammaṃ abhijāyati. Idha panāvuso, ekacco kaṇhābhijātiko samāno sukkaṃ dhammaṃ abhijāyati. Idha panāvuso, ekacco kaṇhābhijātiko samāno akaṇhaṃ asukkaṃ nibbānaṃ abhijāyati. Idha panāvuso, ekacco sukkābhijātiko samāno sukkaṃ dhammaṃ abhijāyati. Idha panāvuso, ekacco sukkābhijātiko samāno kaṇhaṃ dhammaṃ abhijāyati. Idha panāvuso, ekacco sukkābhijātiko samāno akaṇhaṃ asukkaṃ nibbānaṃ abhijāyati. ‘‘Cha nibbedhabhāgiyā saññā nibbedhabhāgiyasaññā (syā. kaṃ.) – aniccasaññā anicce, dukkhasaññā dukkhe, anattasaññā, pahānasaññā, virāgasaññā, nirodhasaññā. nibbedhabhāgiyā nibbedhabhāgiyasaññā (syā. kaṃ.) ‘‘Ime kho, āvuso, tena bhagavatā jānatā passatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena cha dhammā sammadakkhātā; tattha sabbeheva saṅgāyitabbaṃ…pe… atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ. Sattakaṃ
329. “Six kinds of birth.

“Here, friends, one born in darkness gives birth to dark qualities.

“Again, friends, one born in darkness gives birth to bright qualities.

“Again, friends, one born in darkness gives birth to unbinding, neither dark nor bright (nirvāṇa).

“Again, friends, one born in brightness gives birth to bright qualities.

“Again, friends, one born in brightness gives birth to dark qualities.

“Again, friends, one born in brightness gives birth to unbinding, neither dark nor bright (nirvāṇa).

“Six perceptions conducive to penetration (nibbedhabhāgiyā saññā) – the perception of impermanence (aniccasaññā) in what is impermanent, the perception of suffering (dukkhasaññā) in what is suffering, the perception of not-self (anattasaññā), the perception of abandoning (pahānasaññā), the perception of dispassion (virāgasaññā), the perception of cessation (nirodhasaññā).

“These, friends, are the six dharmas that are rightly expounded by the Blessed One—the one who knows, the one who sees, the Worthy One (arhat), rightly self-awakened (sammāsambuddha). Those should all be recited together… for the benefit and happiness of gods and humans (devamanussānaṃ).

The chapter on Sevens.
329.Abhijātiyoti jātiyo. Kaṇhābhijātiko samānoti kaṇhe nīcakule jāto hutvā. Kaṇhaṃ dhammaṃ abhijāyatīti kāḷakaṃ dasadussīlyadhammaṃ pasavati karoti. So taṃ abhijāyitvā niraye nibbattati. Sukkaṃ dhammanti ahaṃ pubbepi puññānaṃ akatattā nīcakule nibbatto. Idāni puññaṃ karomīti puññasaṅkhātaṃ paṇḍaraṃ dhammaṃ abhijāyati. So tena sagge nibbattati. Akaṇhaṃ asukkaṃ nibbānanti nibbānañhi sace kaṇhaṃ bhaveyya, kaṇhavipākaṃ dadeyya. Sace sukkaṃ, sukkavipākaṃ dadeyya. Dvinnampi appadānato pana ‘‘akaṇhaṃ asukka’’nti vuttaṃ. Nibbānañca nāma imasmiṃ atthe arahattaṃ adhippetaṃ. Tañhi kilesanibbānante jātattā nibbānaṃ nāma. Taṃ esa abhijāyati pasavati karoti. Sukkābhijātiko samānoti sukke uccakule jāto hutvā. Sesaṃ vuttanayeneva veditabbaṃ. Nibbedhabhāgiyachakkavaṇṇanā Nibbedhabhāgiyāti nibbedho vuccati nibbānaṃ, taṃ bhajanti upagacchantīti nibbedhabhāgiyā. Aniccasaññādayo pañcake vuttā. Nirodhānupassanāñāṇe saññā nirodhasaññā nāma. Nibbedhabhāgiyā nibbedhabhāgiyā. nirodhasaññā ‘‘Ime kho, āvuso’’tiādi vuttanayeneva yojetabbaṃ. Iti dvāvīsatiyā chakkānaṃ vasena bāttiṃsasatapañhe kathento thero sāmaggirasaṃ dassesīti. Chakkavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā. Sattakavaṇṇanā
329. Abhijātiyo means births. Kaṇhābhijātiko samāno means having been born in a low, dark family. Kaṇhaṃ dhammaṃ abhijāyatī means he generates and does the dark ten unwholesome moral principles. Having generated that, he is reborn in hell. ukkaṃ dhammanti I was born in a low family because of not having done merit even before. Now I will do merit, he generates the white dharma counted as merit. By that he is reborn in heaven. Akaṇhaṃ asukkaṃ nibbānanti For if nirvāṇa were dark, it would give a dark result. If it were white, it would give a white result. But since it does not give either, it is said to be "neither dark nor white." And in this context, nirvāṇa means arahantship. For since it is born at the end of the destruction of defilements, it is called nirvāṇa. That, he generates, produces, and does. Sukkābhijātiko samāno means having been born in a high, white family. The rest should be understood in the way already described.

Explanation of the Group of Six that Leads to Penetration

Nibbedhabhāgiyāti nibbedho is called nirvāṇa. Those who cultivate and approach that are called nibbedhabhāgiyā. The perception of impermanence, etc., are stated in the set of five. In the knowledge of contemplating cessation, the perception is called nirodhasaññā.

Nibbedhabhāgiyā nibbedhabhāgiyā. Nirodhasaññā “ime kho, āvuso” etc., should be connected in the way already described. Thus, while the elder was speaking about the thirty-two questions based on the twenty-two sets of six, he showed the flavor of harmony.

The Explanation of the Sets of Six is Finished.

Explanation of the Sets of Seven
330
330. ‘‘Atthi kho, āvuso, tena bhagavatā jānatā passatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena satta dhammā sammadakkhātā; tattha sabbeheva saṅgāyitabbaṃ…pe… atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ. Katame satta? ‘‘Satta ariyadhanāni – saddhādhanaṃ, sīladhanaṃ, hiridhanaṃ, ottappadhanaṃ, sutadhanaṃ, cāgadhanaṃ, paññādhanaṃ. ariyadhanāni – ‘‘Satta bojjhaṅgā – satisambojjhaṅgo, dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgo , vīriyasambojjhaṅgo, pītisambojjhaṅgo, passaddhisambojjhaṅgo, samādhisambojjhaṅgo, upekkhāsambojjhaṅgo. bojjhaṅgā – ‘‘Satta samādhiparikkhārā – sammādiṭṭhi, sammāsaṅkappo, sammāvācā, sammākammanto, sammāājīvo, sammāvāyāmo, sammāsati. samādhiparikkhārā – ‘‘Satta asaddhammā – idhāvuso, bhikkhu assaddho hoti, ahiriko hoti, anottappī hoti, appassuto hoti, kusīto hoti, muṭṭhassati hoti, duppañño hoti. asaddhammā – ‘‘Satta saddhammā – idhāvuso, bhikkhu saddho hoti, hirimā hoti, ottappī hoti, bahussuto hoti, āraddhavīriyo hoti, upaṭṭhitassati hoti, paññavā hoti. saddhammā – ‘‘Satta sappurisadhammā – idhāvuso, bhikkhu dhammaññū ca hoti atthaññū ca attaññū ca mattaññū ca kālaññū ca parisaññū ca puggalaññū ca. sappurisadhammā –
“Friends, these are the seven dhammas that are rightly expounded by the Blessed One—the one who knows, the one who sees, the Worthy One, rightly self-awakened. Those should all be recited together…so that this will be for the benefit & happiness of devas & human beings. What are the seven?

“Seven noble treasures (ariyadhanāni): the treasure of faith (saddhādhanaṃ), the treasure of moral conduct (sīladhanaṃ), the treasure of conscience (hiridhanaṃ), the treasure of concern (ottappadhanaṃ), the treasure of learning (sutadhanaṃ), the treasure of generosity (cāgadhanaṃ), and the treasure of wisdom (paññādhanaṃ).

“Seven factors of enlightenment (bojjhaṅgā): mindfulness as a factor of enlightenment (satisambojjhaṅgo), investigation of qualities as a factor of enlightenment (dhammavicayasambojjhaṅgo), persistence as a factor of enlightenment (vīriyasambojjhaṅgo), rapture as a factor of enlightenment (pītisambojjhaṅgo), calm as a factor of enlightenment (passaddhisambojjhaṅgo), concentration as a factor of enlightenment (samādhisambojjhaṅgo), and equanimity as a factor of enlightenment (upekkhāsambojjhaṅgo).

“Seven accessories to concentration (samādhiparikkhārā): right view (sammādiṭṭhi), right thought (sammāsaṅkappo), right speech (sammāvācā), right action (sammākammanto), right livelihood (sammāājīvo), right effort (sammāvāyāmo), and right mindfulness (sammāsati).

“Seven unwholesome qualities (asaddhammā): Here, friends, a bhikkhu is faithless (assaddho), without conscience (ahiriko), without concern (anottappī), unlearned (appassuto), lazy (kusīto), unmindful (muṭṭhassati), and unwise (duppañño).

“Seven wholesome qualities (saddhammā): Here, friends, a bhikkhu has faith (saddho), has conscience (hirimā), has concern (ottappī), is learned (bahussuto), energetic (āraddhavīriyo), mindful (upaṭṭhitassati), and wise (paññavā).

“Seven qualities of a true person (sappurisadhammā): Here, friends, a bhikkhu knows the Dhamma (dhammaññū), knows the meaning (atthaññū), knows himself (attaññū), knows moderation (mattaññū), knows the proper time (kālaññū), knows the assembly (parisaññū), and knows individuals (puggalaññū).
330. Iti chakkavasena sāmaggirasaṃ dassetvā idāni sattakavasena dassetuṃ puna desanaṃ ārabhi. Tattha sampattipaṭilābhaṭṭhena saddhāva dhanaṃ saddhādhanaṃ. Esa nayo sabbattha. Paññādhanaṃ panettha sabbaseṭṭhaṃ. Paññāya hi ṭhatvā tīṇi sucaritāni pañcasīlāni dasasīlāni pūretvā saggūpagā honti, sāvakapāramīñāṇaṃ, paccekabodhiñāṇaṃ, sabbaññutaññāṇañca paṭivijjhanti. Imāsaṃ sampattīnaṃ paṭilābhakāraṇato paññā ‘‘dhana’’nti vuttā. Sattapi cetāni lokiyalokuttaramissakāneva kathitāni. Bojjhaṅgakathā kathitāva. saddhādhanaṃ. Paññādhanaṃ Bojjhaṅga Samādhiparikkhārāti samādhiparivārā. Sammādiṭṭhādīni vuttatthāneva. Imepi satta parikkhārā lokiyalokuttarāva kathitā. Samādhiparikkhārā Asataṃ dhammā asantā vā dhammā lāmakā dhammāti asaddhammā. Vipariyāyena saddhammā veditabbā. Sesamettha uttānatthameva. Saddhammesu pana saddhādayo sabbepi vipassakasseva kathitā. Tesupi paññā lokiyalokuttarā. Ayaṃ viseso. asaddhammā. saddhammā Saddhammesu Sappurisānaṃ dhammāti sappurisadhammā. Tattha suttageyyādikaṃ dhammaṃ jānātīti dhammaññū. Tassa tasseva bhāsitassa atthaṃ jānātīti atthaññū. ‘‘Ettakomhi sīlena samādhinā paññāyā’’ti evaṃ attānaṃ jānātīti attaññū. Paṭiggahaṇaparibhogesu mattaṃ jānātīti mattaññū. Ayaṃ kālo uddesassa, ayaṃ kālo paripucchāya, ayaṃ kālo yogassa adhigamāyāti evaṃ kālaṃ jānātīti kālaññū. Ettha ca pañca vassāni uddesassa kālo. Dasa paripucchāya. Idaṃ atisambādhaṃ. Dasa vassāni pana uddesassa kālo. Vīsati paripucchāya. Tato paraṃ yoge kammaṃ kātabbaṃ. Aṭṭhavidhaṃ parisaṃ jānātīti parisaññū. Sevitabbāsevitabbaṃ puggalaṃ jānātīti puggalaññū. sappurisadhammā. dhammaññū. atthaññū. attaññū. mattaññū. kālaññū. parisaññū. puggalaññū.
330. Thus, having shown the taste of harmony by way of the six, now, in order to show [it] by way of the seven, he began the teaching again.

There, faith is wealth in the sense of attaining prosperity, [therefore] faith is called wealth ( śraddhādhana). This method [applies] everywhere. Wisdom (prajñā) is the best here. For, having stood in wisdom, having fulfilled the three kinds of good conduct, the five precepts, [and] the ten precepts, they go to heaven. They penetrate the knowledge of a disciple's perfection, the knowledge of a solitary Buddha, and the knowledge of omniscience. Because wisdom is the cause of attaining these prosperities, wisdom is called "wealth". These seven are also said to be a mixture of mundane and supramundane. The bojjhaṅga (factors of enlightenment) story has been told.

Faith as wealth (śraddhādhana). Wisdom as wealth (prajñādhana). Factors of enlightenment (bojjhaṅga). The requisites of concentration (samādhi-parikkhārā), and the retinue of concentration (samādhi-parivārā). Right view and the like are in the same place as stated. These seven requisites are also said to be mundane and supramundane.

Requisites of concentration (samādhi-parikkhārā). Asaddhammā: the dharmas of the untrue, the non-existent, or the base are unrighteous dharmas (asaddhammā). The righteous dharmas (saddhammā) are to be understood in the opposite way. The rest is clear here. Among righteous dharmas (saddhammesu), however, faith and the like are all said to belong to the one who sees clearly (vipassaka). Among them too, wisdom (prajñā) is mundane and supramundane. This is the distinction.

Unrighteous dharmas (asaddhammā). Righteous dharmas (saddhammā). Among righteous dharmas (saddhammesu). Sappurisadhammā: the dharmas of good people are the sappurisadhammā. There, he knows the dhamma, such as the sutta and geyya, [therefore] he is a knower of the dharma (dhammaññū). He knows the meaning of that very speech of his, [therefore] he is a knower of the meaning (atthaññū). "I am so much in ethics, concentration, [and] wisdom," thus he knows himself, [therefore] he is a knower of himself (attaññū). He knows the right measure in receiving and using, [therefore] he is a knower of the measure (mattaññū). This is the time for instruction, this is the time for questioning, this is the time for the attainment of yoga," thus he knows the time, [therefore] he is a knower of the time (kālaññū). And here, five years is the time for instruction. Ten [years is the time] for questioning. This is very crowded. Ten years, however, is the time for instruction. Twenty [years is the time] for questioning. After that, work should be done in yoga. He knows the eightfold assembly, [therefore] he is a knower of the assembly (parisaññū). He knows the person who should be served and the person who should not be served, [therefore] he is a knower of the person (puggalaññū).

Sappurisadhammā. Knower of the Dharma (dhammaññū).
Knowing the meaning (atthaññū).
Knowing oneself (attaññū).
Knowing moderation (mattaññū).
Knowing the proper time (kālaññū).
Knowing the assembly (parisaññū).
Knowing individuals (puggalaññū).
331
331. ‘‘Satta niddasavatthūni. Idhāvuso, bhikkhu sikkhāsamādāne tibbacchando hoti, āyatiñca sikkhāsamādāne avigatapemo. Dhammanisantiyā tibbacchando hoti, āyatiñca dhammanisantiyā avigatapemo. Icchāvinaye tibbacchando hoti, āyatiñca icchāvinaye avigatapemo. Paṭisallāne tibbacchando hoti, āyatiñca paṭisallāne avigatapemo. Vīriyārambhe tibbacchando hoti, āyatiñca vīriyārambhe avigatapemo. Satinepakke tibbacchando hoti, āyatiñca satinepakke avigatapemo . Diṭṭhipaṭivedhe tibbacchando hoti, āyatiñca diṭṭhipaṭivedhe avigatapemo. ‘‘Satta saññā – aniccasaññā, anattasaññā, asubhasaññā, ādīnavasaññā, pahānasaññā, virāgasaññā, nirodhasaññā. ‘‘Satta saññā – ‘‘Sattabalāni – saddhābalaṃ, vīriyabalaṃ, hiribalaṃ, ottappabalaṃ, satibalaṃ, samādhibalaṃ, paññābalaṃ. ‘‘Satta balāni –
331.
‘‘Seven grounds for [right] view.
There is the case, friends, where a monk has keen desire to undertake the training & has not discarded his love for undertaking the training in the future.
He has keen desire to attend carefully to the Dhamma…
He has keen desire to subdue his wants…
He has keen desire to find solitude…
He has keen desire to arouse persistence…
He has keen desire to be mindful & adept…
He has keen desire to penetrate view & has not discarded his love for penetrating view in the future.

‘‘Seven perceptions: the perception of impermanence (aniccasaññā), the perception of not-self (anattasaññā), the perception of unattractiveness (asubhasaññā), the perception of drawbacks (ādīnavasaññā), the perception of abandoning (pahānasaññā), the perception of dispassion (virāgasaññā), the perception of cessation (nirodhasaññā).

‘‘Seven strengths (balāni): the strength of conviction (saddhābalaṃ), the strength of persistence (vīriyabalaṃ), the strength of a sense of shame (hiribalaṃ), the strength of compunction (ottappabalaṃ), the strength of mindfulness (satibalaṃ), the strength of concentration (samādhibalaṃ), the strength of discernment (paññābalaṃ).
331.Niddasavatthūnīti niddasādivatthūni. Niddaso bhikkhu, nibbīso, nittiṃso, niccattālīso, nippaññāso bhikkhūti evaṃ vacanakāraṇāni. Ayaṃ kira pañho titthiyasamaye uppanno. Titthiyā hi dasavassakāle mataṃ nigaṇṭhaṃ niddasoti vadanti. So kira puna dasavasso na hoti. Na kevalañca dasavassova. Navavassopi…pe… ekavassopi na hoti. Eteneva nayena vīsativassādikālepi mataṃ nibbīso, nittiṃso, niccattālīso, nippaññāsoti vadanti. Āyasmā ānando gāme vicaranto taṃ kathaṃ sutvā vihāraṃ gantvā bhagavato ārocesi. Bhagavā āha – ‘‘Na idaṃ, ānanda, titthiyānaṃ adhivacanaṃ mama sāsane khīṇāsavassetaṃ adhivacanaṃ. Khīṇāsavo hi dasavassakāle parinibbuto puna dasavasso na hoti. Na kevalañca dasavassova, navavassopi…pe… ekavassopi. Na kevalañca ekavassova, dasamāsikopi…pe… ekamāsikopi. Ekadivasikopi. Ekamuhuttopi na hoti eva. Kasmā? Puna paṭisandhiyā abhāvā. Nibbīsādīsupi eseva nayo. Iti bhagavā mama sāsane khīṇāsavassetaṃ adhivacana’’nti – Vatvā yehi kāraṇehi so niddaso hoti, tāni dassetuṃ satta niddasavatthūni deseti. Theropi tameva desanaṃ uddharitvā satta niddasavatthūni idhāvuso, bhikkhu, sikkhāsamādānetiādimāha. Tattha idhāti imasmiṃ sāsane. Sikkhāsamādāne tibbacchando hotīti sikkhattayapūraṇe bahalacchando hoti. Āyatiñca sikkhāsamādāne avigatapemoti anāgate punadivasādīsupi sikkhāpūraṇe avigatapemena samannāgato hoti. Dhammanisantiyāti dhammanisāmanāya. Vipassanāyetaṃ adhivacanaṃ. Icchāvinayeti taṇhāvinayane. Paṭisallāneti ekībhāve. Vīriyārambheti kāyikacetasikassa vīriyassa pūraṇe. Satinepakketi satiyañceva nepakkabhāve ca. Diṭṭhipaṭivedheti maggadassane. Sesaṃ sabbattha vuttanayeneva veditabbaṃ. idhāvuso, bhikkhu, sikkhāsamādāne idhā Sikkhāsamādāne tibbacchando hotī Āyatiñca sikkhāsamādāne avigatapemo Dhammanisantiyā Icchāvinaye Paṭisallāne Vīriyārambhe Satinepakke Diṭṭhipaṭivedhe Saññāsu asubhānupassanāñāṇe saññā asubhasaññā. Ādīnavānupassanāñāṇe saññā ādīnavasaññā nāma. Sesā heṭṭhā vuttā eva. Balasattakaviññāṇaṭṭhitisattakapuggalasattakāni vuttanayāneva . Appahīnaṭṭhena anusayantīti anusayā. Thāmagato kāmarāgo kāmarāgānusayo. Esa nayo sabbattha. Saṃyojanasattakaṃ uttānatthameva. Saññāsu asubhasaññā. ādīnavasaññā anusayā. Adhikaraṇasamathasattakavaṇṇanā Adhikaraṇasamathesu adhikaraṇāni samenti vūpasamentīti adhikaraṇasamathā. Uppannuppannānanti uppannānaṃ uppannānaṃ. Adhikaraṇānanti vivādādhikaraṇaṃ anuvādādhikaraṇaṃ āpattādhikaraṇaṃ kiccādhikaraṇanti imesaṃ catunnaṃ. Samathāya vūpasamāyāti samathatthañceva vūpasamanatthañca. Sammukhāvinayo dātabbo…pe… tiṇavatthārakoti ime satta samathā dātabbā. adhikaraṇasamathā. Uppannuppannāna Adhikaraṇāna Samathāya vūpasamāyā Sammukhāvinayo dātabbo…pe… tiṇavatthārako Tatrāyaṃ vinicchayanayo. Adhikaraṇesu tāva dhammoti vā adhammoti vā aṭṭhārasahi vatthūhi vivadantānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ yo vivādo, idaṃ vivādādhikaraṇaṃ nāma. Sīlavipattiyā vā ācāradiṭṭhiājīvavipattiyā vā anuvadantānaṃ anuvādo upavadanā ceva codanā ca, idaṃ anuvādādhikaraṇaṃ nāma. Mātikāya āgatā pañca, vibhaṅge dveti sattapi āpattikkhandhā, idaṃ āpattādhikaraṇaṃ nāma. Saṅghassa apalokanādīnaṃ catunnaṃ kammānaṃ karaṇaṃ, idaṃ kiccādhikaraṇaṃ nāma. vivādādhikaraṇaṃ anuvādādhikaraṇaṃ āpattādhikaraṇaṃ kiccādhikaraṇaṃ Tattha vivādādhikaraṇaṃ dvīhi samathehi sammati sammukhāvinayena ca yebhuyyasikāya ca. Sammukhāvinayeneva sammamānaṃ yasmiṃ vihāre uppannaṃ tasmiṃyeva vā aññattha vūpasametuṃ gacchantānaṃ antarāmagge vā yattha gantvā saṅghassa niyyātitaṃ tattha saṅghena vā saṅghe vūpasametuṃ asakkonte tattheva ubbāhikāya sammatapuggalehi vā vinicchitaṃ sammati. Evaṃ sammamāne ca panetasmiṃ yā saṅghasammukhatā dhammasammukhatā vinayasammukhatā puggalasammukhatā, ayaṃ sammukhāvinayo nāma. sammukhāvinayo Tattha ca kārakasaṅghassa saṅghasāmaggivasena sammukhībhāvo saṅghasammukhatā. Sametabbassa vatthuno bhūtatā dhammasammukhatā. Yathā taṃ sametabbaṃ, tatheva sammanaṃ vinayasammukhatā. Yo ca vivadati, yena ca vivadati, tesaṃ ubhinnaṃ atthapaccatthikānaṃ sammukhībhāvo puggalasammukhatā. Ubbāhikāya vūpasame panettha saṅghasammukhatā parihāyati. Evaṃ tāva sammukhāvinayeneva sammati. saṅghasammukhatā. dhammasammukhatā. vinayasammukhatā. puggalasammukhatā. Sace panevampi na sammati, atha naṃ ubbāhikāya sammatā bhikkhū ‘‘na mayaṃ sakkoma vūpasametu’’nti saṅghasseva niyyātenti, tato saṅgho pañcaṅgasamannāgataṃ bhikkhuṃ salākaggāhāpakaṃ sammannati. Tena guḷhakavivaṭakasakaṇṇajappakesu tīsu salākaggāhesu aññataravasena salākaṃ gāhāpetvā sannipatitaparisāya dhammavādīnaṃ yebhuyyatāya yathā te dhammavādino vadanti, evaṃ vūpasantaṃ adhikaraṇaṃ sammukhāvinayena ca yebhuyyasikāya ca vūpasantaṃ hoti. Tattha sammukhāvinayo vuttanayo eva. Yaṃ pana yebhuyyasikākammassa karaṇaṃ, ayaṃ yebhuyyasikā nāma. Evaṃ vivādādhikaraṇaṃ dvīhi samathehi sammati. Anuvādādhikaraṇaṃ catūhi samathehi sammati – sammukhāvinayena ca sativinayena ca amūḷhavinayena ca tassapāpiyasikāya ca. Sammukhāvinayeneva sammamānaṃ yo ca anuvadati, yañca anuvadati, tesaṃ vacanaṃ sutvā sace kāci āpatti natthi, ubho khamāpetvā, sace atthi, ayaṃ nāmettha āpattīti evaṃ vinicchitaṃ vūpasammati. Tattha sammukhāvinayalakkhaṇaṃ vuttanayameva. Yadā pana khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno amūlikāya sīlavipattiyā anuddhaṃsitassa sativinayaṃ yācamānassa saṅgho ñatticatutthena kammena sativinayaṃ deti, tadā sammukhāvinayena ca sativinayena ca vūpasantaṃ hoti. Dinne pana sativinaye puna tasmiṃ puggale kassaci anuvādo na ruhati. yebhuyyasikā Anuvādādhikaraṇaṃ Sammukhāvinayeneva Yadā ummattako bhikkhu ummādavasena assāmaṇake ajjhācāre ‘‘saratāyasmā evarūpiṃ āpatti’’nti bhikkhūhi codiyamāno ‘‘ummattakena me , āvuso, etaṃ kataṃ, nāhaṃ taṃ sarāmī’’ti bhaṇantopi bhikkhūhi codiyamānova puna acodanatthāya amūḷhavinayaṃ yācati, saṅgho cassa ñatticatutthena kammena amūḷhavinayaṃ deti, tadā sammukhāvinayena ca amūḷhavinayena ca vūpasantaṃ hoti. Dinne pana amūḷhavinaye puna tasmiṃ puggale kassaci tappaccayā anuvādo na ruhati. Yadā pana pārājikena vā pārājikasāmantena vā codiyamānassa aññenaññaṃ paṭicarato pāpussannatāya pāpiyassa puggalassa ‘‘sacāyaṃ acchinnamūlo bhavissati, sammā vattitvā osāraṇaṃ labhissati. Sace chinnamūlo ayamevassa nāsanā bhavissatī’’ti maññamāno saṅgho ñatticatutthena kammena tassapāpiyasikaṃ karoti, tadā sammukhāvinayena ca tassapāpiyasikāya ca vūpasantaṃ hotīti. Evaṃ anuvādādhikaraṇaṃ catūhi samathehi sammati. Āpattādhikaraṇaṃ tīhi samathehi sammati sammukhāvinayena ca paṭiññātakaraṇena ca tiṇavatthārakena ca. Tassa sammukhāvinayeneva vūpasamo natthi. Yadā pana ekassa vā bhikkhuno santike saṅghagaṇamajjhesu vā bhikkhu lahukaṃ āpattiṃ deseti, tadā āpattādhikaraṇaṃ sammukhāvinayena ca paṭiññātakaraṇena ca vūpasammati. Āpattādhikaraṇaṃ āpattādhikaraṇaṃ Tattha sammukhāvinaye tāva yo ca deseti, yassa ca deseti, tesaṃ sammukhībhāvo puggalasammukhatā. Sesaṃ vuttanayameva. Puggalassa gaṇassa ca desanākāle saṅghasammukhatā parihāyati. Yā panettha ahaṃ, bhante, itthannāmaṃ āpattiṃ āpannoti ca āma, passāmīti ca paṭiññā, tāya paṭiññāya ‘‘āyatiṃ saṃvareyyāsī’’ti karaṇaṃ, taṃ paṭiññātakaraṇaṃ nāma. Saṅghādisese hi parivāsādiyācanā paṭiññā. Parivāsādīnaṃ dānaṃ paṭiññātakaraṇaṃ nāma. Dve pakkhajātā pana bhaṇḍanakārakā bhikkhū bahuṃ assāmaṇakaṃ ajjhācaritvā puna lajjidhamme uppanne sace mayaṃ imāhi āpattīhi aññamaññaṃ kāressāma, siyāpi taṃ adhikaraṇaṃ kakkhaḷattāya saṃvatteyyāti aññamaññaṃ āpattiyā kārāpane dosaṃ disvā yadā tiṇavatthārakakammaṃ karonti, tadā āpattādhikaraṇaṃ sammukhāvinayena ca tiṇavatthārakena ca sammati. paṭiññātakaraṇaṃ Tattha hi yattakā hatthapāsūpagatā ‘‘na metaṃ khamatī’’ti evaṃ diṭṭhāvikammaṃ akatvā niddampi okkantā honti, sabbesaṃ ṭhapetvā thullavajjañca gihipaṭisaṃyuttañca sabbāpattiyo vuṭṭhahanti, evaṃ āpattādhikaraṇaṃ tīhi samathehi sammati. Kiccādhikaraṇaṃ ekena samathena sammati sammukhāvinayeneva. Imāni cattāri adhikaraṇāni yathānurūpaṃ imehi sattahi samathehi sammanti. Tena vuttaṃ – uppannuppannānaṃ adhikaraṇānaṃ samathāya vūpasamāya sammukhāvinayo dātabbo…pe… tiṇavatthārakoti. Ayamettha vinicchayanayo. Vitthāro pana samathakkhandhake āgatoyeva. Vinicchayopissa samantapāsādikāyaṃ vutto. Kiccādhikaraṇaṃ ‘‘Ime kho, āvuso’’tiādi vuttanayeneva yojetabbaṃ. Iti cuddasannaṃ sattakānaṃ vasena aṭṭhanavuti pañhe kathento thero sāmaggirasaṃ dassesīti. Sattakavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā. Aṭṭhakavaṇṇanā
331. Niddasavatthūnī: the bases beginning with niddasa.

Niddaso bhikkhu, nibbīso, nittiṃso, niccattālīso, nippaññāso bhikkhūti evaṃ vacanakāraṇāni: These are the reasons for saying "a niddasa monk, a nibbīsa monk, a nittiṃsa monk, a niccattālīsa monk, a nippaññāsa monk."

This question arose during the time of the heretics.

The heretics say that the nigaṇṭha who died at the age of ten is a niddasa.

But he is no longer ten years old.

Not only ten years old.

He is not nine years old...pe...he is not one year old.

In the same way, they say that the one who died at the age of twenty, etc., is a nibbīsa, a nittiṃsa, a niccattālīsa, a nippaññāsa.

The venerable Ānanda, while wandering in the village, heard this and went to the monastery and told the Blessed One.

The Blessed One said:

“Ānanda, this is not a designation of the heretics. This is a designation of a khīṇāsava in my dispensation.

Because the khīṇāsava who attained parinibbāna at the age of ten is no longer ten years old.

Not only ten years old, nine years old...pe...one year old.

Not only one year old, ten months old...pe...one month old.

[Not only] one day old.

He is not even one moment old.

Why? Because there is no more rebirth paṭisandhi.

The same method applies in the case of nibbīsa, etc.

Thus, the Blessed One said, “This is the designation of a khīṇāsava in my dispensation.”

Having said which, he teaches seven niddasavatthūni to show the reasons why he is a niddasa.

The Elder, having extracted that very teaching, said the seven niddasavatthūni, beginning with "idhāvuso, bhikkhu, sikkhāsamādāne."

There, idhā means in this dispensation (śāsana).

"Sikkhāsamādāne tibbacchando hotī" means he has intense desire for the fulfillment of the threefold training (śikṣā).

"Āyatiñca sikkhāsamādāne avigatapemo" means in the future, too, he is endowed with unfailing affection for the fulfillment of the training.

Dhammanisantiyā means for the contemplation of the Dharma.

This is a designation for insight (vipaśyanā).

Icchāvinaye means in the subduing of craving (tṛṣṇā).

Paṭisallāne means in unification (ekībhāva).

Vīriyārambhe means in the fulfillment of the energy of body and mind.

Satinepakke means in mindfulness (smṛti) and prudence (naipunya).

Diṭṭhipaṭivedhe means in the vision of the path (mārga).

The rest should be understood in the way already described everywhere.

Idhāvuso, bhikkhu, sikkhāsamādāne idhā sikkhāsamādāne tibbacchando hotī āyatiñca sikkhāsamādāne avigatapemo dhammanisantiyā icchāvinaye paṭisallāne vīriyārambhe satinepakke diṭṭhipaṭivedhe saññāsu in perceptions the perception of unattractiveness, the consciousness that is the aśubhasaṃjñā.

In the perception of danger, the consciousness that is called the ādīnavasaṃjñā.

The rest has already been stated.

The seven powers, the seven abodes of consciousness, and the seven individuals are to be understood by the method already stated.
Because of what is not abandoned, they are tendencies (anusayā).
Desire-passion that has gained strength is the tendency of desire-passion (kāmarāgānusayo).
This method applies to all instances.
The meaning of the collection of fetters is indeed clear.

In perceptions (saññāsu) [there is] the perception of unattractiveness (asubhasaññā).
[There is] the perception of danger (ādīnavasaññā).

Tendencies (anusayā).

Explanation of what possesses the nature of appeasement of a dispute (adhikaraṇasamathasattakavaṇṇanā).

In the appeasement of disputes, disputes are calmed and are appeased, thus [they are called] appeasements of disputes (adhikaraṇasamathā).
"Of those that have arisen and arisen" (uppannuppannānanti) means of those that have arisen and arisen (uppannānaṃ uppannānaṃ).
"Of disputes" (adhikaraṇānanti) means dispute-dispute (vivādādhikaraṇaṃ), accusation-dispute (anuvādādhikaraṇaṃ), offense-dispute (āpattādhikaraṇaṃ), duty-dispute (kiccādhikaraṇaṃ), these four.
"For appeasement and for calming" (samathāya vūpasamāyāti) means for the purpose of appeasement and for the purpose of calming (samathatthañceva vūpasamanatthañca).
"The sammukhāvinaya is to be given...pe...the covering with grass" (sammukhāvinayo dātabbo…pe… tiṇavatthārakoti) means these seven appeasements are to be given (ime satta samathā dātabbā).

Appeasements of disputes (adhikaraṇasamathā).
Of those that have arisen and arisen (uppannuppannāna), of disputes (adhikaraṇāna), for appeasement and for calming (samathāya vūpasamāyā), the sammukhāvinaya is to be given...pe...the covering with grass (sammukhāvinayo dātabbo…pe… tiṇavatthārako).
There, this is the method of deciding (tatrāyaṃ vinicchayanayo).
In disputes, to the extent that there is a dispute among monks who dispute with eighteen grounds, [saying] "It is the Dharma" or "It is not the Dharma," this is called dispute-dispute (vivādādhikaraṇaṃ nāma).
Due to failure of morality (sīlavipattiyā) or due to failure of conduct, view, or livelihood (ācāradiṭṭhiājīvavipattiyā) or to the extent that accusation is accusing and censure is censure, this is called accusation-dispute (anuvādādhikaraṇaṃ nāma).
Five are come in the matrix, two in the vibhaṅga, seven are groups of offenses, this is called offense-dispute (āpattādhikaraṇaṃ nāma).
The doing of the four karmas beginning with proclamation of the saṃgha, this is called duty-dispute (kiccādhikaraṇaṃ nāma).

Dispute-dispute (vivādādhikaraṇaṃ).
Accusation-dispute (anuvādādhikaraṇaṃ).
Offense-dispute (āpattādhikaraṇaṃ).
Duty-dispute (kiccādhikaraṇaṃ).
There, dispute-dispute (vivādādhikaraṇaṃ) is settled by two appeasements, by the sammukhāvinaya and by majority (yebhuyyasikāya ca).
Approving with the sammukhāvinaya, in whichever vihāra it has arisen, in that very one or elsewhere, for those going to appease, on the road or wherever, having gone, it is delivered to the saṃgha, there by the saṃgha or, being unable to appease in the saṃgha, just there, by those monks approved by the conveyor (ubbāhikāya), it is decided, it is approved.
And thus, while approving, whichever presence of the saṃgha (saṅghasammukhatā), presence of the Dharma (dhammasammukhatā), presence of the vinaya (vinayasammukhatā), presence of the individual (puggalasammukhatā) [there is], this is called sammukhāvinaya (sammukhāvinayo nāma).

The sammukhāvinaya.
And there, the facing of the active saṃgha with the harmony of the saṃgha is the presence of the saṃgha (saṅghasammukhatā).
The truth of the thing to be settled is the presence of the Dharma (dhammasammukhatā).
Just as that is to be settled, so is the approval, [this is] the presence of the vinaya (vinayasammukhatā).
And whoever disputes and with whatever he disputes, the facing of those two opponents is the presence of the individual (puggalasammukhatā).
In the appeasement by the conveyor (ubbāhikāya), here, the presence of the saṃgha declines.
Thus indeed, it is approved only by the sammukhāvinaya.

Presence of the saṃgha (saṅghasammukhatā).
Presence of the Dharma (dhammasammukhatā).
Presence of the vinaya (vinayasammukhatā).
Presence of the individual (puggalasammukhatā).
If, however, it is not approved in this way, then those monks approved by the conveyor (ubbāhikāya), [saying] "We are not able to appease," deliver it to the saṃgha itself, then the saṃgha approves a monk possessing the five qualities, the taker of the ballot (salākaggāhāpakaṃ sammannati).
By that, in the three takings of the ballot, the secret, the open, and the ear-whispered (guḷhakavivaṭakasakaṇṇajappakesu tīsu salākaggāhesu), having caused a ballot to be taken in one way or another, to the assembly that has gathered, according to the majority of those speaking of the Dharma (dhammavādīnaṃ), just as those speaking of the Dharma (dhammavādino) say, thus is the dispute that has been appeased, by the sammukhāvinaya and by majority (yebhuyyasikāya ca) it is appeased.

There, the sammukhāvinaya is just the method that has been spoken of.
That which is the cause of the majority's action, this is called yebhuyyasikā.

Thus, a legal issue arising from a dispute (vivādādhikaraṇaṃ) is settled through two principles for settling (samathehi).

A legal issue arising from an accusation (anuvādādhikaraṇaṃ) is settled through four principles for settling (samathehi)— through resolution face-to-face (sammukhāvinayena), resolution through recollection (sati-vinayena), resolution because of past insanity (amūḷha-vinayena), and through further penalty (tassa-pāpiyasikāya).

When one who approves (sammānamānaṃ) and one who accuses, having heard their words, if there is no offense (āpatti), having had both forgiven, if there is, [saying] "this is the offense here" thus, having decided, it subsides through resolution face-to-face (sammukhāvinayeneva).

Therein, the characteristic of resolution face-to-face (sammukhāvinayalakkhaṇaṃ) is as already stated (vuttanayameva).

When a monk whose outflows are ended (khīṇāsavassa bhikkhuno), not reviled for a transgression of morality that is without basis (amūlikāya sīlavipattiyā anuddhaṃsitassa), requests resolution through recollection (sati-vinayaṃ yācamānassa), the saṃgha gives resolution through recollection (sati-vinayaṃ deti) by means of a motion with announcement as fourth (ñatti-catutthena kammena), then it is calmed through resolution face-to-face (sammukhāvinayena) and resolution through recollection (sati-vinayena) and subsides (vūpasantaṃ hoti).

However, once resolution through recollection (sati-vinaye) is given, an accusation (anuvādo) does not arise against that person again (puna tasmiṃ puggale kassaci anuvādo na ruhati).

Yebhuyyasikā Anuvādādhikaraṇaṃ

When an insane monk (ummattako bhikkhu), due to insanity (ummādavasena) commits an improper act, being questioned by the monks, “Venerable sir, you remember such an offense (saratāyasmā evarūpiṃ āpatti),” even though saying, “Friend (āvuso), this was done by me while insane, I do not remember that (ummattakena me etaṃ kataṃ, nāhaṃ taṃ sarāmī),” and being questioned by the monks, then again he requests resolution because of past insanity (amūḷha-vinayaṃ yācati) in order not to be questioned (acodana-atthāya), and the saṃgha gives him resolution because of past insanity (amūḷha-vinayaṃ deti) by means of a motion with announcement as fourth (ñatti-catutthena kammena), then it is calmed through resolution face-to-face (sammukhāvinayena) and resolution because of past insanity (amūḷha-vinayena) and subsides (vūpasantaṃ hoti).

However, once resolution because of past insanity (amūḷha-vinaye) is given, an accusation (anuvādo) based on that cause (tappaccayā) does not arise against that person again (puna tasmiṃ puggale kassaci tappaccayā anuvādo na ruhati).

When someone being questioned by one who has transgressed the defeat (pārājikena) or is connected with a defeat (pārājika-sāmantena), retaliating against each other (aññenaññaṃ paṭicarato) says, “If he is not cut off at the root (acchinna-mūlo bhavissati), having behaved rightly, he will obtain release (sammā vattitvā osāraṇaṃ labhissati). If he is cut off at the root (chinna-mūlo), this very thing will be his destruction (ayamevassa nāsanā bhavissati),” thinking thus (maññamāno), the saṃgha carries out further penalty (tassa-pāpiyasikaṃ karoti) by means of a motion with announcement as fourth (ñatti-catutthena kammena), then it is calmed through resolution face-to-face (sammukhāvinayena) and by further penalty (tassa-pāpiyasikāya) and subsides (vūpasantaṃ hoti).

Thus, a legal issue arising from an accusation (anuvādādhikaraṇaṃ) is settled through four principles for settling (catūhi samathehi sammati).

A legal issue arising from an offense (āpattādhikaraṇaṃ) is settled through three principles for settling (tīhi samathehi sammati): through resolution face-to-face (sammukhāvinayena), through resolution by acknowledgement (paṭiññāta-karaṇena), and through covering with grass (tiṇavatthārakena).

For him, there is no subsidence (vūpasamo natthi) through resolution face-to-face alone (sammukhāvinayeneva).

When a monk confesses a minor offense (lahukaṃ āpattiṃ deseti) in the presence of one monk or in the midst of the assembly of the saṃgha, then a legal issue arising from an offense (āpattādhikaraṇaṃ) is calmed through resolution face-to-face (sammukhāvinayena) and through resolution by acknowledgement (paṭiññāta-karaṇena) and subsides (vūpasammati).

A legal issue arising from an offense (āpattādhikaraṇaṃ)

A legal issue arising from an offense (āpattādhikaraṇaṃ)

Therein, in resolution face-to-face (sammukhāvinaye), the fact that he who confesses (yo ca deseti) and he to whom he confesses (yassa ca deseti) are in each other's presence (tesaṃ sammukhībhāvo) is personal presence (puggala-sammukhatā).

The rest is as already stated (sesaṃ vuttanayameva).

At the time of confession (desanā-kāle) by a person or a group (puggalassa gaṇassa ca), presence of the saṃgha is lost (saṅgha-sammukhatā parihāyati).

Moreover, that which [is said], “I, venerable sir (bhante), have incurred such an offense (itthannāmaṃ āpattiṃ āpanno’ti),” and "yes (āma)," and acknowledgement, “I see (passāmi’ti ca paṭiññā),” by that acknowledgement (tāya paṭiññāya) [comes] the act [of saying], “Restrain yourself in the future (āyatiṃ saṃvareyyāsī’ti karaṇaṃ),” that is called resolution by acknowledgement (paṭiññāta-karaṇaṃ nāma).

In the case of offenses entailing suspension (saṃghādisese), requesting probation (parivāsa-ādi-yācanā) is acknowledgement (paṭiññā).

Giving probation and so on (parivāsa-ādīnaṃ dānaṃ) is called resolution by acknowledgement (paṭiññāta-karaṇaṃ nāma).

However, when two monks who have arisen from factions, who are makers of quarrels (bhaṇḍana-kārakā bhikkhū), having committed many improper acts (assāmaṇakaṃ ajjhācaritvā), when shame arises again (puna lajji-dhamme uppanne) [and they think], “If we should make each other confess these offenses (imāhi āpattīhi aññamaññaṃ kāressāma), that legal issue (adhikaraṇaṃ) would tend to be harsh (kakkhaḷattāya saṃvatteyya),” having seen the fault in making each other confess offenses (aññamaññaṃ āpattiyā kārāpane dosaṃ disvā), when they perform covering with grass (tiṇavatthāraka-kammaṃ karonti), then a legal issue arising from an offense (āpattādhikaraṇaṃ) is settled through resolution face-to-face (sammukhāvinayena) and through covering with grass (tiṇavatthārakena) and subsides (sammati).

Resolution by Acknowledgment (paṭiññāta-karaṇaṃ)

Therein, indeed, as many as have come within arm's reach (hattha-pāsa-ūpagatā), without making an act of seeing what is seen, [saying] “This is not pleasing to me (na metaṃ khamati),” even sleep has overtaken [them] (niddampi okkantā honti), setting aside all [offenses], both serious offenses and those connected with laypeople (thulla-vajjañca gihi-paṭisaṃyuttañca), all offenses are cleared away (sabbāpattiyo vuṭṭhahanti), thus, a legal issue arising from an offense (āpattādhikaraṇaṃ) is settled through three principles for settling (tīhi samathehi sammati).

A legal issue concerning an act (kiccādhikaraṇaṃ) is settled through one principle for settling (ekena samathena sammati)—through resolution face-to-face alone (sammukhāvinayeneva).

These four legal issues (imāni cattāri adhikaraṇāni) are settled appropriately (yathānurūpaṃ) through these seven principles for settling (imehi sattahi samathehi sammanti).

Therefore it was said (tena vuttaṃ)—"For the calming and subsiding of legal issues that have arisen (uppanna-uppannānaṃ adhikaraṇānaṃ samathāya vūpasamāya), resolution face-to-face (sammukhāvinayo) should be given…pe… covering with grass (tiṇavatthārako’ti)."

This is the way of deciding in this case (ayamettha vinicchaya-nayo).

However, the detailed account (vitthāro) has already come (āgato yeva) in the Section on Calming (samatha-khandhake).

The decision (vinicchayo pi) has been stated (vutto) in the Samantapāsādikā.

A legal issue concerning an act (kiccādhikaraṇaṃ) should be connected (yojetabbaṃ) in the same way as already stated (vuttanayeneva) [in the passage beginning], "These here, friends (ime kho, āvuso’ti ādi)."
Thus, the elder, explaining ninety-eight questions by way of fourteen sets, showed the taste of concord.

The exposition of the sets is finished.

The exposition of the eights.
