332. ‘‘Satta viññāṇaṭṭhitiyo. Santāvuso, sattā nānattakāyā nānattasaññino, seyyathāpi manussā ekacce ca devā ekacce ca vinipātikā. Ayaṃ paṭhamā viññāṇaṭṭhiti. ‘‘Santāvuso, sattā nānattakāyā ekattasaññino seyyathāpi devā brahmakāyikā paṭhamābhinibbattā. Ayaṃ dutiyā viññāṇaṭṭhiti. ‘‘Santāvuso, sattā ekattakāyā nānattasaññino seyyathāpi devā ābhassarā. Ayaṃ tatiyā viññāṇaṭṭhiti. ‘‘Santāvuso, sattā ekattakāyā ekattasaññino seyyathāpi devā subhakiṇhā. Ayaṃ catutthī viññāṇaṭṭhiti. ‘‘Santāvuso, sattā sabbaso rūpasaññānaṃ samatikkamā paṭighasaññānaṃ atthaṅgamā nānattasaññānaṃ amanasikārā ‘ananto ākāso’ti ākāsānañcāyatanūpagā. Ayaṃ pañcamī viññāṇaṭṭhiti. ‘‘Santāvuso, sattā sabbaso ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘anantaṃ viññāṇa’nti viññāṇañcāyatanūpagā. Ayaṃ chaṭṭhī viññāṇaṭṭhiti. ‘‘Santāvuso , sattā sabbaso viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘natthi kiñcī’ti ākiñcaññāyatanūpagā. Ayaṃ sattamī viññāṇaṭṭhiti. ‘‘Satta puggalā dakkhiṇeyyā – ubhatobhāgavimutto , paññāvimutto, kāyasakkhi, diṭṭhippatto, saddhāvimutto, dhammānusārī, saddhānusārī. puggalā dakkhiṇeyyā – ‘‘Satta anusayā – kāmarāgānusayo, paṭighānusayo, diṭṭhānusayo, vicikicchānusayo, mānānusayo, bhavarāgānusayo, avijjānusayo. anusayā – ‘‘Satta saññojanāni – anunayasaññojanaṃ kāmasaññojanaṃ (syā. kaṃ.), paṭighasaññojanaṃ, diṭṭhisaññojanaṃ, vicikicchāsaññojanaṃ, mānasaññojanaṃ, bhavarāgasaññojanaṃ, avijjāsaññojanaṃ. saññojanāni – kāmasaññojanaṃ (syā. kaṃ.) ‘‘Satta adhikaraṇasamathā – uppannuppannānaṃ adhikaraṇānaṃ samathāya vūpasamāya sammukhāvinayo dātabbo, sativinayo dātabbo, amūḷhavinayo dātabbo, paṭiññāya kāretabbaṃ, yebhuyyasikā, tassapāpiyasikā, tiṇavatthārako. adhikaraṇasamathā – ‘‘Ime kho, āvuso, tena bhagavatā jānatā passatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena satta dhammā sammadakkhātā; tattha sabbeheva saṅgāyitabbaṃ…pe… atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ. Dutiyabhāṇavāro niṭṭhito. Aṭṭhakaṃ
332. "Seven stations of consciousness. Friends, there are beings with multiplicity of body & multiplicity of perception, such as human beings, some devas, & some beings in the lower realms. This is the first station for consciousness.

"Friends, there are beings with multiplicity of body & singularity of perception, such as the devas of Brahmā’s retinue generated by the first . This is the second station for consciousness.

“Friends, there are beings with singularity of body & multiplicity of perception, such as the Radiant Devas. This is the third station for consciousness.

“Friends, there are beings with singularity of body & singularity of perception, such as the Beautiful Black Devas. This is the fourth station for consciousness.

“There are beings who, with the complete transcending of perceptions of form (rūpasaññā), with the disappearance of perceptions of resistance (paṭighasaññā), and not attending to perceptions of multiplicity (nānattasaññā), ‘Infinite space,’ arrive at the dimension of the infinitude of space (ākāsānañcāyatana). This is the fifth station of consciousness.

“Friends, there are beings who, with the complete transcending of the dimension of the infinitude of space (ākāsānañcāyatana), perceiving, ‘Infinite consciousness,’ arrive at the dimension of the infinitude of consciousness (viññāṇañcāyatana). This is the sixth station of consciousness.

“Friends, there are beings who, having completely surmounted the base of the infinity of consciousness (viññāṇañcāyatana), perceiving, ‘There is nothing,’ arrive at the base of nothingness (ākiñcaññāyatana). This is the seventh station for consciousness.

“Seven persons worthy of offerings (dakkhiṇeyyā): one liberated-in-both ways (ubhatobhāgavimutto), one liberated-by-wisdom (paññāvimutto), a body-witness (kāyasakkhi), one attained-to-view (diṭṭhippatto), one liberated-by-faith (saddhāvimutto), a Dhamma-follower (dhammānusārī), and a faith-follower (saddhānusārī).

“Seven latent tendencies (anusayā): the latent tendency of sensual lust (kāmarāgānusayo), the latent tendency of aversion (paṭighānusayo), the latent tendency of views (diṭṭhānusayo), the latent tendency of doubt (vicikicchānusayo), the latent tendency of conceit (mānānusayo), the latent tendency of passion for becoming (bhavarāgānusayo), the latent tendency of ignorance (avijjānusayo).

“Seven fetters (saññojanāni): the fetter of sensuality (anunayasaññojanaṃ), the fetter of irritation (paṭighasaññojanaṃ), the fetter of views (diṭṭhisaññojanaṃ), the fetter of uncertainty (vicikicchāsaññojanaṃ), the fetter of conceit (mānasaññojanaṃ), the fetter of passion for becoming (bhavarāgasaññojanaṃ), the fetter of ignorance (avijjāsaññojanaṃ).

“Seven means for the allaying of legal issues (adhikaraṇasamathā): A judgement of presence (sammukhāvinayo) should be given for the allaying, for the settling of legal issues that have arisen, a judgement of mindfulness (sativinayo) should be given, a judgement of non-delusion (amūḷhavinayo) should be given, confession should be enacted, acting on the decision of the majority (yebhuyyasikā), acting on the acknowledgement of the crime (tassapāpiyasikā), covering over with grass (tiṇavatthārako).

“These, friends, are the seven dharmas that are rightly expounded by the Blessed One—the one who knows, the one who sees, the Worthy One, rightly self-awakened. Those should all be recited together…for the benefit & happiness of devas & human beings.

The second recitation section is finished.

The Eight Section
333
333. ‘‘Atthi kho, āvuso, tena bhagavatā jānatā passatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena aṭṭha dhammā sammadakkhātā; tattha sabbeheva saṅgāyitabbaṃ…pe… atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ. Katame aṭṭha? ‘‘Aṭṭha micchattā – micchādiṭṭhi, micchāsaṅkappo, micchāvācā, micchākammanto, micchāājīvo, micchāvāyāmo micchāsati, micchāsamādhi. micchattā – ‘‘Aṭṭha sammattā – sammādiṭṭhi, sammāsaṅkappo, sammāvācā, sammākammanto, sammāājīvo, sammāvāyāmo, sammāsati, sammāsamādhi. sammattā – ‘‘Aṭṭha puggalā dakkhiṇeyyā – sotāpanno, sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno; sakadāgāmī, sakadāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno; anāgāmī, anāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno; arahā, arahattaphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno. puggalā dakkhiṇeyyā –
333. "These, friends, are the eight dharmas that are rightly expounded by the Blessed One—the one who knows, the one who sees, the Worthy One, rightly self-awakened. Those should all be recited together…for the benefit & happiness of devas & human beings.

What are the eight?

"Eight wrongnesses (micchattā): wrong view (micchādiṭṭhi), wrong intention (micchāsaṅkappo), wrong speech (micchāvācā), wrong action (micchākammanto), wrong livelihood (micchāājīvo), wrong effort (micchāvāyāmo), wrong mindfulness (micchāsati), wrong concentration (micchāsamādhi).

Eight rightnesses (sammattā): right view (sammādiṭṭhi), right intention (sammāsaṅkappo), right speech (sammāvācā), right action (sammākammanto), right livelihood (sammāājīvo), right effort (sammāvāyāmo), right mindfulness (sammāsati), right concentration (sammāsamādhi).

Eight persons worthy of offerings (puggalā dakkhiṇeyyā): the stream-enterer (sotāpanno), the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry (sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno); the once-returner (sakadāgāmī), the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of once-returning (sakadāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno); the non-returner (anāgāmī), the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of non-returning (anāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno); the arhat (arahā), the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of arhatship (arahattaphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno).
333. Iti sattakavasena sāmaggirasaṃ dassetvā idāni aṭṭhakavasena dassetuṃ puna desanaṃ ārabhi. Tattha micchattāti ayāthāvā micchāsabhāvā. Sammattāti yāthāvā sammāsabhāvā.
333. Having shown the essence of concord through the sevenfold [aggregation], now, in order to show [it] through the eightfold [aggregation], he again began the teaching. There, micchattā means untrue, having a false nature. sammattā means true, having a right nature.
334
334. ‘‘Aṭṭha kusītavatthūni. Idhāvuso, bhikkhunā kammaṃ kātabbaṃ hoti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘kammaṃ kho me kātabbaṃ bhavissati, kammaṃ kho pana me karontassa kāyo kilamissati, handāhaṃ nipajjāmī’ti! So nipajjati na vīriyaṃ ārabhati appattassa pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyāya. Idaṃ paṭhamaṃ kusītavatthu. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhunā kammaṃ kataṃ hoti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘ahaṃ kho kammaṃ akāsiṃ, kammaṃ kho pana me karontassa kāyo kilanto, handāhaṃ nipajjāmī’ti! So nipajjati na vīriyaṃ ārabhati…pe… idaṃ dutiyaṃ kusītavatthu. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhunā maggo gantabbo hoti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘maggo kho me gantabbo bhavissati, maggaṃ kho pana me gacchantassa kāyo kilamissati, handāhaṃ nipajjāmī’ti! So nipajjati na vīriyaṃ ārabhati… idaṃ tatiyaṃ kusītavatthu. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhunā maggo gato hoti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘ahaṃ kho maggaṃ agamāsiṃ, maggaṃ kho pana me gacchantassa kāyo kilanto, handāhaṃ nipajjāmī’ti! So nipajjati na vīriyaṃ ārabhati… idaṃ catutthaṃ kusītavatthu. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya caranto na labhati lūkhassa vā paṇītassa vā bhojanassa yāvadatthaṃ pāripūriṃ. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘ahaṃ kho gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya caranto nālatthaṃ lūkhassa vā paṇītassa vā bhojanassa yāvadatthaṃ pāripūriṃ, tassa me kāyo kilanto akammañño, handāhaṃ nipajjāmī’ti! So nipajjati na vīriyaṃ ārabhati… idaṃ pañcamaṃ kusītavatthu. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya caranto labhati lūkhassa vā paṇītassa vā bhojanassa yāvadatthaṃ pāripūriṃ. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘ahaṃ kho gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya caranto alatthaṃ lūkhassa vā paṇītassa vā bhojanassa yāvadatthaṃ pāripūriṃ, tassa me kāyo garuko akammañño, māsācitaṃ maññe , handāhaṃ nipajjāmī’ti! So nipajjati na vīriyaṃ ārabhati… idaṃ chaṭṭhaṃ kusītavatthu. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhuno uppanno hoti appamattako ābādho. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘uppanno kho me ayaṃ appamattako ābādho; atthi kappo nipajjituṃ, handāhaṃ nipajjāmī’ti! So nipajjati na vīriyaṃ ārabhati… idaṃ sattamaṃ kusītavatthu. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu gilānā vuṭṭhito gilānavuṭṭhito (saddanīti) a. ni. 6.16 nakulapitusuttaṭīkā passitabbā hoti aciravuṭṭhito gelaññā. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘ahaṃ kho gilānā vuṭṭhito aciravuṭṭhito gelaññā, tassa me kāyo dubbalo akammañño, handāhaṃ nipajjāmī’ti! So nipajjati na vīriyaṃ ārabhati appattassa pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyāya. Idaṃ aṭṭhamaṃ kusītavatthu. gilānavuṭṭhito (saddanīti) a. ni. 6.16 nakulapitusuttaṭīkā passitabbā
334.
‘‘The eight grounds for laziness.
Again, friends, there is the case where a monk has some work to do.
It occurs to him: ‘I have work to do. But in doing the work, my body will get tired. Why don’t I lie down?’ So he lies down, and doesn't make an effort for the attaining of the as-yet-unattained, the reaching of the as-yet-unreached, the realization of the as-yet-unrealized.
This is the first ground for laziness.

‘‘Again, friends, there is the case where a monk has done some work.
It occurs to him: ‘I have done some work. But in doing the work, my body got tired. Why don’t I lie down?’ So he lies down, and doesn't make an effort… This is the second ground for laziness.

Again, friends, there is the case where a monk has to go on a journey.
It occurs to him: ‘I have to go on a journey. But in going on a journey, my body will get tired. Why don’t I lie down?’ So he lies down, and doesn't make an effort… This is the third ground for laziness.

Again, friends, there is the case where a monk has gone on a journey.
It occurs to him: ‘I have gone on a journey. But in going on a journey, my body got tired. Why don’t I lie down?’ So he lies down, and doesn't make an effort… This is the fourth ground for laziness.

Again, friends, there is the case where a monk, while wandering for alms in a village or town, does not get as much coarse or refined food as he would like for his fill.
It occurs to him: ‘While wandering for alms in a village or town, I didn’t get as much coarse or refined food as I would like for my fill. This body of mine is tired & unsuitable for work. Why don’t I lie down?’ So he lies down, and doesn't make an effort… This is the fifth ground for laziness.

Again, friends, there is the case where a monk, while wandering for alms in a village or town, gets as much coarse or refined food as he would like for his fill.
It occurs to him: ‘While wandering for alms in a village or town, I got as much coarse or refined food as I would like for my fill. This body of mine is heavy & unsuitable for work—stuffed with beans, as it were. Why don’t I lie down?’ So he lies down, and doesn't make an effort… This is the sixth ground for laziness.

Again, friends, there is the case where a monk has a slight illness.
It occurs to him: ‘I have this slight illness; there is the opportunity to lie down. Why don’t I lie down?’ So he lies down, and doesn't make an effort… This is the seventh ground for laziness.

Again, friends, there is the case where a monk has recovered from an illness, and it’s not long since he recovered.
It occurs to him: ‘I have recovered from my illness, and it’s not long since I recovered. This body of mine is weak & unsuitable for work. Why don’t I lie down?’ So he lies down, and doesn't make an effort for the attaining of the as-yet-unattained, the reaching of the as-yet-unreached, the realization of the as-yet-unrealized.
This is the eighth ground for laziness.
334.Kusītavatthūnīti kusītassa alasassa vatthūni patiṭṭhā kosajjakāraṇānīti attho. Kammaṃ kattabbaṃ hotīti cīvaravicāraṇādikammaṃ kātabbaṃ hoti. Na vīriyaṃ ārabhatīti duvidhampi vīriyaṃ nārabhati. Appattassāti jhānavipassanāmaggaphaladhammassa appattassa pattiyā. Anadhigatassāti tasseva anadhigatassa adhigamatthāya. Asacchikatassāti tasseva apaccakkhakatassa sacchikaraṇatthāya. Idaṃ paṭhamanti idaṃ handāhaṃ nipajjāmīti evaṃ osīdanaṃ paṭhamaṃ kusītavatthu. Iminā nayena sabbattha attho veditabbo. ‘‘Māsācitaṃ maññe’’ti ettha pana māsācitaṃ nāma tintamāso. Yathā tintamāso garuko hoti, evaṃ garukoti adhippāyo. Gilānā vuṭṭhito hotīti gilāno hutvā pacchā vuṭṭhito hoti.
334.
“Kusītavatthūnī” means the things, the bases, the causes of laziness (kosajja-kāraṇāni), of a lazy, indolent person (kusītassa alasassa).
“Kammaṃ kattabbaṃ hotī” means that the action of considering robes (cīvara-vicāraṇā) and so on, is to be done (kātabbaṃ hoti).
"Na vīriyaṃ ārabhatī" means he does not undertake energy in two ways (duvidhampi vīriyaṃ nārabhati).
"Appattassā" [means] for the attainment (pattiyā) of the unattained dhamma of jhāna, vipassanā, path, and fruit (jhāna-vipassanā-magga-phala-dhamma).
"Anadhigatassā" [means] for the purpose of acquiring the unacquired.
"Asacchikatassā" means for the purpose of realizing what has not been directly experienced.
"Idaṃ paṭhama" means: "Come now, I will lie down" (idaṃ handāhaṃ nipajjāmi); thus, this is the first occasion for sinking down ( osīdanaṃ paṭhamaṃ kusītavatthu).
By this method, the meaning should be understood everywhere.
‘‘Māsācitaṃ maññe’’ means that "māsācitaṃ" means a saturated month (tintamāso).
Just as a saturated month is heavy (garuko hoti), so the intention is “heavy” (garuko).
"Gilānā vuṭṭhito hotī" means having been sick, afterward, he is recovered (gilāno hutvā pacchā vuṭṭhito hoti).
335
335. ‘‘Aṭṭha ārambhavatthūni. Idhāvuso, bhikkhunā kammaṃ kātabbaṃ hoti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘kammaṃ kho me kātabbaṃ bhavissati, kammaṃ kho pana me karontena na sukaraṃ buddhānaṃ sāsanaṃ manasi kātuṃ, handāhaṃ vīriyaṃ ārabhāmi appattassa pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya, asacchikatassa sacchikiriyāyā’ti! So vīriyaṃ ārabhati appattassa pattiyā, anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyāya. Idaṃ paṭhamaṃ ārambhavatthu. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhunā kammaṃ kataṃ hoti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘ahaṃ kho kammaṃ akāsiṃ, kammaṃ kho panāhaṃ karonto nāsakkhiṃ buddhānaṃ sāsanaṃ manasi kātuṃ, handāhaṃ vīriyaṃ ārabhāmi…pe… so vīriyaṃ ārabhati… idaṃ dutiyaṃ ārambhavatthu. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhunā maggo gantabbo hoti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘maggo kho me gantabbo bhavissati, maggaṃ kho pana me gacchantena na sukaraṃ buddhānaṃ sāsanaṃ manasi kātuṃ. Handāhaṃ vīriyaṃ ārabhāmi…pe… so vīriyaṃ ārabhati… idaṃ tatiyaṃ ārambhavatthu. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhunā maggo gato hoti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘ahaṃ kho maggaṃ agamāsiṃ, maggaṃ kho panāhaṃ gacchanto nāsakkhiṃ buddhānaṃ sāsanaṃ manasi kātuṃ, handāhaṃ vīriyaṃ ārabhāmi…pe… so vīriyaṃ ārabhati… idaṃ catutthaṃ ārambhavatthu. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya caranto na labhati lūkhassa vā paṇītassa vā bhojanassa yāvadatthaṃ pāripūriṃ . Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘ahaṃ kho gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya caranto nālatthaṃ lūkhassa vā paṇītassa vā bhojanassa yāvadatthaṃ pāripūriṃ, tassa me kāyo lahuko kammañño, handāhaṃ vīriyaṃ ārabhāmi…pe… so vīriyaṃ ārabhati… idaṃ pañcamaṃ ārambhavatthu. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya caranto labhati lūkhassa vā paṇītassa vā bhojanassa yāvadatthaṃ pāripūriṃ. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘ahaṃ kho gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā piṇḍāya caranto alatthaṃ lūkhassa vā paṇītassa vā bhojanassa yāvadatthaṃ pāripūriṃ, tassa me kāyo balavā kammañño, handāhaṃ vīriyaṃ ārabhāmi…pe… so vīriyaṃ ārabhati… idaṃ chaṭṭhaṃ ārambhavatthu . ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhuno uppanno hoti appamattako ābādho. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘uppanno kho me ayaṃ appamattako ābādho, ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ vijjati yaṃ me ābādho pavaḍḍheyya, handāhaṃ vīriyaṃ ārabhāmi…pe… so vīriyaṃ ārabhati… idaṃ sattamaṃ ārambhavatthu. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu gilānā vuṭṭhito hoti aciravuṭṭhito gelaññā. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘ahaṃ kho gilānā vuṭṭhito aciravuṭṭhito gelaññā, ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ vijjati yaṃ me ābādho paccudāvatteyya, handāhaṃ vīriyaṃ ārabhāmi appattassa pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyāyā’’ti! So vīriyaṃ ārabhati appattassa pattiyā anadhigatassa adhigamāya asacchikatassa sacchikiriyāya. Idaṃ aṭṭhamaṃ ārambhavatthu.
335. "Aṭṭha ārambhavatthūni. Furthermore, friends, there is the case where a monk has some work to do. The thought occurs to him: ‘I will have to do this work. But when I am doing this work, it will not be easy to attend to the Buddha’s message. Why don’t I make an effort beforehand for the attaining of the as-yet-unattained, the reaching of the as-yet-unreached, the realization of the as-yet-unrealized?’ So he makes an effort for the attaining of the as-yet-unattained, the reaching of the as-yet-unreached, the realization of the as-yet-unrealized. This is the first ground for the arousal of energy.

“Again, friends, there is the case where a monk has done some work. The thought occurs to him: ‘I have done some work. While I was doing work, I couldn’t attend to the Buddha’s message. Why don’t I make an effort for the attaining of the as-yet-unattained, the reaching of the as-yet-unreached, the realization of the as-yet-unrealized?’ So he makes an effort... This is the second ground for the arousal of energy.

“Again, friends, there is the case where a monk has to go on a journey. The thought occurs to him: ‘I will have to go on this journey. But when I am going on the journey, it will not be easy to attend to the Buddha’s message. Why don’t I make an effort beforehand for the attaining of the as-yet-unattained, the reaching of the as-yet-unreached, the realization of the as-yet-unrealized?’ So he makes an effort. This is the third ground for the arousal of energy.

“Again, friends, there is the case where a monk has gone on a journey. The thought occurs to him: ‘I have gone on a journey. While I was going on the journey, I couldn’t attend to the Buddha’s message. Why don’t I make an effort for the attaining of the as-yet-unattained, the reaching of the as-yet-unreached, the realization of the as-yet-unrealized?’ So he makes an effort… This is the fourth ground for the arousal of energy.

“Again, friends, there is the case where a monk, having gone for alms in a village or town, does not get as much coarse or refined food as he would like for his fill. The thought occurs to him: ‘I, having gone for alms in a village or town, have not gotten as much coarse or refined food as I would like for my fill. This body of mine is light & suitable for work. Why don’t I make an effort for the attaining of the as-yet-unattained, the reaching of the as-yet-unreached, the realization of the as-yet-unrealized?’ So he makes an effort… This is the fifth ground for the arousal of energy.

“Again, friends, there is the case where a monk, having gone for alms in a village or town, gets as much coarse or refined food as he would like for his fill. The thought occurs to him: ‘I, having gone for alms in a village or town, have gotten as much coarse or refined food as I would like for my fill. This body of mine is strong & suitable for work. Why don’t I make an effort for the attaining of the as-yet-unattained, the reaching of the as-yet-unreached, the realization of the as-yet-unrealized?’ So he makes an effort… This is the sixth ground for the arousal of energy.

“Again, friends, there is the case where a monk comes down with a slight illness. The thought occurs to him: ‘I have come down with a slight illness. Now, there’s the possibility that it could get worse. Why don’t I make an effort beforehand for the attaining of the as-yet-unattained, the reaching of the as-yet-unreached, the realization of the as-yet-unrealized?’ So he makes an effort… This is the seventh ground for the arousal of energy.

“Again, friends, there is the case where a monk has recovered from his illness, not long after his recovery. The thought occurs to him: ‘I have recovered from my illness. It’s not long after my recovery. Now, there’s the possibility that the illness could come back. Why don’t I make an effort beforehand for the attaining of the as-yet-unattained, the reaching of the as-yet-unreached, the realization of the as-yet-unrealized?’ So he makes an effort for the attaining of the as-yet-unattained, the reaching of the as-yet-unreached, the realization of the as-yet-unrealized. This is the eighth ground for the arousal of energy.
335.Ārambhavatthūnīti vīriyakāraṇāni. Tesampi imināva nayena attho veditabbo.
The causes of exertion, namely, the ārambhavatthūnī.

In the same way, the meaning should be understood for them as well.
336
336. ‘‘Aṭṭha dānavatthūni. Āsajja dānaṃ deti, bhayā dānaṃ deti, ‘adāsi me’ti dānaṃ deti, ‘dassati me’ti dānaṃ deti, ‘sāhu dāna’nti dānaṃ deti, ‘ahaṃ pacāmi, ime na pacanti, nārahāmi pacanto apacantānaṃ dānaṃ na dātu’nti dānaṃ deti, ‘idaṃ me dānaṃ dadato kalyāṇo kittisaddo abbhuggacchatī’ti dānaṃ deti. Cittālaṅkāra-cittaparikkhāratthaṃ dānaṃ deti.
336. The eight bases for giving (dānavatthūni):

One gives a gift (dānaṃ deti) on impulse (āsajja); one gives a gift out of fear (bhayā); one gives a gift thinking, "He/she gave to me (adāsi me);" one gives a gift thinking, "He/she will give to me (dassati me);" one gives a gift thinking, "It is good to give (sāhu dānaṃ);" one gives a gift thinking, "I am cooking (ahaṃ pacāmi), these are not cooking (ime na pacanti), it is not right for me, while cooking, not to give to those who are not cooking (nārahaṃ pacanto apacantānaṃ dānaṃ na dātuṃ);" one gives a gift thinking, "When this gift has been given (idaṃ me dānaṃ dadato), my admirable reputation (kalyāṇo kittisaddo) will spread about (abbhuggacchati)."

One gives a gift as an ornament for the mind (cittālaṅkāra), as a support for the mind (citta-parikkhāratthaṃ).
336.Dānavatthūnīti dānakāraṇāni. Āsajja dānaṃ detīti patvā dānaṃ deti. Āgataṃ disvāva muhuttaṃyeva nisīdāpetvā sakkāraṃ katvā dānaṃ deti, dassāmi dassāmīti na kilameti. Iti ettha āsādanaṃ dānakāraṇaṃ nāma hoti. Bhayā dānaṃ detītiādīsupi bhayādīni dānakāraṇānīti veditabbāni. Tattha bhayaṃ nāma ayaṃ adāyako akārakoti garahābhayaṃ vā apāyabhayaṃ vā. Adāsi meti mayhaṃ pubbe esa idaṃ nāma adāsīti deti. Dassati meti anāgate idaṃ nāma dassatīti deti. Sāhu dānanti dānaṃ nāma sādhu sundaraṃ, buddhādīhi paṇḍitehi pasatthanti deti. Cittālaṅkāracittaparikkhāratthaṃ dānaṃ detīti samathavipassanācittassa alaṅkāratthañceva parivāratthañca deti. Dānañhi cittaṃ mudukaṃ karoti. Yena laddhaṃ hoti, sopi laddhaṃ meti muducitto hoti, yena dinnaṃ, sopi dinnaṃ mayāti muducitto hoti, iti ubhinnampi cittaṃ mudukaṃ karoti, teneva ‘‘adantadamanaṃ dāna’’nti vuccati. Yathāha – ‘‘Adantadamanaṃ dānaṃ, adānaṃ dantadūsakaṃ; Dānena piyavācāya, unnamanti namanti cā’’ti. Imesu pana aṭṭhasu dānesu cittālaṅkāradānameva uttamaṃ.
336. "Dānavatthūnī" means the causes of generosity (dānakāraṇāni).

"Āsajja dānaṃ detī" means giving generosity, having attained [something].

Having seen someone arrive, having seated them for just a moment, having shown respect, one gives generosity (dānaṃ deti), without tiring of saying "I will give, I will give."

Thus, here, āsādanaṃ is said to be a cause of generosity (dānakāraṇaṃ nāma hoti).

"Bhayā dānaṃ detī," and so forth, it should be understood that fear and so on are causes of generosity (dānakāraṇāni).

There, bhayaṃ, namely, this giver is a non-giver, a non-doer; or fear of blame (garahābhayaṃ vā), or fear of the lower realms (apāyabhayaṃ vā).

"Adāsi me" means, "This one gave this to me before," thus he gives.

"Dassati me" means, "In the future, this one will give to me," thus he gives.

"Sāhu dāna" means generosity (dānaṃ) is good (sādhu), beautiful (sundaraṃ), praised by the Buddhas and wise people, thus he gives.

"Cittālaṅkāracittaparikkhāratthaṃ dānaṃ detī" means he gives for the sake of adorning and purifying the mind (samathavipassanācittassa alaṅkāratthañceva parivāratthañca deti).

Indeed, generosity (dānañhi) makes the mind soft (cittaṃ mudukaṃ karoti).

By whom it has been received (yena laddhaṃ hoti), he also has a softened mind (mudu-citto hoti) thinking, "I have received it" (laddhaṃ me iti); by whom it has been given (yena dinnaṃ), he also has a softened mind thinking, "It was given by me" (dinnaṃ mayāti); thus, it makes the mind soft for both (iti ubhinnampi cittaṃ mudukaṃ karoti), therefore it is said, "Generosity tames the untamed" (‘‘adantadamanaṃ dāna’’nti vuccati).

As it is said:

“Generosity tames the untamed, non-generosity corrupts the tamed;

Through generosity and kind speech, they rise up and bow down.”

Among these eight kinds of generosity (aṭṭhasu dānesu), generosity which adorns the mind (cittālaṅkāradānameva) is the best (uttamaṃ).
337
337. ‘‘Aṭṭha dānūpapattiyo. Idhāvuso, ekacco dānaṃ deti samaṇassa vā brāhmaṇassa vā annaṃ pānaṃ vatthaṃ yānaṃ mālāgandhavilepanaṃ seyyāvasathapadīpeyyaṃ. So yaṃ deti taṃ paccāsīsati paccāsiṃsati (sī. syā. kaṃ. pī.). So passati khattiyamahāsālaṃ vā brāhmaṇamahāsālaṃ vā gahapatimahāsālaṃ vā pañcahi kāmaguṇehi samappitaṃ samaṅgībhūtaṃ paricārayamānaṃ. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā khattiyamahāsālānaṃ vā brāhmaṇamahāsālānaṃ vā gahapatimahāsālānaṃ vā sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya’nti! So taṃ cittaṃ dahati, taṃ cittaṃ adhiṭṭhāti, taṃ cittaṃ bhāveti, tassa taṃ cittaṃ hīne vimuttaṃ uttari abhāvitaṃ tatrūpapattiyā saṃvattati . Tañca kho sīlavato vadāmi no dussīlassa. Ijjhatāvuso, sīlavato cetopaṇidhi visuddhattā. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, idhekacco dānaṃ deti samaṇassa vā brāhmaṇassa vā annaṃ pānaṃ…pe… seyyāvasathapadīpeyyaṃ. So yaṃ deti taṃ paccāsīsati. Tassa sutaṃ hoti – ‘cātumahārājikā cātummahārājikā (sī. syā. pī.) devā dīghāyukā vaṇṇavanto sukhabahulā’’ti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā cātumahārājikānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya’’nti! So taṃ cittaṃ dahati, taṃ cittaṃ adhiṭṭhāti, taṃ cittaṃ bhāveti, tassa taṃ cittaṃ hīne vimuttaṃ uttari abhāvitaṃ tatrūpapattiyā saṃvattati. Tañca kho sīlavato vadāmi no dussīlassa. Ijjhatāvuso, sīlavato cetopaṇidhi visuddhattā. cātummahārājikā (sī. syā. pī.) ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, idhekacco dānaṃ deti samaṇassa vā brāhmaṇassa vā annaṃ pānaṃ…pe… seyyāvasathapadīpeyyaṃ. So yaṃ deti taṃ paccāsīsati. Tassa sutaṃ hoti – ‘tāvatiṃsā devā…pe… yāmā devā…pe… tusitā devā …pe… nimmānaratī devā…pe… paranimmitavasavattī devā dīghāyukā vaṇṇavanto sukhabahulā’ti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā paranimmitavasavattīnaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya’’nti! So taṃ cittaṃ dahati, taṃ cittaṃ adhiṭṭhāti, taṃ cittaṃ bhāveti, tassa taṃ cittaṃ hīne vimuttaṃ uttari abhāvitaṃ tatrūpapattiyā saṃvattati. Tañca kho sīlavato vadāmi no dussīlassa. Ijjhatāvuso, sīlavato cetopaṇidhi visuddhattā. ‘‘Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, idhekacco dānaṃ deti samaṇassa vā brāhmaṇassa vā annaṃ pānaṃ vatthaṃ yānaṃ mālāgandhavilepanaṃ seyyāvasathapadīpeyyaṃ. So yaṃ deti taṃ paccāsīsati. Tassa sutaṃ hoti – ‘brahmakāyikā devā dīghāyukā vaṇṇavanto sukhabahulā’ti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – ‘aho vatāhaṃ kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā brahmakāyikānaṃ devānaṃ sahabyataṃ upapajjeyya’nti! So taṃ cittaṃ dahati, taṃ cittaṃ adhiṭṭhāti, taṃ cittaṃ bhāveti, tassa taṃ cittaṃ hīne vimuttaṃ uttari abhāvitaṃ tatrūpapattiyā saṃvattati. Tañca kho sīlavato vadāmi no dussīlassa; vītarāgassa no sarāgassa. Ijjhatāvuso, sīlavato cetopaṇidhi vītarāgattā. ‘‘Aṭṭha parisā – khattiyaparisā, brāhmaṇaparisā, gahapatiparisā, samaṇaparisā, cātumahārājikaparisā, tāvatiṃsaparisā, māraparisā, brahmaparisā . parisā – ‘‘Aṭṭha lokadhammā – lābho ca, alābho ca, yaso ca, ayaso ca, nindā ca, pasaṃsā ca, sukhañca, dukkhañca. lokadhammā –
337.
’’Eight grounds for giving ( dānūpapattiyo ).
Here, friends, a certain individual gives a gift to a samaṇa or a brāhmaṇa—food, drink, cloth, vehicles, garlands, scents, ointments, bedding, lodging, and lighting.
Whatever he gives, he expects something in return.
He sees a prosperous noble warrior, a prosperous brāhman, or a prosperous householder going about endowed & provided with the five strings of sensuality.
It occurs to him: ‘Oh, with the breakup of the body, after death, may I be reborn in companionship with the prosperous noble warriors, the prosperous brāhmans, or the prosperous householders!’ He fixes on that thought, determines on that thought, develops that thought. His mind—unreleased from what is inferior, uncultivated in what is higher— leads to his rebirth there.
But I say this of a virtuous person, not of an unvirtuous one.
A virtuous person’s act of directing awareness succeeds through purity.

’’And further, friends, a certain person gives a gift to a samaṇa or a brāhmaṇa—food, drink…and lighting.
Whatever he gives, he expects something in return.
He has heard: ‘The Cātumahārājika gods are long-lived, beautiful, and abound in happiness.’
It occurs to him: ‘Oh, with the breakup of the body, after death, may I be reborn in companionship with the Cātumahārājika gods!’ He fixes on that thought, determines on that thought, develops that thought. His mind—unreleased from what is inferior, uncultivated in what is higher— leads to his rebirth there.
But I say this of a virtuous person, not of an unvirtuous one.
A virtuous person’s act of directing awareness succeeds through purity.

’’And further, friends, a certain person gives a gift to a samaṇa or a brāhmaṇa—food, drink…and lighting.
Whatever he gives, he expects something in return.
He has heard: ‘The Tāvatiṃsa gods… the Yāma gods… the Tusita gods… the Nimmānarati gods… the Paranimmitavasavattī gods are long-lived, beautiful, and abound in happiness.’
It occurs to him: ‘Oh, with the breakup of the body, after death, may I be reborn in companionship with the Paranimmitavasavattī gods!’ He fixes on that thought, determines on that thought, develops that thought. His mind—unreleased from what is inferior, uncultivated in what is higher— leads to his rebirth there.
But I say this of a virtuous person, not of an unvirtuous one.
A virtuous person’s act of directing awareness succeeds through purity.

’’And further, friends, a certain person gives a gift to a samaṇa or a brāhmaṇa—food, drink, cloth, vehicles, garlands, scents, ointments, bedding, lodging, and lighting.
Whatever he gives, he expects something in return.
He has heard: ‘The Brahmakāyika gods are long-lived, beautiful, and abound in happiness.’
It occurs to him: ‘Oh, may I, on the dissolution of the body, after death, be reborn in companionship with the Brahmakāyika devas!’ He directs his mind towards this, he establishes his mind towards this, he develops his mind towards this. But that mind of his is inferior and not freed, undeveloped for rebirth there.

But I say this of a virtuous person (śīlavat), not of an unvirtuous one (duḥśīla); of one devoid of passion (vītarāga), not of one with passion (sarāga).

A virtuous person’s (śīlavat) act of directing awareness succeeds through absence of passion (vītarāgattā).

The eight assemblies (aṭṭha parisā) are the assembly of Kṣatriyas (khattiyaparisā), the assembly of Brāhmaṇas (brāhmaṇaparisā), the assembly of householders (gahapatiparisā), the assembly of ascetics (samaṇaparisā), the assembly of the Cāturmahārājikas (cātumahārājikaparisā), the assembly of the Thirty-Three Gods (tāvatiṃsaparisā), the assembly of Māras (māraparisā), the assembly of Brahmās (brahmaparisā).

The eight worldly conditions (aṭṭha lokadhammā) are: gain (lābha), and loss (alābha), fame (yaśa), and infamy (ayaśa), blame (nindā), and praise (pasaṃsā), pleasure (sukha), and pain (duḥkha).
337.Dānūpapattiyoti dānapaccayā upapattiyo. Dahatīti ṭhapeti. Adhiṭṭhātīti tasseva vevacanaṃ. Bhāvetīti vaḍḍheti. Hīne vimuttanti hīnesu pañcakāmaguṇesu vimuttaṃ. Uttari abhāvitanti tato uttari maggaphalatthāya abhāvitaṃ. Tatrūpapattiyā saṃvattatīti yaṃ patthetvā kusalaṃ kataṃ, tattha tattha nibbattanatthāya saṃvattati. Vītarāgassāti maggena vā samucchinnarāgassa samāpattiyā vā vikkhambhitarāgassa. Dānamatteneva hi brahmaloke nibbattituṃ na sakkā. Dānaṃ pana samādhivipassanācittassa alaṅkāro parivāro hoti. Tato dānena muducitto brahmavihāre bhāvetvā brahmaloke nibbattati. Tena vuttaṃ ‘‘vītarāgassa no sarāgassā’’ti. Vītarāgassā Khattiyānaṃ parisā khattiyaparisā, samūhoti attho. Esa nayo sabbattha. khattiyaparisā, Lokassa dhammā lokadhammā. Etehi mutto nāma natthi, buddhānampi hontiyeva. Vuttampi cetaṃ – ‘‘aṭṭhime, bhikkhave, lokadhammā lokaṃ anuparivattanti, loko ca aṭṭha lokadhamme anuparivattatī’’ti (a. ni. 8.5). Lābho alābhoti lābhe āgate alābho āgato evāti veditabbo. Yasādīsupi eseva nayo. lokadhammā. Lābho alābho Yasā
337. Dānūpapattiyo: rebirths due to giving are dānapaccayā upapattiyo.
Dahatī: he places.
Adhiṭṭhātī: that is its synonym.
Bhāvetī: he increases.
Hīne vimuttaṃ: liberated from inferior, the five sensory pleasures are vimuttaṃ.
Uttari abhāvitaṃ: further uncultivated, is uncultivated for the sake of the path and its fruit.
Tatrūpapattiyā saṃvattatī: it leads to rebirth there, means that which having desired, good was done, it leads to the sake of arising in that very place.

Vītarāgassā: by the path, or by the eradicated passion, or by the suppressed passion through attainment (samāpatti).
Merely by giving, it is not possible to be reborn in the Brahmā realm.
Giving, however, is an adornment, an entourage of the mind of concentration and insight.
Therefore, with a mind softened by giving, having cultivated the Brahmā abodes, one is reborn in the Brahmā realm.
Therefore, it was said, "of one without passion, not of one with passion."

Vītarāgassā:
A gathering of kṣatriyas is a kṣatriyaparisā, meaning a group.
This method applies everywhere.

Kṣatriyaparisā,
The dharmas of the world are lokadhammā.
There is no one freed from these; even the Buddhas have them.
It was also said: “These eight worldly conditions, monks, keep the world turning around, and the world turns around these eight worldly conditions” (A.
Ni.
8.5).
Lābho alābho: having gained, loss has come, thus it should be understood.
This same method applies to fame, etc.

Lokadhammā.
Lābho alābho yasā

338. ‘‘Aṭṭha abhibhāyatanāni. Ajjhattaṃ rūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati parittāni suvaṇṇadubbaṇṇāni, ‘tāni abhibhuyya jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti. Idaṃ paṭhamaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ. ‘‘Ajjhattaṃ rūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati appamāṇāni suvaṇṇadubbaṇṇāni, ‘tāni abhibhuyya jānāmi passāmī’ti – evaṃsaññī hoti. Idaṃ dutiyaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ. ‘‘Ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati parittāni suvaṇṇadubbaṇṇāni, ‘tāni abhibhuyya jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti. Idaṃ tatiyaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ. ‘‘Ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati appamāṇāni suvaṇṇadubbaṇṇāni, ‘tāni abhibhuyya jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti. Idaṃ catutthaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ. ‘‘Ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati nīlāni nīlavaṇṇāni nīlanidassanāni nīlanibhāsāni. Seyyathāpi nāma umāpupphaṃ nīlaṃ nīlavaṇṇaṃ nīlanidassanaṃ nīlanibhāsaṃ, seyyathā vā pana taṃ vatthaṃ bārāṇaseyyakaṃ ubhatobhāgavimaṭṭhaṃ nīlaṃ nīlavaṇṇaṃ nīlanidassanaṃ nīlanibhāsaṃ. Evameva evamevaṃ (ka.) ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati nīlāni nīlavaṇṇāni nīlanidassanāni nīlanibhāsāni, ‘tāni abhibhuyya jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti. Idaṃ pañcamaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ. evamevaṃ (ka.) ‘‘Ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati pītāni pītavaṇṇāni pītanidassanāni pītanibhāsāni. Seyyathāpi nāma kaṇikārapupphaṃ kaṇṇikārapupphaṃ (syā. kaṃ.) pītaṃ pītavaṇṇaṃ pītanidassanaṃ pītanibhāsaṃ, seyyathā vā pana taṃ vatthaṃ bārāṇaseyyakaṃ ubhatobhāgavimaṭṭhaṃ pītaṃ pītavaṇṇaṃ pītanidassanaṃ pītanibhāsaṃ. Evameva ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati pītāni pītavaṇṇāni pītanidassanāni pītanibhāsāni, ‘tāni abhibhuyya jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti. Idaṃ chaṭṭhaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ. kaṇṇikārapupphaṃ (syā. kaṃ.) ‘‘Ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati lohitakāni lohitakavaṇṇāni lohitakanidassanāni lohitakanibhāsāni. Seyyathāpi nāma bandhujīvakapupphaṃ lohitakaṃ lohitakavaṇṇaṃ lohitakanidassanaṃ lohitakanibhāsaṃ, seyyathā vā pana taṃ vatthaṃ bārāṇaseyyakaṃ ubhatobhāgavimaṭṭhaṃ lohitakaṃ lohitakavaṇṇaṃ lohitakanidassanaṃ lohitakanibhāsaṃ. Evameva ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati lohitakāni lohitakavaṇṇāni lohitakanidassanāni lohitakanibhāsāni, ‘tāni abhibhuyya jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti. Idaṃ sattamaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ. ‘‘Ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati odātāni odātavaṇṇāni odātanidassanāni odātanibhāsāni. Seyyathāpi nāma osadhitārakā odātā odātavaṇṇā odātanidassanā odātanibhāsā, seyyathā vā pana taṃ vatthaṃ bārāṇaseyyakaṃ ubhatobhāgavimaṭṭhaṃ odātaṃ odātavaṇṇaṃ odātanidassanaṃ odātanibhāsaṃ. Evameva ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko bahiddhā rūpāni passati odātāni odātavaṇṇāni odātanidassanāni odātanibhāsāni , ‘tāni abhibhuyya jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti. Idaṃ aṭṭhamaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ.
338. Eight dimensions of mastery (aṭṭha abhibhāyatanāni): One percipient of form internally (ajjhattaṃ rūpasaññī eko) sees forms externally as limited (parittāni), beautiful & ugly (suvaṇṇadubbaṇṇāni). Mastering them (tāni abhibhuyya), he is percipient of ‘I know; I see’ (jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti). This is the first dimension of mastery (idaṃ paṭhamaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ).

One percipient of form internally (ajjhattaṃ rūpasaññī eko) sees forms externally as immeasurable (appamāṇāni), beautiful & ugly (suvaṇṇadubbaṇṇāni). Mastering them (tāni abhibhuyya), he is percipient of ‘I know; I see’ (jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti). This is the second dimension of mastery (idaṃ dutiyaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ).

One percipient of the formless internally (ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko) sees forms externally as limited (parittāni), beautiful & ugly (suvaṇṇadubbaṇṇāni). Mastering them (tāni abhibhuyya), he is percipient of ‘I know; I see’ (jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti). This is the third dimension of mastery (idaṃ tatiyaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ).

One percipient of the formless internally (ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko) sees forms externally as immeasurable (appamāṇāni), beautiful & ugly (suvaṇṇadubbaṇṇāni). Mastering them (tāni abhibhuyya), he is percipient of ‘I know; I see’ (jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti). This is the fourth dimension of mastery (idaṃ catutthaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ).

One percipient of the formless internally (ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko) sees forms externally as blue (nīlāni), blue in their color (nīlavaṇṇāni), blue in their features (nīlanidassanāni), blue in their glow (nīlanibhāsāni). Just as a flax-flower (umāpupphaṃ) is blue (nīlaṃ), blue in its color (nīlavaṇṇaṃ), blue in its features (nīlanidassanaṃ), blue in its glow (nīlanibhāsaṃ), or just as Vārāṇasī muslin (vatthaṃ bārāṇaseyyakaṃ) smooth on both sides (ubhatobhāgavimaṭṭhaṃ) is blue (nīlaṃ), blue in its color (nīlavaṇṇaṃ), blue in its features (nīlanidassanaṃ), blue in its glow (nīlanibhāsaṃ). In the same way (evameva) one percipient of the formless internally (ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko) sees forms externally as blue (nīlāni), blue in their color (nīlavaṇṇāni), blue in their features (nīlanidassanāni), blue in their glow (nīlanibhāsāni). Mastering them (tāni abhibhuyya), he is percipient of ‘I know; I see’ (jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti). This is the fifth dimension of mastery (idaṃ pañcamaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ).

One percipient of the formless internally (ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko) sees forms externally as yellow (pītāni), yellow in their color (pītavaṇṇāni), yellow in their features (pītanidassanāni), yellow in their glow (pītanibhāsāni). Just as a kaṇikāra-flower (kaṇikārapupphaṃ) is yellow (pītaṃ), yellow in its color (pītavaṇṇaṃ), yellow in its features (pītanidassanaṃ), yellow in its glow (pītanibhāsaṃ), or just as Vārāṇasī muslin (vatthaṃ bārāṇaseyyakaṃ) smooth on both sides (ubhatobhāgavimaṭṭhaṃ) is yellow (pītaṃ), yellow in its color (pītavaṇṇaṃ), yellow in its features (pītanidassanaṃ), yellow in its glow (pītanibhāsaṃ). In the same way (evameva) one percipient of the formless internally (ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko) sees forms externally as yellow (pītāni), yellow in their color (pītavaṇṇāni), yellow in their features (pītanidassanāni), yellow in their glow (pītanibhāsāni). Mastering them (tāni abhibhuyya), he is percipient of ‘I know; I see’ (jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti). This is the sixth dimension of mastery (idaṃ chaṭṭhaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ).

One percipient of the formless internally (ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko) sees forms externally as red (lohitakāni), red in their color (lohitakavaṇṇāni), red in their features (lohitakanidassanāni), red in their glow (lohitakanibhāsāni). Just as a bandhujīvaka-flower (bandhujīvakapupphaṃ) is red (lohitakaṃ), red in its color (lohitakavaṇṇaṃ), red in its features (lohitakanidassanaṃ), red in its glow (lohitakanibhāsaṃ), or just as Vārāṇasī muslin (vatthaṃ bārāṇaseyyakaṃ) smooth on both sides (ubhatobhāgavimaṭṭhaṃ) is red (lohitakaṃ), red in its color (lohitakavaṇṇaṃ), red in its features (lohitakanidassanaṃ), red in its glow (lohitakanibhāsaṃ). In the same way (evameva) one percipient of the formless internally (ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko) sees forms externally as red (lohitakāni), red in their color (lohitakavaṇṇāni), red in their features (lohitakanidassanāni), red in their glow (lohitakanibhāsāni). Mastering them (tāni abhibhuyya), he is percipient of ‘I know; I see’ (jānāmi passāmī’ti evaṃsaññī hoti). This is the seventh dimension of mastery (idaṃ sattamaṃ abhibhāyatanaṃ).

One percipient of the formless internally (ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī eko) sees forms externally as white (odātāni), white in their color (odātavaṇṇāni), white in their features (odātanidassanāni), white in their glow (odātanibhāsāni). Just as the morning star (osadhitārakā) is white (odātā), white in its color (odātavaṇṇā), white in its features (odātanidassanā), white in its glow (odātanibhāsā), or just as Vārāṇasī muslin (vatthaṃ bārāṇaseyyakaṃ) smooth on both sides (ubhatobhāgavimaṭṭhaṃ) is white (odātaṃ), white in its color (odātavaṇṇaṃ), white in its features (odātanidassanaṃ), white in its glow (odātanibhāsaṃ).
Just so, not perceiving form internally, one sees external forms that are white, white in color, white in appearance, white in radiance, and mastering them, one is aware that one knows and sees them. This is the eighth sphere of mastery (abhibhāyatana).
338. Abhibhāyatanavimokkhakathā heṭṭhā kathitā eva. ‘‘Ime kho, āvuso’’tiādi vuttanayeneva yojetabbaṃ. Iti ekādasannaṃ aṭṭhakānaṃ vasena aṭṭhāsīti pañhe kathento thero sāmaggirasaṃ dassesīti. Aṭṭhakavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā. Navakavaṇṇanā
338. The discourse on the spheres of mastery and deliverance has already been spoken of earlier.

"These, friend," etc., should be connected in the same way as stated before.

Thus, the elder, while speaking of eighty-eight questions in terms of eleven groups of eight, showed the essence of concord.

The explanation of the groups of eight is finished.

Explanation of the groups of nine.

339. ‘‘Aṭṭha vimokkhā. Rūpī rūpāni passati. Ayaṃ paṭhamo vimokkho. ‘‘Ajjhattaṃ arūpasaññī bahiddhā rūpāni passati. Ayaṃ dutiyo vimokkho. ‘‘Subhanteva adhimutto hoti. Ayaṃ tatiyo vimokkho. ‘‘Sabbaso rūpasaññānaṃ samatikkamā paṭighasaññānaṃ atthaṅgamā nānattasaññānaṃ amanasikārā ‘ananto ākāso’ti ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ catuttho vimokkho. ‘‘Sabbaso ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘anantaṃ viññāṇa’nti viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ pañcamo vimokkho. ‘‘Sabbaso viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘natthi kiñcī’ti ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ chaṭṭho vimokkho. ‘‘Sabbaso ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ sattamo vimokkho. ‘‘Sabbaso nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma saññāvedayita nirodhaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ aṭṭhamo vimokkho. ‘‘Ime kho, āvuso, tena bhagavatā jānatā passatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena aṭṭha dhammā sammadakkhātā; tattha sabbeheva saṅgāyitabbaṃ…pe… atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ. Navakaṃ
339. Eight liberations (aṣṭau vimokṣāḥ): Possessed of form, one sees forms. This is the first liberation.

Not percipient of form internally, one sees forms externally. This is the second liberation.

“One is intent only on the beautiful. This is the third liberation.

“Through the complete surmounting of perceptions of material form, the passing away of perceptions of resistance, and non-attention to perceptions of diversity, ‘Space is infinite,’ one enters and dwells in the base of the infinity of space (ākāśānantyāyatana). This is the fourth liberation.

“Having completely surmounted the base of the infinity of space (ākāśānantyāyatana), ‘Consciousness is infinite,’ one enters and dwells in the base of the infinity of consciousness (vijñānānantyāyatana). This is the fifth liberation.

“Having completely surmounted the base of the infinity of consciousness (vijñāṇānantyāyatana), ‘There is nothing,’ one enters and dwells in the base of nothingness (ākiñcaññāyatana). This is the sixth liberation.

“Having completely surmounted the base of nothingness (ākiñcaññāyatana), one enters and dwells in the base of neither perception nor non-perception (nevasaññānāsaññāyatana). This is the seventh liberation.

“With the complete transcending of the dimension of neither perception nor non-perception (nevasaññānāsaññāyatana), one enters and remains in the cessation of perception and feeling (saññāvedayita nirodha). This is the eighth liberation.

“These, friends, are the eight qualities (aṭṭha dhammā) that are rightly expounded by the Blessed One—the one who knows, the one who sees, the Worthy One, rightly self-awakened. Those should all be recited together… for the benefit & happiness of devas & human beings.

The Ninth

340. ‘‘Atthi kho, āvuso, tena bhagavatā jānatā passatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena nava dhammā sammadakkhātā; tattha sabbeheva saṅgāyitabbaṃ…pe… atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ. Katame nava? ‘‘Nava āghātavatthūni. ‘Anatthaṃ me acarī’ti āghātaṃ bandhati; ‘anatthaṃ me caratī’ti āghātaṃ bandhati; ‘anatthaṃ me carissatī’ti āghātaṃ bandhati; ‘piyassa me manāpassa anatthaṃ acarī’ti āghātaṃ bandhati…pe… anatthaṃ caratīti āghātaṃ bandhati…pe… anatthaṃ carissatīti āghātaṃ bandhati; ‘appiyassa me amanāpassa atthaṃ acarī’ti āghātaṃ bandhati…pe… atthaṃ caratīti āghātaṃ bandhati…pe… atthaṃ carissatīti āghātaṃ bandhati. āghātavatthūni. ‘‘Nava āghātapaṭivinayā. ‘Anatthaṃ me acari acarīti (syā. ka.) evaṃ ‘‘carati carissati’’ padesupi, taṃ kutettha labbhā’ti āghātaṃ paṭivineti ; ‘anatthaṃ me carati, taṃ kutettha labbhā’ti āghātaṃ paṭivineti; ‘anatthaṃ me carissati, taṃ kutettha labbhā’ti āghātaṃ paṭivineti; ‘piyassa me manāpassa anatthaṃ acari…pe… anatthaṃ carati…pe… anatthaṃ carissati, taṃ kutettha labbhā’ti āghātaṃ paṭivineti; ‘appiyassa me amanāpassa atthaṃ acari…pe… atthaṃ carati…pe… atthaṃ carissati, taṃ kutettha labbhā’ti āghātaṃ paṭivineti. āghātapaṭivinayā. acarīti (syā. ka.) evaṃ ‘‘carati carissati’’ padesupi
340. “There are, friends, nine dhammas that are rightly expounded by the Blessed One—the one who knows, the one who sees, the Worthy One, rightly self-awakened (sammāsambuddha); those should all be recited together...for the benefit, welfare and happiness of devas and human beings (devamanussānaṃ). What are the nine?

“Nine grounds for resentment (āghātavatthūni). ‘He has done me harm,’ one binds hatred (āghātaṃ bandhati); ‘He is doing me harm,’ one binds hatred; ‘He is going to do me harm,’ one binds hatred; ‘He has done harm to people who are dear & pleasing to me,’ one binds hatred…‘He is doing harm,’ one binds hatred…‘He is going to do harm,’ one binds hatred; ‘He has aided people who are not dear or pleasing to me,’ one binds hatred…‘He is aiding,’ one binds hatred…‘He is going to aid,’ one binds hatred.

Grounds for resentment (āghātavatthūni).

“Nine ways of subduing hatred (āghātapaṭivinayā). ‘He has done me harm, but what should I expect?’ one subdues hatred; ‘He is doing me harm, but what should I expect?’ one subdues hatred; ‘He is going to do me harm, but what should I expect?’ one subdues hatred; ‘He has done harm to people who are dear & pleasing to me…He is doing harm…He is going to do harm, but what should I expect?’ one subdues hatred; ‘He has aided people who are not dear or pleasing to me…He is aiding…He is going to aid, but what should I expect?’ one subdues hatred.

Ways of subduing hatred (āghātapaṭivinayā).
340. Iti aṭṭhakavasena sāmaggirasaṃ dassetvā idāni navakavasena dassetuṃ puna desanaṃ ārabhi. Tattha āghātavatthūnīti āghātakāraṇāni. Āghātaṃ bandhatīti kopaṃ bandhati karoti uppādeti. Taṃ kutettha labbhāti taṃ anatthacaraṇaṃ mā ahosīti etasmiṃ puggale kuto labbhā, kena kāraṇena sakkā laddhuṃ ? Paro nāma parassa attano cittaruciyā anatthaṃ karotīti evaṃ cintetvā āghātaṃ paṭivinodeti. Atha vā sacāhaṃ paṭikopaṃ kareyyaṃ, taṃ kopakaraṇaṃ ettha puggale kuto labbhā, kena kāraṇena laddhabbanti attho. Kuto lābhātipi pāṭho, sacāhaṃ ettha kopaṃ kareyyaṃ, tasmiṃ me kopakaraṇe kuto lābhā, lābhā nāma ke siyunti attho. Imasmiñca atthe tanti nipātamattameva hoti. Taṃ kutettha labbhā ta

Thus, having shown the entirety with the eightfold division, now, to show [it] with the ninefold division, again he began the teaching.
There, āghātavatthūnī means causes of striking.
āghātaṃ bandhatī means he binds anger, he does, he produces.
taṃ kutettha labbhā means may that non-benefit conduct not be; where is it obtained in this person, by what means is it possible to obtain?
1046"/>? "Another, named, by the mind-desire of another's self, does non-benefit" — having thought thus, he wards off striking.
Or, if I should do counter-anger, where is that anger-instrument obtained in this person, by what means is it obtained?, is the meaning.
"Where is gain?" is also a reading; if I should do anger here, where is gain for me in that anger-instrument, what are gains called?, is the meaning.
And in this meaning, ta is merely an indeclinable.
taṃ kutettha labbhā

341. ‘‘Nava sattāvāsā. Santāvuso, sattā nānattakāyā nānattasaññino, seyyathāpi manussā ekacce ca devā ekacce ca vinipātikā. Ayaṃ paṭhamo sattāvāso. ‘‘Santāvuso, sattā nānattakāyā ekattasaññino, seyyathāpi devā brahmakāyikā paṭhamābhinibbattā. Ayaṃ dutiyo sattāvāso. ‘‘Santāvuso, sattā ekattakāyā nānattasaññino, seyyathāpi devā ābhassarā. Ayaṃ tatiyo sattāvāso. ‘‘Santāvuso , sattā ekattakāyā ekattasaññino, seyyathāpi devā subhakiṇhā. Ayaṃ catuttho sattāvāso. ‘‘Santāvuso, sattā asaññino appaṭisaṃvedino, seyyathāpi devā asaññasattā asaññisattā (syā. kaṃ.). Ayaṃ pañcamo sattāvāso. asaññisattā (syā. kaṃ.) ‘‘Santāvuso, sattā sabbaso rūpasaññānaṃ samatikkamā paṭighasaññānaṃ atthaṅgamā nānattasaññānaṃ amanasikārā ‘ananto ākāso’ti ākāsānañcāyatanūpagā. Ayaṃ chaṭṭho sattāvāso. ‘‘Santāvuso, sattā sabbaso ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘anantaṃ viññāṇa’nti viññāṇañcāyatanūpagā. Ayaṃ sattamo sattāvāso. ‘‘Santāvuso, sattā sabbaso viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘natthi kiñcī’ti ākiñcāññāyatanūpagā. Ayaṃ aṭṭhamo sattāvāso. ‘‘Santāvuso , sattā sabbaso ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma samatikkamma santametaṃ paṇītametanti (syā. kaṃ.) nevasaññānāsaññāyatanūpagā. Ayaṃ navamo sattāvāso. samatikkamma santametaṃ paṇītametanti (syā. kaṃ.)
"Friends, there are nine abodes of beings."

"Friends, there are beings with diversity of body and diversity of perception, such as human beings, some gods, and some beings in the lower realms. This is the first abode for beings."

"Friends, there are beings with diversity of body and unity of perception, such as the gods of Brahmā’s retinue generated by the first. This is the second abode for beings."

"Friends, there are beings with unity of body and diversity of perception, such as the Radiant Gods. This is the third abode for beings."

"Friends, there are beings with unity of body and unity of perception, such as the Beautifully Lustrous Gods. This is the fourth abode for beings."

"Friends, there are beings without perception, experiencing no feeling, namely, the Gods who are Non-percipient Creatures. This is the fifth abode for beings."

"Friends, there are beings who, by completely surmounting all perceptions of form, with the disappearance of perceptions of resistance, and by not attending to perceptions of diversity, [thinking,] ‘Space is endless,’ reach the base of the infinity of space. This is the sixth abode for beings."

"Friends, there are beings who, by completely surmounting the base of the infinity of space, [thinking,] ‘Consciousness is endless,’ reach the base of the infinity of consciousness. This is the seventh abode for beings."

"Friends, there are beings who, by completely surmounting the base of the infinity of consciousness, [thinking,] ‘There is nothing,’ reach the base of nothingness. This is the eighth abode for beings."

"Friends, there are beings who, by completely surmounting the base of nothingness, reach the base of neither perception nor non-perception. This is the ninth abode for beings."
341.Sattāvāsāti sattānaṃ āvāsā, vasanaṭṭhānānīti attho. Tattha suddhāvāsāpi sattāvāsova, asabbakālikattā pana na gahitā. Suddhāvāsā hi buddhānaṃ khandhāvārasadisā. Asaṅkhyeyyakappe buddhesu anibbattantesu taṃ ṭhānaṃ suññaṃ hotīti asabbakālikattā na gahitā. Sesamettha yaṃ vattabbaṃ, taṃ heṭṭhā vuttameva.
" Sattāvāsā means the abodes ( āvāsā) of beings (sattānaṃ), that is, places of dwelling ( vasanaṭṭhānāni).

Here, the Pure Abodes (suddhāvāsā) are also abodes of beings (sattāvāsova), but they are not included because they are not always [inhabited].

The Pure Abodes are similar to the camps of Buddhas.

For countless aeons ( asaṅkhyeyyakappe) when Buddhas have not arisen ( anibbattantesu), that place is empty (suññaṃ hoti); therefore, they are not included because they are not always [inhabited].

Whatever else needs to be said about this has already been said below."
